Bible Quiz Questions – Old Testament
Collection index and Volume 1: Torah
929 chapters · 7,432 questions across eight linked volumes


Each chapter has eight questions: three Easy, three Medium and two Hard. Question order and multiple-choice answer positions are mixed. Hard questions use closer recall, comparison of passages or careful interpretation.
Easy = green · Medium = yellow · Hard = red


Quotation questions marked WEB use the World English Bible. Other questions preserve the study material’s wording or paraphrase a passage. Accept a clearly equivalent answer when exact wording is not being tested. WEB uses Yahweh for God’s name where the existing material often uses LORD.
Read each passage in its literary and historical setting before applying it today or connecting it to Christ. A character’s statement is not automatically the narrator’s conclusion. Theological questions draw on Scripture and the two course summaries below; disputed interpretations are identified in the explanations.


Linked volumes
Volume 1 — Torah: Genesis–Deuteronomy (this document)
Volume 2 — Historical Books I: Joshua–2 Kings
Volume 3 — Historical Books II: 1 Chronicles–Esther
Volume 4 — Wisdom and Psalms: Job–Psalms
Volume 5 — Wisdom and Love Poetry: Proverbs–Song of Solomon
Volume 6 — Major Prophets I: Isaiah–Jeremiah
Volume 7 — Major Prophets II: Lamentations–Daniel
Volume 8 — Minor Prophets: Hosea–Malachi


Study sources
Old testament — course summary
Gospel Worldview — course summary
World English Bible


Volume 1 — Torah
Genesis–Deuteronomy · 187 chapters · 1,496 questions


Genesis
Genesis 1
Q1. True or false: God created both male and female in His image. (1:27) Easy
Answer: True. Men and women carry God’s image equally, and that gives every person dignity and worth.


Q2. Multiple choice: God made light on Day 1 but did not make the sun until Day 4. What can we learn from that? Medium
A) Day 1 was really nighttime   B) The sun is not important   C) God is the source of light, and the sun is just something He made   D) The writer made a mistake
Answer: C. God is the source of light, and the sun is just something He made. The nations around Israel worshiped the sun. Genesis shows it is a created thing God uses, not a god.


Q3. Multiple choice: After God created people, what was the first thing He did for them? (1:28) Medium
A) Blessed them   B) Gave them a list of rules   C) Demanded sacrifices   D) Tested them
Answer: A. Blessed them. The gods of other nations had to be kept happy. Israel’s God starts with blessing.


Q4. Multiple choice: God told people to “rule over” the fish, birds and animals (1:28). What kind of ruling does God mean? Medium
A) Taking care of creation for God, like a steward   B) It only applied to Adam   C) Letting animals rule over people   D) Using creation however we want
Answer: A. Taking care of creation for God, like a steward. We are stewards. Ruling well means keeping and caring for what belongs to God.


Q5. True or false: In the Old Testament course's reading, Genesis 1 primarily teaches who God is and humanity's place in creation rather than offering a modern scientific account. (1:1, 26-31) Hard
Answer: True. This is the course's interpretive approach. Christians differ about creation's timing and mechanism while affirming God as Creator.


Q6. Fill in the blank: “In the beginning God ______ the heavens and the earth.” (1:1) Easy
Answer: created. God is the Creator and everything else is created. That line between Creator and creature runs through the whole Bible.


Q7. Multiple choice: How did God make light? (1:3) Easy
A) He gathered it from the stars   B) He took it from the sun   C) Angels made it for Him   D) He spoke, and it was
Answer: D. He spoke, and it was. God’s word has power. When He speaks, it happens, so His words to us can be trusted.


Q8. Multiple choice: After describing the completed creation, what verdict does Genesis 1 give? (1:31) Hard
A) Creation was evil   B) God regretted creating   C) Everything God had made was very good   D) Only people were good
Answer: C. Everything God had made was very good. The verdict concerns the whole creation, including humanity, and affirms the goodness of God's completed work.


Genesis 2
Q1. Multiple choice: God calls the woman a “helper” (ezer) for the man (2:18). Where else does the Old Testament use that same word? Medium
A) For slaves   B) For farm animals   C) For servants   D) For God helping Israel
Answer: D. For God helping Israel. Ezer is often used of God Himself, so “helper” does not mean lesser. Man and woman were made as partners.


Q2. True or false: Adam and Eve were naked and felt no shame. (2:25) Medium
Answer: True. Before sin there was nothing to hide, from God or from each other.


Q3. Multiple choice: Why does the Old Testament professor use Eden as a picture of restored life with God? (2:8-9, 15-25; Revelation 22:1-5) Hard
A) Eden has no work   B) God provides a good home, meaningful work and unashamed relationship   C) Adam cannot disobey   D) Eden has golden streets
Answer: B. God provides a good home, meaningful work and unashamed relationship. The course draws a theological connection between Eden and restoration. Revelation echoes Eden's life and God's presence; Genesis does not call Eden heaven.


Q4. Fill in the blank: “It is not good for the man to be ______.” (2:18) Easy
Answer: alone. Loneliness is the first thing God calls “not good.” We were made for relationship with God and with each other.


Q5. Multiple choice: God brought the animals to Adam to see what he would name them (2:19). What does this show? Medium
A) God forgot their names   B) Adam was bored   C) The animals named themselves   D) God shares His authority with people and invites them to work with Him
Answer: D. God shares His authority with people and invites them to work with Him. Naming is a sign of authority and relationship. God made people partners in caring for His world.


Q6. True or false: God gave Adam the job of working the garden and taking care of it. (2:15) Easy
Answer: True. Work was part of God’s good design before sin ever entered the world.


Q7. Multiple choice: God rested on the seventh day and made it holy (2:2–3). Why did He rest? Hard
A) He ran out of ideas   B) His work was finished, and He set a pattern of rest for His people   C) He was tired   D) He was waiting for Adam
Answer: B. His work was finished, and He set a pattern of rest for His people. God doesn’t get tired. The Sabbath is a sign of trust: we stop working and remember that God provides.


Q8. Multiple choice: What did God form the man from? (2:7) Easy
A) A tree   B) Stone   C) The dust of the ground   D) Water
Answer: C. The dust of the ground. God formed the man from dust and breathed life into him. Genesis 7:22 also speaks of animals having the breath of life; this verse alone does not make that breath exclusive to humans.


Genesis 3
Q1. Multiple choice: What three things did Eve do with the fruit, in order? (3:6) Medium
A) Heard, ran, hid   B) Asked, waited, ate   C) Saw, took, ate   D) Smelled, touched, threw
Answer: C. Saw, took, ate. Saw, took, ate. The same pattern shows up again with Achan and with David. Sin often starts with what we let ourselves look at.


Q2. Multiple choice: God told the serpent that the woman’s offspring would crush its head (3:15). How have Christians understood this promise? Hard
A) The first promise of a rescuer who would defeat evil, pointing to Jesus   B) A rule for farming   C) A warning about snakes   D) A curse on Eve
Answer: A. The first promise of a rescuer who would defeat evil, pointing to Jesus. In the same moment He judges sin, God promises a way back. The hope of the whole Bible starts here.


Q3. True or false: Genesis 3:6 explicitly says Adam was absent when Eve gave him the fruit. (3:6) Medium
Answer: False. It describes her husband as with her when she gave him the fruit. The verse does not specify every detail of his location throughout the earlier conversation.


Q4. Multiple choice: What was at the root of the first sin? Hard
A) Wanting to be like God and decide good and evil for themselves   B) The serpent forcing them   C) Not knowing the rule   D) Being hungry
Answer: A. Wanting to be like God and decide good and evil for themselves. Sin is choosing self over God: trusting our own judgment instead of the Creator’s.


Q5. True or false: After they ate the fruit, Adam and Eve hid from God. (3:8) Easy
Answer: True. Shame came first. Sin makes us want to hide from God, when He is the one we need most.


Q6. True or false: Even after Adam and Eve sinned, God made clothes for them. (3:21) Easy
Answer: True. God judged their sin but still cared for them. He is both just and merciful.


Q7. Multiple choice: God told Adam not to eat from the tree. What did Eve say God had commanded? (3:3) Medium
A) Only Adam can’t eat it   B) You may eat it on the seventh day   C) Exactly what God said   D) Don’t eat it, and don’t even touch it
Answer: D. Don’t eat it, and don’t even touch it. Eve added “don’t touch it” to God’s words. It matters to know what God actually said.


Q8. Fill in the blank: After Adam and Eve sinned, God called to the man, “Where are ______?” (3:9) Easy
Answer: you. God already knew the answer. He asked to draw Adam back into relationship. God pursues us even after we fail.


Genesis 4
Q1. True or false: God put a mark on Cain to protect him from being killed. (4:15) Medium
Answer: True. God showed mercy even to a murderer.


Q2. Multiple choice: Lamech boasted that he would be avenged “seventy-seven times” (4:24). Jesus told Peter to forgive “seventy-seven times” (Matthew 18:22). What is the connection? Hard
A) Both are about counting   B) Coincidence   C) Lamech was a disciple   D) Jesus reverses the spiral of revenge with forgiveness
Answer: D. Jesus reverses the spiral of revenge with forgiveness. Where sin multiplies revenge, Jesus multiplies forgiveness.


Q3. Multiple choice: God told Cain, “Sin is crouching at your door; it desires to have you, but you must rule over it” (4:7). What does that teach? Medium
A) We are responsible for our choices, and sin can be resisted   B) Only Cain was tempted   C) Sin can’t be resisted   D) Sin is an animal
Answer: A. We are responsible for our choices, and sin can be resisted. God warns us before we fall.


Q4. Multiple choice: Just eight verses after leaving the garden, one brother kills another. What does that show? Hard
A) Sin quickly grows into violence and breaks our relationships with each other   B) Cain was always evil   C) It’s just a story   D) Abel deserved it
Answer: A. Sin quickly grows into violence and breaks our relationships with each other. The fall broke our relationship with God and with one another.


Q5. True or false: God warned Cain before he killed Abel. (4:6–7) Easy
Answer: True. God gave Cain a chance to turn back.


Q6. Multiple choice: God asked Cain, “Where is your brother Abel?” (4:9). Why ask when He already knew? Medium
A) To find Abel   B) He didn’t know   C) To give Cain a chance to confess   D) To trick him
Answer: C. To give Cain a chance to confess. Like “Where are you?” to Adam, God asks to draw people back.


Q7. Multiple choice: What work did Cain and Abel do? (4:2) Easy
A) Both were builders   B) Cain worked the soil and Abel kept flocks   C) Both were hunters   D) Cain kept flocks and Abel farmed
Answer: B. Cain worked the soil and Abel kept flocks. Each brought an offering from his work.


Q8. Fill in the blank: Cain asked God, “Am I my brother’s ______?” (4:9) Easy
Answer: keeper. Yes, we are responsible for how we treat one another.


Genesis 5
Q1. Multiple choice: Enoch breaks the pattern of “and then he died.” What does his story teach? Medium
A) Walking with God is what matters most, and death isn’t the final word   B) Nothing   C) He ran away   D) He was lucky
Answer: A. Walking with God is what matters most, and death isn’t the final word. A life close to God points to hope beyond death.


Q2. Multiple choice: Genesis 5:1 repeats that God created people “in the likeness of God.” Why repeat it after the fall? Hard
A) It was lost   B) Only Adam had it   C) The writer forgot   D) The image of God remains in people even after sin
Answer: D. The image of God remains in people even after sin. Sin damaged us, but it didn’t erase God’s image.


Q3. Multiple choice: The phrase “and then he died” repeats again and again. What is the point? Medium
A) Nothing   B) People were unhealthy   C) The writer was bored   D) Death had entered the world through sin, just as God warned
Answer: D. Death had entered the world through sin, just as God warned. The repeated line shows the cost of the fall.


Q4. Fill in the blank: “Enoch walked faithfully with God; then he was no more, because God ______ him away.” (5:24) Easy
Answer: took. Enoch didn’t die like the others.


Q5. Multiple choice: Lamech named his son Noah, hoping he would bring comfort from the “painful toil” caused by the cursed ground (5:29). What does that show? Hard
A) People longed to be rescued from the curse, and that hope points forward   B) Nothing   C) Lamech liked farming   D) Noah was a farmer
Answer: A. People longed to be rescued from the curse, and that hope points forward. From the start, people waited for God to undo the curse.


Q6. Multiple choice: Who lived the longest in Genesis 5? (5:27) Easy
A) Adam   B) Enoch   C) Methuselah   D) Noah
Answer: C. Methuselah. Methuselah lived 969 years.


Q7. True or false: Adam had a son “in his own likeness, in his own image.” (5:3) Medium
Answer: True. Adam passed on God’s image, and also a fallen nature.


Q8. True or false: Genesis 5 traces the family line from Adam to Noah. Easy
Answer: True. It connects the first people to the flood story.


Genesis 6
Q1. Fill in the blank: “But Noah found ______ in the eyes of the Lord.” (6:8) Easy
Answer: favor (or grace). Noah was saved by God’s grace.


Q2. True or false: Noah did everything just as God commanded him. (6:22) Easy
Answer: True. Faith shows up in obedience.


Q3. Multiple choice: What did God tell Noah to build? (6:14) Easy
A) A temple   B) A tower   C) An ark   D) A wall
Answer: C. An ark. A huge boat to save his family and the animals.


Q4. Multiple choice: What filled the earth before the flood? (6:11) Medium
A) Cities   B) Music   C) Violence   D) Animals
Answer: C. Violence. The violence that started with Cain had spread everywhere.


Q5. Multiple choice: God tells Noah, “I will establish my covenant with you” (6:18), the first time the word covenant appears. What does that teach? Hard
A) Nothing   B) Noah asked for it   C) God was making a deal   D) Even while announcing judgment, God was already planning rescue and relationship
Answer: D. Even while announcing judgment, God was already planning rescue and relationship. God’s judgment and mercy come together.


Q6. Multiple choice: “The Lord regretted that he had made human beings on the earth, and his heart was deeply troubled” (6:6). What does that show about God? Medium
A) He grieves over sin. He is not distant or uncaring   B) He made a mistake   C) He didn’t know what would happen   D) He was angry at animals
Answer: A. He grieves over sin. He is not distant or uncaring. Sin breaks God’s heart.


Q7. Multiple choice: Noah “walked faithfully with God” (6:9). What does his life teach? Hard
A) Noah was perfect   B) Walking is good exercise   C) One person can stay faithful even in a corrupt world   D) Everyone around him was good
Answer: C. One person can stay faithful even in a corrupt world. Noah’s faith stood out because everything around him was dark.


Q8. True or false: The Bible clearly explains who the “sons of God” in 6:2 were. Medium
Answer: False. It’s unclear. What is clear is that God was grieved by human wickedness.


Genesis 7
Q1. Multiple choice: How long did the rain fall? (7:12) Easy
A) 7 days   B) 40 days and 40 nights   C) A year   D) 150 days
Answer: B. 40 days and 40 nights. The water then covered the earth for 150 days.


Q2. Multiple choice: What connection does 1 Peter make between Noah's rescue and baptism? (7:7; 1 Peter 3:20-22) Hard
A) Rescue through water points to salvation through Jesus' resurrection   B) Noah baptizes his sons   C) Both require an ark   D) Washing dirt away saves by itself
Answer: A. Rescue through water points to salvation through Jesus' resurrection. Peter connects baptism with an appeal or pledge toward God through Christ's resurrection. Christians differ on baptism's precise role; Peter rejects mere external washing.


Q3. True or false: Noah did all that the Lord commanded him. (7:5) Easy
Answer: True. Noah’s faith showed in obedience.


Q4. Fill in the blank: “Then the Lord ______ him in.” (7:16) Easy
Answer: shut. God closed the door of the ark.


Q5. Multiple choice: In the course's reading, which later biblical periods resemble the flood's forty days of rain? (7:12; Numbers 14:33-34; Matthew 4:1-2) Hard
A) Israel's forty years and Jesus' forty days of testing   B) Every reign of an Israelite king   C) Every harvest season   D) Every Sabbath
Answer: A. Israel's forty years and Jesus' forty days of testing. These passages associate forty with testing or judgment. This literary pattern does not make every occurrence of forty a coded symbol.


Q6. Multiple choice: God Himself shut the door of the ark (7:16). What does that show? Medium
A) Noah forgot   B) The door was heavy   C) Safety came from God, not from Noah’s effort   D) God was angry with Noah
Answer: C. Safety came from God, not from Noah’s effort. God is the one who keeps His people safe.


Q7. True or false: Noah took seven pairs of every kind of clean animal. (7:2) Medium
Answer: True. Some clean animals would later be used for sacrifice.


Q8. Multiple choice: The deep springs burst and the floodgates of the heavens opened (7:11). Thinking back to Genesis 1, what is happening? Medium
A) A new sea is made   B) Creation is being undone, as the waters God separated come back together   C) A normal storm   D) Nothing
Answer: B. Creation is being undone, as the waters God separated come back together. The flood is like creation in reverse, before God re-creates.


Genesis 8
Q1. Multiple choice: Noah waited for God to say “Come out of the ark” (8:15–16) even after the ground looked dry. What does that teach? Medium
A) He was lazy   B) He was afraid of animals   C) He liked the ark   D) He waited for God’s timing instead of rushing ahead
Answer: D. He waited for God’s timing instead of rushing ahead. Noah moved when God said move.


Q2. True or false: The first thing Noah did after leaving the ark was build an altar. (8:20) Easy
Answer: True. His first act on dry land was worship.


Q3. True or false: God promised that seedtime and harvest, summer and winter would continue as long as the earth endures. (8:22) Medium
Answer: True. The seasons are a sign of God’s faithfulness.


Q4. Multiple choice: God said every inclination of the human heart is evil from childhood (8:21), yet He promised never to destroy the earth this way again. What does that show? Hard
A) God was tired   B) People had become good   C) God gave up on people   D) God’s mercy doesn’t depend on people being good
Answer: D. God’s mercy doesn’t depend on people being good. God chose patience and mercy toward a broken world.


Q5. Multiple choice: What did the dove bring back to the ark? (8:11) Easy
A) An olive leaf   B) A fish   C) Nothing   D) A stick
Answer: A. An olive leaf. It showed the waters were going down.


Q6. Multiple choice: Noah’s first act was worship. What does that show? Medium
A) Nothing   B) He was hungry   C) It was a custom   D) Gratitude: he gave God the credit, the opposite of making a name for himself
Answer: D. Gratitude: he gave God the credit, the opposite of making a name for himself. Worship gives God the credit for rescue.


Q7. Multiple choice: “God remembered Noah” sits right at the center of the flood story. What does that teach? Hard
A) The flood was almost over   B) In the middle of the darkest moment, God hadn’t forgotten His people   C) Noah reminded God   D) God forgets easily
Answer: B. In the middle of the darkest moment, God hadn’t forgotten His people. Hebrew stories often put the main idea in the middle.


Q8. Fill in the blank: “But God ______ Noah.” (8:1) Easy
Answer: remembered. God had not forgotten the people in the ark.


Genesis 9
Q1. Multiple choice: What was the sign of God’s covenant with Noah? (9:13) Easy
A) Circumcision   B) A stone altar   C) The rainbow   D) The Sabbath
Answer: C. The rainbow. Every rainbow is a reminder of God’s promise.


Q2. Multiple choice: Why is taking a human life so serious? (9:6) Medium
A) It was against Noah’s rules   B) People are rare   C) It makes God tired   D) “In the image of God has God made mankind”
Answer: D. “In the image of God has God made mankind”. Every human life carries God’s image and dignity.


Q3. Multiple choice: The Hebrew word for rainbow is the same as a war bow. What does the bow in the sky picture? Medium
A) Bad weather   B) A hunting trip   C) God getting ready to fight   D) God hanging up His weapon as a sign of peace
Answer: D. God hanging up His weapon as a sign of peace. God set aside judgment by flood for good.


Q4. True or false: God required Noah to do something in return for the promise of no more floods. Medium
Answer: False. God made the promise without asking anything in return.


Q5. True or false: God made this covenant with Noah and also with every living creature. (9:10) Easy
Answer: True. God’s care extends to all of creation.


Q6. Multiple choice: God tells Noah, “Be fruitful and increase in number and fill the earth” (9:1). What does that echo? Hard
A) The Tower of Babel   B) Genesis 1, where God blessed the first humans with the same words   C) Nothing   D) The Ten Commandments
Answer: B. Genesis 1, where God blessed the first humans with the same words. The flood is a new beginning for creation.


Q7. Multiple choice: Right after the covenant, Noah gets drunk and Ham dishonors him (9:20–23). What does that show? Hard
A) It’s unimportant   B) The flood didn’t fix the human heart. Sin is still there   C) Noah wasn’t really righteous   D) Wine is evil
Answer: B. The flood didn’t fix the human heart. Sin is still there. Only God can solve the problem of sin, and the rest of the Bible tells how.


Q8. Fill in the blank: God promised that never again would a ______ destroy the earth. (9:11) Easy
Answer: flood. God bound Himself with a promise.


Genesis 10
Q1. Multiple choice: Nimrod’s kingdom began in Babel and included Nineveh (10:10–11). Why is that worth noticing? Medium
A) Nothing   B) They were nice cities   C) Nimrod was a prophet   D) These cities later became powerful enemies of God’s people
Answer: D. These cities later became powerful enemies of God’s people. Human empire-building shows up early in the story.


Q2. Multiple choice: God told Noah’s family to “fill the earth” (9:1). How does chapter 10 relate to that command? Hard
A) It doesn’t   B) They all stayed in one place   C) It shows God’s blessing at work, even through broken people   D) They refused
Answer: C. It shows God’s blessing at work, even through broken people. God’s purposes move forward even after the flood.


Q3. Fill in the blank: Nimrod was “a mighty ______ before the Lord.” (10:9) Easy
Answer: hunter. He also built cities and a kingdom.


Q4. Multiple choice: Chapter 10 says the nations spread out by language, and chapter 11 explains why. What writing technique is this? Hard
A) Two different authors arguing   B) A riddle   C) A mistake   D) The Bible sometimes gives the summary first, then the story behind it
Answer: D. The Bible sometimes gives the summary first, then the story behind it. Reading for how a story is told helps us understand it.


Q5. Multiple choice: Chapter 10 lists the families of whose three sons? (10:1) Easy
A) Adam’s   B) Jacob’s   C) Abraham’s   D) Noah’s: Shem, Ham and Japheth
Answer: D. Noah’s: Shem, Ham and Japheth. The nations spread out from Noah’s sons.


Q6. True or false: According to Genesis, every nation shares common ancestors. Medium
Answer: True. Every person is part of one human family made in God’s image.


Q7. Multiple choice: Why does Genesis include a long list of nations? Medium
A) To show all peoples come from one family, and God cares about all of them   B) To fill space   C) To list Israel’s enemies only   D) It was a census
Answer: A. To show all peoples come from one family, and God cares about all of them. God later promises to bless all these nations through Abraham.


Q8. True or false: Genesis 10 lists the nations that came from Noah’s family. Easy
Answer: True. It’s often called the Table of Nations.


Genesis 11
Q1. Multiple choice: Their tower was supposed to reach heaven, yet “the Lord came down to see” it (11:5). What is the point? Medium
A) Human pride looks tiny next to God   B) The tower was finished   C) God wanted to visit   D) God couldn’t see well
Answer: A. Human pride looks tiny next to God. The “great” tower was so small God had to come down to look at it.


Q2. True or false: God confused their language so they couldn’t understand each other. (11:7) Easy
Answer: True. Then they scattered over the earth.


Q3. Multiple choice: At Pentecost (Acts 2), people from many languages understood the apostles. How does that relate to Babel? Hard
A) It was another tower   B) It reverses Babel: God brings scattered peoples together by His Spirit   C) Everyone spoke Hebrew   D) It doesn’t
Answer: B. It reverses Babel: God brings scattered peoples together by His Spirit. What pride scattered, God gathers.


Q4. True or false: Building with baked bricks was the sin at Babel. Medium
Answer: False. The sin was trying to make a name for themselves apart from God.


Q5. Multiple choice: Chapter 11 ends by saying Sarai couldn’t have children (11:30), right before God calls Abram. Why mention it? Hard
A) It sets up that God’s plan will come by His power, since it’s humanly impossible   B) Sarai was sick   C) It isn’t important   D) To explain Babel
Answer: A. It sets up that God’s plan will come by His power, since it’s humanly impossible. After people tried to build their own way to heaven, God starts His plan with something only He can do.


Q6. Fill in the blank: The people said, “Let us make a ______ for ourselves.” (11:4) Easy
Answer: name. They wanted their own glory.


Q7. Multiple choice: What was the real problem at Babel? Medium
A) Building with bricks   B) Living in cities   C) Relying on themselves and seeking their own glory instead of God’s   D) Speaking one language
Answer: C. Relying on themselves and seeking their own glory instead of God’s. Technology wasn’t the sin. Pride was.


Q8. Multiple choice: What did the people at Babel set out to build? (11:4) Easy
A) An ark   B) A temple   C) A wall   D) A city with a tower reaching to the heavens
Answer: D. A city with a tower reaching to the heavens. They wanted to reach heaven on their own terms.


Genesis 12
Q1. Multiple choice: At Babel, people wanted to “make a name for ourselves.” Here God tells Abram, “I will make your name great” (12:2). What’s the difference? Medium
A) Abram was richer   B) Abram built a taller tower   C) None   D) God gives as a gift what Babel tried to grab for itself
Answer: D. God gives as a gift what Babel tried to grab for itself. Greatness that comes from God is received, not seized.


Q2. Multiple choice: God’s answer to the brokenness of Genesis 1–11 begins with what? Hard
A) One old man and his wife who couldn’t have children   B) A new flood   C) A new tower   D) A great army
Answer: A. One old man and his wife who couldn’t have children. God starts small and works through the unlikely.


Q3. Fill in the blank: God told Abram, “All peoples on earth will be ______ through you.” (12:3) Easy
Answer: blessed. God’s plan for one family was for the whole world.


Q4. True or false: In Egypt, Abram told people Sarai was his sister because he was afraid. (12:11–13) Medium
Answer: True. Right after a great step of faith, fear led him into a lie.


Q5. True or false: When Abram arrived in Canaan, he built an altar to the Lord. (12:7) Easy
Answer: True. His first act in the new land was worship.


Q6. Multiple choice: How old was Abram when he left Harran? (12:4) Easy
A) 100   B) 40   C) 99   D) 75
Answer: D. 75. It’s never too late to follow God.


Q7. Multiple choice: Why did God bless Abram? Hard
A) Because he asked   B) So Abram’s family would be a blessing to every nation   C) Because Abram was perfect   D) To make him king of Egypt
Answer: B. So Abram’s family would be a blessing to every nation. God’s people are blessed in order to be a blessing.


Q8. Multiple choice: God told Abram to leave his country, people and father’s household and go “to the land I will show you” (12:1). What did that require? Medium
A) A lot of money   B) Trusting God without knowing where he was going   C) A good map   D) An army
Answer: B. Trusting God without knowing where he was going. Hebrews 11:8 says Abraham obeyed “even though he did not know where he was going.”


Genesis 13
Q1. Multiple choice: Which land did Lot choose? (13:10–12) Easy
A) Egypt   B) The hill country   C) The well-watered plain of the Jordan, near Sodom   D) The desert
Answer: C. The well-watered plain of the Jordan, near Sodom. It looked like the garden of the Lord.


Q2. Multiple choice: Lot chose land because it looked well-watered (13:10). What did he ignore? Medium
A) The weather   B) The price   C) The distance   D) That the people of Sodom were very wicked
Answer: D. That the people of Sodom were very wicked. Choosing by appearance alone can lead us into trouble.


Q3. Multiple choice: Why could Abram afford to give Lot first choice? Hard
A) The land was all bad   B) He trusted God’s promise and didn’t need to grab   C) He didn’t care about land   D) He was afraid of Lot
Answer: B. He trusted God’s promise and didn’t need to grab. Faith frees us from having to win.


Q4. True or false: Abram let Lot choose his land first. (13:9) Easy
Answer: True. Abram didn’t need to grab the best for himself.


Q5. True or false: After Lot left, God repeated His promise of land to Abram. (13:14–17) Medium
Answer: True. Abram gave up first choice, and God gave him the whole land by promise.


Q6. Multiple choice: Lot first “pitched his tents near Sodom” (13:12). Later he lives in Sodom (14:12; 19:1). What does that show? Hard
A) Nothing   B) He got lost   C) Drifting away usually happens step by step   D) Sodom was a good place
Answer: C. Drifting away usually happens step by step. Small compromises add up.


Q7. Multiple choice: Abram said, “Let’s not have any quarreling between you and me” (13:8). What does that teach? Medium
A) Lot was right   B) Peace in a family is worth more than getting the best for yourself   C) Never disagree   D) Arguing is fun
Answer: B. Peace in a family is worth more than getting the best for yourself. Abram chose the relationship over the advantage.


Q8. Fill in the blank: God said Abram’s offspring would be like the ______ of the earth. (13:16) Easy
Answer: dust. Too many to count.


Genesis 14
Q1. Multiple choice: Lot had taken the best land for himself, yet Abram risked his life to rescue him. What does that show? Medium
A) Abram liked fighting   B) Love for family that doesn’t depend on how they treated you   C) He was ordered to   D) Abram wanted Lot’s land
Answer: B. Love for family that doesn’t depend on how they treated you. Abram didn’t hold Lot’s selfishness against him.


Q2. Multiple choice: Melchizedek said God Most High “delivered your enemies into your hand” (14:20). What is the lesson? Hard
A) Luck won the battle   B) The enemies gave up   C) Abram was a great general   D) The victory came from God, not from Abram’s strength
Answer: D. The victory came from God, not from Abram’s strength. Worship gives God the credit.


Q3. Multiple choice: Abram refused any reward from the king of Sodom (14:22–23). Why? Medium
A) He didn’t like gifts   B) It wasn’t enough   C) He was rich already   D) He didn’t want anyone but God to get credit for blessing him
Answer: D. He didn’t want anyone but God to get credit for blessing him. Abram wanted it clear that God, not Sodom, made him rich.


Q4. True or false: Melchizedek brought out bread and wine and blessed Abram. (14:18–19) Medium
Answer: True. A priest-king blessed the man God had chosen.


Q5. Multiple choice: Which relative was captured in a war, so that Abram went to rescue him? (14:12–14) Easy
A) Terah   B) Isaac   C) Nahor   D) Lot
Answer: D. Lot. Lot had been living in Sodom.


Q6. Fill in the blank: Melchizedek was king of Salem and priest of God ______ ______. (14:18) Easy
Answer: Most High. He served the same God Abram worshiped.


Q7. Multiple choice: Hebrews 7 compares Jesus to Melchizedek. Why? Hard
A) They were cousins   B) Both were fishermen   C) Melchizedek was both king and priest, and not from the tribe of Levi, just like Jesus   D) Both lived in Egypt
Answer: C. Melchizedek was both king and priest, and not from the tribe of Levi, just like Jesus. Melchizedek is a picture of Jesus, our King and our Priest.


Q8. True or false: Abram gave Melchizedek a tenth of everything. (14:20) Easy
Answer: True. Abram honored God by giving back part of what he had.


Genesis 15
Q1. Multiple choice: What did God tell Abram to count? (15:5) Easy
A) Sheep   B) His servants   C) The stars   D) Grains of sand
Answer: C. The stars. “So shall your offspring be.”


Q2. Multiple choice: Paul quotes Genesis 15:6 in Romans 4. What point does he make? Hard
A) Abraham was perfect   B) People are made right with God by faith, not by keeping the law   C) Circumcision saves   D) Only Jews can be saved
Answer: B. People are made right with God by faith, not by keeping the law. Abraham is the example of faith for everyone who believes.


Q3. Multiple choice: What is distinctive about the covenant ceremony Abram sees? (15:12, 17-18) Hard
A) Abram builds a temple   B) No promise is made   C) Abram walks through with a king   D) A smoking firepot and flaming torch pass between the pieces while Abram is in a deep sleep
Answer: D. A smoking firepot and flaming torch pass between the pieces while Abram is in a deep sleep. The course reads these symbols as God's solemn commitment to the promise. Genesis 17 also calls Abraham to faithful covenant obedience.


Q4. True or false: Abram was counted righteous because of all the good things he had done. Medium
Answer: False. He was counted righteous because he believed God, before he had obeyed any law.


Q5. Fill in the blank: “Abram believed the Lord, and he credited it to him as ______.” (15:6) Easy
Answer: righteousness. Abram was made right with God by trusting Him.


Q6. Multiple choice: Abram told God he was worried about having no heir (15:2–3). How did God respond? Medium
A) He ignored him   B) He answered with a promise   C) He gave him a servant as heir   D) He was angry
Answer: B. He answered with a promise. God welcomes honest prayers about our worries.


Q7. True or false: God told Abram, “I am your shield, your very great reward.” (15:1) Easy
Answer: True. God Himself was Abram’s protection and reward.


Q8. Multiple choice: God told Abram his descendants would be strangers and mistreated for 400 years (15:13). What does that teach? Medium
A) Abram did something wrong   B) God’s plan includes hard seasons He sees ahead of time   C) It wouldn’t happen   D) God changed His mind
Answer: B. God’s plan includes hard seasons He sees ahead of time. God also promised to bring them out (15:14).


Genesis 16
Q1. Multiple choice: Why did Sarai give Hagar to Abram? (16:2) Medium
A) She was tired of waiting and tried to get God’s promise her own way   B) God told her to   C) Abram demanded it   D) Hagar asked
Answer: A. She was tired of waiting and tried to get God’s promise her own way. Trying to force God’s plan causes pain.


Q2. Multiple choice: In chapter 15, Abram believed God. In chapter 16, he goes along with Sarai’s plan. What does that teach? Hard
A) Abram stopped believing   B) Faith can be strong one day and shaky the next, and waiting is hard   C) Sarai was in charge   D) Chapter 15 didn’t happen
Answer: B. Faith can be strong one day and shaky the next, and waiting is hard. Even people of great faith struggle to wait for God.


Q3. Multiple choice: Hagar is the first person in the Bible to give God a name. What does that show? Hard
A) She was in charge   B) Nothing   C) God sees and cares for outsiders and overlooked people   D) She was a priest
Answer: C. God sees and cares for outsiders and overlooked people. God welcomes people others push aside.


Q4. Fill in the blank: Hagar called God “the God who ______ me.” (16:13) Easy
Answer: sees. God saw her when no one else did.


Q5. Multiple choice: What happened after Sarai’s plan? (16:4–6) Medium
A) Peace and joy   B) Abram got rich   C) Jealousy, mistreatment and family conflict   D) Nothing
Answer: C. Jealousy, mistreatment and family conflict. Taking shortcuts around God hurt everyone involved.


Q6. True or false: The angel of the Lord found Hagar in the desert and spoke to her. (16:7) Medium
Answer: True. God went looking for a runaway foreign servant.


Q7. True or false: Hagar ran away into the desert. (16:6) Easy
Answer: True. She ran because Sarai was mistreating her.


Q8. Multiple choice: Who was Hagar? (16:1) Easy
A) Sarai’s Egyptian servant   B) Abram’s sister   C) Pharaoh’s daughter   D) Lot’s wife
Answer: A. Sarai’s Egyptian servant. She had no power in the household.


Genesis 17
Q1. Multiple choice: Abraham means “father of many,” but at that point he had only one son. Why give him that name? Medium
A) It was a mistake   B) God named him by what God was going to do   C) He had many servants   D) It was a joke
Answer: B. God named him by what God was going to do. God speaks of His promises as already sure.


Q2. Multiple choice: God said, “I am God Almighty; walk before me faithfully and be blameless” (17:1). What comes first? Medium
A) Abraham’s obedience   B) A list of rules   C) God showing who He is, and then calling Abraham to respond   D) A sacrifice
Answer: C. God showing who He is, and then calling Abraham to respond. Grace comes first, and obedience is the response.


Q3. Multiple choice: What new name did God give Abram? (17:5) Easy
A) Israel   B) Isaac   C) Adam   D) Abraham
Answer: D. Abraham. Abraham means “father of many.”


Q4. Multiple choice: Abraham laughed when told Sarah would have a son at 90 (17:17). How did God respond? Hard
A) He took the promise back   B) He punished Abraham   C) He repeated the promise and named the son Isaac, “he laughs”   D) He ignored him
Answer: C. He repeated the promise and named the son Isaac, “he laughs”. God was patient with Abraham’s doubt.


Q5. True or false: God also promised to bless Ishmael. (17:20) Medium
Answer: True. Ishmael would become a great nation, though the covenant would go through Isaac.


Q6. True or false: God changed Sarai’s name to Sarah. (17:15) Easy
Answer: True. She would be the mother of nations.


Q7. Multiple choice: Circumcision marked both God’s promise of a son and Abraham’s obedience. What does that teach about covenant? Hard
A) It’s one-sided   B) God makes the promise, and His people respond with trust and obedience   C) It’s only about health   D) It doesn’t matter
Answer: B. God makes the promise, and His people respond with trust and obedience. A covenant is a relationship: God commits first, and His people answer.


Q8. Fill in the blank: The sign of God’s covenant with Abraham was ______. (17:10–11) Easy
Answer: circumcision. Every male in the family was to carry the sign.


Genesis 18
Q1. Fill in the blank: “Is anything too ______ for the Lord?” (18:14) Easy
Answer: hard. Nothing is impossible for God.


Q2. Multiple choice: God said, “Shall I hide from Abraham what I am about to do?” (18:17). What does that show? Hard
A) God treated Abraham as a friend and shared His plans with him   B) God keeps everything secret   C) Abraham asked first   D) God needed advice
Answer: A. God treated Abraham as a friend and shared His plans with him. Abraham is called God’s friend (James 2:23).


Q3. True or false: Sarah laughed when she heard she would have a son. (18:12) Easy
Answer: True. She was about 90 and thought it was impossible.


Q4. Multiple choice: Abraham hurried to welcome three strangers with water and food (18:2–8). What does that teach? Medium
A) It was required by law   B) He wanted something   C) He was hungry   D) Welcoming strangers matters to God
Answer: D. Welcoming strangers matters to God. Hebrews 13:2 says some have welcomed angels without knowing it.


Q5. Multiple choice: Abraham asked, “Will not the Judge of all the earth do right?” (18:25). What does that show? Medium
A) He was angry   B) He doubted God   C) He wanted to be judge   D) He trusted God’s justice and felt free to speak honestly with Him
Answer: D. He trusted God’s justice and felt free to speak honestly with Him. Abraham appealed to God’s own character.


Q6. True or false: Sarah denied laughing because she was afraid. (18:15) Medium
Answer: True. God gently pointed out that she did laugh.


Q7. Multiple choice: Abraham kept asking God to spare Sodom. What was the smallest number of righteous people he asked about? (18:32) Easy
A) 50   B) 10   C) 1   D) 30
Answer: B. 10. God agreed to spare the city for ten.


Q8. Multiple choice: God let Abraham bargain with Him over Sodom. What does that show about God? Hard
A) God can be tricked   B) Abraham was in charge   C) God didn’t know what to do   D) God is relational and invites His people to pray for others
Answer: D. God is relational and invites His people to pray for others. God welcomes our prayers for others.


Genesis 19
Q1. Multiple choice: What happened to Lot’s wife when she looked back? (19:26) Easy
A) She went blind   B) She ran back   C) She became a pillar of salt   D) Nothing
Answer: C. She became a pillar of salt. Jesus later said, “Remember Lot’s wife!” (Luke 17:32).


Q2. Multiple choice: What reason does the narrator give for the messengers taking Lot's family by the hand? (19:15-16) Medium
A) The city had repented   B) Lot had paid them   C) Lot lingered, and the LORD was merciful to him   D) The gates were locked
Answer: C. Lot lingered, and the LORD was merciful to him. The text names both Lot's hesitation and God's mercy. It does not fully explain every family member's motives.


Q3. True or false: The angels told Lot’s family not to look back. (19:17) Easy
Answer: True. They were to flee and not stop.


Q4. Multiple choice: Lot hesitated, so the angels grabbed his hand, “for the Lord was merciful to them” (19:16). What does that teach? Medium
A) Lot was very brave   B) Lot was ready   C) Angels are rough   D) Rescue comes from God’s mercy, not from our being worthy
Answer: D. Rescue comes from God’s mercy, not from our being worthy. God pulled Lot out even when he dragged his feet.


Q5. True or false: God rescued Lot partly because He remembered Abraham. (19:29) Medium
Answer: True. Abraham’s prayers in chapter 18 mattered.


Q6. Fill in the blank: When Lot warned them, his sons-in-law thought he was ______. (19:14) Easy
Answer: joking. They didn’t take God’s warning seriously.


Q7. Multiple choice: Back in chapter 13, Lot chose the well-watered land near Sodom. Where did that choice lead? Hard
A) Choosing by appearance pulled him into a corrupt city   B) Nowhere   C) To great blessing   D) To Egypt
Answer: A. Choosing by appearance pulled him into a corrupt city. Small choices can shape where a whole life ends up.


Q8. Multiple choice: Jesus said, “Remember Lot’s wife!” (Luke 17:32). What was His point? Hard
A) Salt is dangerous   B) Women shouldn’t travel   C) Cities are bad   D) Don’t cling to your old life when God is rescuing you
Answer: D. Don’t cling to your old life when God is rescuing you. Following God means not looking back longingly at what He’s saving us from.


Genesis 20
Q1. True or false: A foreign king had to correct Abraham for what he had done. (20:9) Medium
Answer: True. Sometimes God uses outsiders to correct His people.


Q2. Multiple choice: How did God warn Abimelek? (20:3) Easy
A) Through a prophet   B) Through Sarah   C) In a dream   D) With a plague of frogs
Answer: C. In a dream. God spoke to a foreign king to protect Sarah.


Q3. Fill in the blank: Abraham ______ to God, and God healed Abimelek and his household. (20:17) Easy
Answer: prayed. Abraham interceded for the king.


Q4. True or false: Abraham told King Abimelek that Sarah was his sister. (20:2) Easy
Answer: True. He told the same half-truth he told in Egypt.


Q5. Multiple choice: Abraham said, “I said to myself, ‘There is surely no fear of God in this place’” (20:11). Was he right? Medium
A) Abimelek didn’t believe in God   B) Yes   C) We can’t tell   D) No. Abimelek listened to God and acted with more integrity than Abraham
Answer: D. No. Abimelek listened to God and acted with more integrity than Abraham. Abraham misjudged people he assumed were godless.


Q6. Multiple choice: Abraham told this same lie years earlier (chapter 12). What does that show? Medium
A) Even people of faith can fall back into old sins when they are afraid   B) He forgot   C) It wasn’t really a lie   D) It always worked
Answer: A. Even people of faith can fall back into old sins when they are afraid. Fear pulls us back to old habits.


Q7. Multiple choice: God stepped in to protect Sarah, through whom the promised son would come. What does that show? Hard
A) Abimelek was good   B) God protects His promise even when His people put it at risk   C) Sarah was in charge   D) Nothing
Answer: B. God protects His promise even when His people put it at risk. God’s faithfulness carries the promise forward.


Q8. Multiple choice: Right after Abraham’s failure, God still used him to pray for healing. What does that teach? Hard
A) Abraham earned it   B) God’s calling doesn’t rest on our being perfect   C) Sin doesn’t matter   D) Abimelek was wrong
Answer: B. God’s calling doesn’t rest on our being perfect. God uses flawed people without approving their sin.


Genesis 21
Q1. Multiple choice: “The Lord was gracious to Sarah as he had said” (21:1). What is the lesson? Medium
A) It was luck   B) God forgets   C) God keeps His promises, even when it takes 25 years   D) Sarah earned it
Answer: C. God keeps His promises, even when it takes 25 years. God’s timing is slow by our clocks, but He is faithful.


Q2. Multiple choice: Abraham called on the name of “the Eternal God” (21:33). Why does that name matter? Hard
A) It was a new god   B) It doesn’t   C) It means the God of Beersheba   D) Unlike local gods, He rules over all time and every place
Answer: D. Unlike local gods, He rules over all time and every place. Abraham’s God isn’t one god among many.


Q3. Multiple choice: Sarah once laughed in disbelief (18:12). Now she laughs with joy. What does that show? Hard
A) Sarah changed her mind for no reason   B) Laughing is wrong   C) Nothing   D) God can turn doubt into joy
Answer: D. God can turn doubt into joy. God was patient with Sarah’s doubt and kept His word anyway.


Q4. Fill in the blank: Sarah said, “God has brought me ______.” (21:6) Easy
Answer: laughter. Isaac’s name means “he laughs.”


Q5. True or false: Abraham was happy to send Hagar and Ishmael away. Medium
Answer: False. It “distressed Abraham greatly” (21:11).


Q6. True or false: God took care of Hagar and Ishmael in the desert. (21:17–20) Easy
Answer: True. God showed Hagar a well and was with the boy as he grew up.


Q7. Multiple choice: “God heard the boy crying” (21:17). Ishmael’s name means “God hears.” What does this show? Medium
A) God hears those who have been pushed out   B) Only the chosen son matters   C) Ishmael was loud   D) God was angry
Answer: A. God hears those who have been pushed out. God cared for the outsider.


Q8. Multiple choice: How old was Abraham when Isaac was born? (21:5) Easy
A) 75   B) 99   C) 86   D) 100
Answer: D. 100. A hundred years old.


Genesis 22
Q1. Multiple choice: What is the main question in this story? Hard
A) Will Abraham trust God even when God asks the unthinkable?   B) Will Abraham find a lamb?   C) Does God want child sacrifice?   D) Can Isaac carry the wood?
Answer: A. Will Abraham trust God even when God asks the unthinkable?. God stopped it, which shows He doesn’t want child sacrifice. The test was about trust: obedience, faith, provision, blessing.


Q2. Multiple choice: When God called, Abraham answered, “Here I am” (22:1). What does that show? Medium
A) He was ready to listen and obey   B) He was hiding   C) He was confused   D) He didn’t hear
Answer: A. He was ready to listen and obey. “Here I am” is the answer of someone available to God.


Q3. Multiple choice: What did God provide to be sacrificed in Isaac’s place? (22:13) Easy
A) A lamb   B) A goat   C) A ram caught in a thicket   D) A dove
Answer: C. A ram caught in a thicket. A ram took Isaac’s place.


Q4. Multiple choice: Isaac carried the wood up the mountain, and God provided a substitute. What do Christians see pictured here? Hard
A) Nothing   B) The flood   C) The Tower of Babel   D) Jesus carrying His cross, and God providing His own Son in our place
Answer: D. Jesus carrying His cross, and God providing His own Son in our place. What God spared Abraham from doing, He did Himself.


Q5. True or false: Abraham’s trust on the mountain came out of nowhere. Medium
Answer: False. It grew from a lifetime of seeing God keep His word, including the birth of Isaac.


Q6. Multiple choice: Isaac asked, “Where is the lamb?” Abraham said, “God himself will provide the lamb” (22:7–8). What does that show? Medium
A) Abraham trusted God before he saw the answer   B) Abraham was lying   C) Isaac was afraid   D) Abraham forgot the lamb
Answer: A. Abraham trusted God before he saw the answer. Faith trusts God before the answer comes.


Q7. True or false: God stopped Abraham before he killed Isaac. (22:11-13) Easy
Answer: True. The messenger stopped him, and a ram was offered in Isaac's place. The chapter introduces the command as a test in verse 1.


Q8. Fill in the blank: Abraham named the place “The Lord Will ______.” (22:14) Easy
Answer: Provide. God provided what Abraham needed at the last moment.


Genesis 23
Q1. True or false: Abraham mourned and wept for Sarah. (23:2) Easy
Answer: True. A man of great faith still grieved deeply.


Q2. Multiple choice: Abraham grieved deeply and still acted in faith. What can we learn? Hard
A) Grief means faith is weak   B) Real faith means not being sad   C) We should hide our sadness   D) Faith and grief can exist together
Answer: D. Faith and grief can exist together. God doesn’t ask us to pretend loss doesn’t hurt.


Q3. True or false: Abraham paid 400 shekels of silver for the field. (23:16) Medium
Answer: True. He paid full price.


Q4. Multiple choice: How does buying a burial place for Sarah relate to the promise of land? (23:4, 17-20; 12:7) Hard
A) Abraham already owns all Canaan   B) He secures a family burial place while still living as a foreigner   C) He abandons Canaan   D) He conquers Hebron
Answer: B. He secures a family burial place while still living as a foreigner. The purchase gives Abraham a defined holding in the promised land without completing the wider land promise.


Q5. Multiple choice: How old was Sarah when she died? (23:1) Easy
A) 90   B) 175   C) 127   D) 100
Answer: C. 127. She lived to see the son God promised.


Q6. Fill in the blank: Abraham bought the cave of ______ to bury Sarah. (23:19) Easy
Answer: Machpelah. Abraham, Isaac and Jacob would be buried there too.


Q7. Multiple choice: Abraham called himself “a foreigner and stranger among you” (23:4). What does that teach? Medium
A) He was ashamed   B) God’s people live as travelers, still waiting for God’s full promise   C) He was lost   D) He wanted to move
Answer: B. God’s people live as travelers, still waiting for God’s full promise. Hebrews 11:13 says they were “foreigners and strangers on earth.”


Q8. Multiple choice: Ephron offered the field as a gift, but Abraham insisted on paying for it. Why? Medium
A) He didn’t trust gifts   B) He was showing off   C) He had too much money   D) He wanted clear ownership, so no one could ever say the land wasn’t his
Answer: D. He wanted clear ownership, so no one could ever say the land wasn’t his. He wanted a real, lasting claim in the land God promised.


Genesis 24
Q1. Multiple choice: Rebekah left her home for a land she had never seen, just as Abraham did. What does that teach? Hard
A) It was a vacation   B) She was forced to   C) She didn’t like her family   D) Faith means stepping toward what God is doing even without seeing all of it
Answer: D. Faith means stepping toward what God is doing even without seeing all of it. Rebekah’s “I will go” echoes Abraham’s obedience.


Q2. Multiple choice: Watering ten thirsty camels is hard work. What did it show about Rebekah? Medium
A) She was generous and hardworking   B) She was showing off   C) She liked camels   D) She owned the well
Answer: A. She was generous and hardworking. The servant looked for character, not just appearance.


Q3. Multiple choice: The servant isn’t named in this chapter, but he’s one of its most faithful people. What does that show? Hard
A) It’s a mistake   B) God works through faithful, ordinary people whose names we may never know   C) He didn’t matter   D) He was a prince
Answer: B. God works through faithful, ordinary people whose names we may never know. Faithfulness matters more than fame.


Q4. Fill in the blank: When asked if she would go with the servant, Rebekah said, “I will ______.” (24:58) Easy
Answer: go. She said yes to a journey into the unknown.


Q5. Multiple choice: Before Rebekah arrived, the servant prayed for God to guide him (24:12–14). What does that teach? Medium
A) He was lost   B) We can bring everyday decisions to God   C) Only big decisions need prayer   D) Prayer is only for priests
Answer: B. We can bring everyday decisions to God. The servant asked, and God led him.


Q6. Multiple choice: Who did Abraham’s servant find to be Isaac’s wife? Easy
A) Rebekah   B) Leah   C) Hagar   D) Rachel
Answer: A. Rebekah. Rebekah, from Abraham’s relatives.


Q7. True or false: Rebekah offered to water all of the servant’s camels. (24:19) Easy
Answer: True. That was exactly the sign the servant had prayed for.


Q8. True or false: The servant bowed down and praised God when his prayer was answered. (24:26–27) Medium
Answer: True. He gave God the credit right away.


Genesis 25
Q1. Multiple choice: God chose the younger twin before either was born. What does that show about how God chooses? Hard
A) His choice wasn’t based on birth order or on anything they had done   B) He chooses the strongest   C) He chooses at random   D) Jacob earned it
Answer: A. His choice wasn’t based on birth order or on anything they had done. God’s blessing is a gift of grace.


Q2. Multiple choice: Rebekah couldn’t have children for twenty years (25:20, 26). What pattern keeps showing up in the promised family? Hard
A) The family keeps depending on God for life itself: Sarah, Rebekah, and later Rachel   B) Bad luck   C) Nothing   D) They didn’t want children
Answer: A. The family keeps depending on God for life itself: Sarah, Rebekah, and later Rachel. The promise moves forward by God’s power, not human strength.


Q3. True or false: Isaac loved Esau, and Rebekah loved Jacob. (25:28) Medium
Answer: True. Each parent had a favorite, and it set up years of trouble.


Q4. Multiple choice: “So Esau despised his birthright” (25:34). What does that mean? Medium
A) He was angry at Jacob   B) He lost it in a game   C) He didn’t know what it was   D) He treated something precious from God as worth less than a moment’s hunger
Answer: D. He treated something precious from God as worth less than a moment’s hunger. It’s easy to trade what lasts for what we want right now.


Q5. Multiple choice: What did Esau trade his birthright for? (25:34) Easy
A) Some silver   B) A bowl of red stew   C) A bow   D) A sheep
Answer: B. A bowl of red stew. He gave up something precious for one meal.


Q6. Multiple choice: Isaac and Ishmael buried their father Abraham together (25:9). What does that show? Medium
A) They were enemies   B) Divided family members can still come together, and God kept His promise to Ishmael too   C) Nothing   D) Ishmael was the heir
Answer: B. Divided family members can still come together, and God kept His promise to Ishmael too. Ishmael’s family grew just as God said it would (25:12–18).


Q7. True or false: Isaac prayed for Rebekah because she couldn’t have children. (25:21) Easy
Answer: True. God answered, and she had twins.


Q8. Fill in the blank: God told Rebekah, “The older will serve the ______.” (25:23) Easy
Answer: younger. God’s plan didn’t follow the usual order.


Genesis 26
Q1. True or false: Isaac planted crops during a famine and reaped a hundred times what he planted. (26:12) Medium
Answer: True. “The Lord blessed him.”


Q2. Multiple choice: King Abimelek told Isaac, “We saw clearly that the Lord was with you” (26:28). What does that show? Hard
A) Abimelek was scared of wells   B) Outsiders notice when God is with someone, so a peaceful, blessed life is a witness   C) Nothing   D) Isaac bragged a lot
Answer: B. Outsiders notice when God is with someone, so a peaceful, blessed life is a witness. God’s people are blessed to be a blessing and a sign to others.


Q3. Multiple choice: When others fought over his wells, Isaac kept moving and digging new ones. What does that show? Medium
A) He was lost   B) He chose peace and trusted God to provide   C) He was weak   D) He liked digging
Answer: B. He chose peace and trusted God to provide. Isaac didn’t have to win every fight to be blessed.


Q4. Multiple choice: Isaac told the men of Gerar that Rebekah was his what? (26:7) Easy
A) Servant   B) Sister   C) Cousin   D) Daughter
Answer: B. Sister. He was afraid they would kill him to take her.


Q5. Multiple choice: God told Isaac He would bless him “because Abraham obeyed me” (26:3–5). What does that teach? Hard
A) Obedience doesn’t matter   B) The faithfulness of one generation can bless the next   C) Isaac didn’t matter   D) Only Abraham was blessed
Answer: B. The faithfulness of one generation can bless the next. God keeps His promises to families across generations.


Q6. Fill in the blank: Isaac named a well Rehoboth, saying, “Now the Lord has given us ______.” (26:22) Easy
Answer: room. After moving on from fights, Isaac found space to flourish.


Q7. True or false: During a famine, God told Isaac not to go down to Egypt. (26:2) Easy
Answer: True. God told him to stay in the land and promised to be with him.


Q8. Multiple choice: Isaac’s lie about Rebekah is the same lie Abraham told twice. What does that show? Medium
A) It was a family joke   B) It was a custom   C) Sin often repeats in families   D) Rebekah asked him to
Answer: C. Sin often repeats in families. Children often pick up their parents’ weaknesses as well as their faith.


Genesis 27
Q1. Multiple choice: After the trick, Esau planned to kill Jacob (27:41). What does that show? Medium
A) Esau was always violent   B) Deception breaks families apart and sin leads to more sin   C) Nothing   D) Jacob deserved it
Answer: B. Deception breaks families apart and sin leads to more sin. Like Cain and Abel, a brother’s anger turns toward violence.


Q2. True or false: Isaac could barely see when he blessed Jacob. (27:1) Easy
Answer: True. His eyes were so weak he couldn’t tell his sons apart.


Q3. Multiple choice: Whose idea was the plan to trick Isaac? (27:5–10) Medium
A) Rebekah’s   B) Esau’s   C) A servant’s   D) Isaac’s
Answer: A. Rebekah’s. Isaac favored Esau and Rebekah favored Jacob. Favoritism split the family.


Q4. Multiple choice: What did Jacob wear to fool his father? (27:15–16) Easy
A) A mask   B) A crown   C) Esau’s clothes and goat skins on his hands and neck   D) Isaac’s robe
Answer: C. Esau’s clothes and goat skins on his hands and neck. He made himself feel and smell like Esau.


Q5. True or false: Jacob had to leave home because of what he did. (27:43) Medium
Answer: True. Getting the blessing by deceit cost him his home and family for many years.


Q6. Fill in the blank: Isaac said, “The voice is the voice of ______, but the hands are the hands of Esau.” (27:22) Easy
Answer: Jacob. Isaac sensed something was wrong but went ahead anyway.


Q7. Multiple choice: God had already said the older would serve the younger (25:23). Did Rebekah and Jacob need to trick Isaac? Hard
A) Yes, or God’s plan would fail   B) No. Grabbing God’s promises our own way causes damage. God could keep His word without their lies   C) It doesn’t matter   D) Yes, Isaac deserved it
Answer: B. No. Grabbing God’s promises our own way causes damage. God could keep His word without their lies. Like Sarah with Hagar, trying to force God’s plan brings pain.


Q8. Multiple choice: God still worked through this deceiving family. Does that mean God approved of what they did? Hard
A) God didn’t know   B) No. God uses flawed people without approving their sin   C) Only Isaac was wrong   D) Yes
Answer: B. No. God uses flawed people without approving their sin. God’s faithfulness doesn’t depend on ours.


Genesis 28
Q1. True or false: Jacob named the place Bethel, which means “house of God.” (28:19) Easy
Answer: True. An ordinary spot became holy because God met him there.


Q2. Multiple choice: Jacob was running away after tricking his family. Why is it surprising that God met him? Medium
A) Jacob was praying   B) It isn’t surprising   C) Jacob had earned it   D) God showed him grace before Jacob had changed
Answer: D. God showed him grace before Jacob had changed. Grace comes first. Change is the response.


Q3. Multiple choice: God promised, “I am with you and will watch over you wherever you go” (28:15). What does that teach? Medium
A) God’s presence goes with His people anywhere   B) Only Bethel is holy   C) God stays in one place   D) Jacob was safe from all trouble
Answer: A. God’s presence goes with His people anywhere. God isn’t limited to a place.


Q4. Multiple choice: How should Jacob's vow be distinguished from God's earlier promise in the chapter? (28:13-22) Hard
A) God refuses to speak   B) Jacob buys God's protection   C) God promises His presence before Jacob responds with a vow   D) Jacob makes the promise first
Answer: C. God promises His presence before Jacob responds with a vow. The promise precedes Jacob's response. Interpreters differ on whether the vow's wording expresses bargaining or a response of trust.


Q5. Multiple choice: What did Jacob see in his dream? (28:12) Easy
A) Seven cows   B) A rainbow   C) A stairway reaching to heaven with angels going up and down   D) A burning bush
Answer: C. A stairway reaching to heaven with angels going up and down. Heaven and earth were connected, and God stood above it.


Q6. True or false: God promised that all peoples on earth would be blessed through Jacob’s offspring. (28:14) Medium
Answer: True. The same promise God gave Abraham.


Q7. Fill in the blank: Jacob woke up and said, “Surely the Lord is in this place, and I was not ______ of it.” (28:16) Easy
Answer: aware. God was with him even when he didn’t realize it.


Q8. Multiple choice: Jesus said people would see “the angels of God ascending and descending on the Son of Man” (John 1:51). What was He saying? Hard
A) Bethel is in heaven   B) He had a dream like Jacob   C) Angels live on stairs   D) He Himself is the real connection between heaven and earth
Answer: D. He Himself is the real connection between heaven and earth. Jesus is the way to God that Jacob’s stairway pictured.


Genesis 29
Q1. Multiple choice: “When the Lord saw that Leah was not loved...” (29:31). What does that show about God? Medium
A) He only notices important people   B) He was angry with Leah   C) He sees the overlooked and unloved   D) Nothing
Answer: C. He sees the overlooked and unloved. No one is invisible to God.


Q2. Multiple choice: Where did Jacob first meet Rachel? (29:9–10) Easy
A) At a well   B) In a field of grain   C) In a tent   D) At a feast
Answer: A. At a well. Like Isaac’s servant meeting Rebekah, the story begins at a well.


Q3. True or false: When Judah was born, Leah said, “This time I will praise the Lord.” (29:35) Medium
Answer: True. Her focus turned from winning Jacob’s love to praising God.


Q4. Fill in the blank: Jacob worked seven years for Rachel, but “they seemed like only a few ______ to him because of his love for her.” (29:20) Easy
Answer: days. Love made the work feel light.


Q5. Multiple choice: Unloved Leah became the mother of Judah (the line of kings and Jesus) and Levi (the priests). What does that teach? Hard
A) God works powerfully through people others overlook   B) Nothing   C) It was luck   D) Leah was secretly favored by Jacob
Answer: A. God works powerfully through people others overlook. God looks at the heart, not at who is popular.


Q6. True or false: Laban tricked Jacob into marrying Leah first. (29:25) Easy
Answer: True. Jacob then worked seven more years for Rachel.


Q7. Multiple choice: What contrast appears between Leah's naming of her first three sons and her naming of Judah? (29:32-35) Hard
A) She stops having children forever   B) The earlier explanations mention her affliction or hoped-for affection; Judah's emphasizes praising the LORD   C) She leaves Jacob   D) Jacob finally praises her
Answer: B. The earlier explanations mention her affliction or hoped-for affection; Judah's emphasizes praising the LORD. Leah says she will praise the LORD when Judah is born. The text does not explicitly describe a complete change in her sense of identity.


Q8. Multiple choice: Jacob asked Laban, “Why have you deceived me?” (29:25). Why is that ironic? Medium
A) Jacob was blind   B) Jacob had deceived his own father, and now he was on the other side   C) It isn’t   D) Laban was honest
Answer: B. Jacob had deceived his own father, and now he was on the other side. The deceiver got deceived.


Genesis 30
Q1. Multiple choice: Rachel cried, “Give me children, or I’ll die!” Jacob answered, “Am I in the place of God?” (30:1–2). What’s the lesson? Medium
A) Jacob was unkind   B) Life is God’s gift, and only He can give it   C) Nothing   D) Rachel was wrong to want children
Answer: B. Life is God’s gift, and only He can give it. Some things only God can do.


Q2. Multiple choice: Jacob tried a breeding plan with streaked branches (30:37–43). Later, who did Jacob say really made his flocks grow? (31:9) Hard
A) God   B) His own cleverness   C) Rachel   D) Laban
Answer: A. God. Jacob came to see that God, not his tricks, was behind his success.


Q3. Multiple choice: What was the name of Rachel’s first son? (30:24) Easy
A) Benjamin   B) Reuben   C) Joseph   D) Judah
Answer: C. Joseph. Joseph, who becomes the hero of the end of Genesis.


Q4. Fill in the blank: “Then God ______ Rachel; he listened to her and enabled her to conceive.” (30:22) Easy
Answer: remembered. God heard her prayer.


Q5. True or false: Jacob’s flocks grew large even though Laban tried to cheat him. (30:43) Medium
Answer: True. God kept blessing Jacob.


Q6. Multiple choice: “God remembered Rachel” sounds like “God remembered Noah” (8:1). What does “remembered” mean here? Hard
A) It means nothing   B) God acted on His care for her   C) God had forgotten her   D) Rachel reminded God
Answer: B. God acted on His care for her. In the Bible, God remembering means God stepping in to act.


Q7. Multiple choice: The sisters competed by giving their servants to Jacob. What did that lead to? Medium
A) Peace   B) Nothing   C) Laban’s blessing   D) More rivalry and jealousy
Answer: D. More rivalry and jealousy. Grabbing blessings our own way, like Sarah did with Hagar, causes more pain.


Q8. True or false: Rachel and Leah got along well. Easy
Answer: False. They competed with each other for Jacob’s love and for children.


Genesis 31
Q1. True or false: God warned Laban in a dream not to harm Jacob. (31:24) Medium
Answer: True. God protected Jacob even when Jacob couldn’t protect himself.


Q2. Multiple choice: Rachel stole her father’s household gods. What does that suggest about her? Medium
A) She wanted to sell them   B) She hated her father   C) She was still attached to idols, and old habits didn’t disappear right away   D) They were Jacob’s
Answer: C. She was still attached to idols, and old habits didn’t disappear right away. Later the family buries their idols (35:2). Following God is a process.


Q3. Fill in the blank: Jacob worked for Laban for ______ years. (31:38) Easy
Answer: twenty. Fourteen for his wives and six for his flocks.


Q4. Multiple choice: Jacob the deceiver was deceived by Laban for twenty years. What does that show? Hard
A) Jacob deserved worse   B) It was random   C) Laban was good   D) We often reap what we sow, and God used it to shape Jacob
Answer: D. We often reap what we sow, and God used it to shape Jacob. God used hard years to humble Jacob.


Q5. Multiple choice: Laban changed Jacob’s wages ten times (31:7). How did Jacob still do well? Medium
A) Laban gave up   B) God protected and blessed him   C) Luck   D) He cheated back
Answer: B. God protected and blessed him. Jacob said, “God has not allowed him to harm me.”


Q6. Multiple choice: What did Rachel secretly take from her father Laban? (31:19) Easy
A) His household gods   B) His sheep   C) His robe   D) Gold
Answer: A. His household gods. She hid them and didn’t tell Jacob.


Q7. Multiple choice: Jacob and Laban set up a pile of stones and said, “May the Lord keep watch between you and me” (31:49). What were they really saying? Hard
A) A warning between two men who didn’t trust each other: God sees what you do   B) A prayer for rain   C) A sweet goodbye   D) A promise to visit
Answer: A. A warning between two men who didn’t trust each other: God sees what you do. They knew God is a witness to how we treat each other.


Q8. True or false: God told Jacob to go back to the land of his fathers. (31:3) Easy
Answer: True. God promised, “I will be with you.”


Genesis 32
Q1. Multiple choice: How many men were coming with Esau? (32:6) Easy
A) 40   B) 400   C) 4,000   D) 12
Answer: B. 400. Jacob was terrified.


Q2. True or false: Jacob walked away from the wrestling match with a limp. (32:31) Medium
Answer: True. Meeting God changed him, and his limp reminded him to depend on God.


Q3. True or false: Jacob wrestled with a man until daybreak. (32:24) Easy
Answer: True. He wouldn’t let go.


Q4. Fill in the blank: After wrestling, Jacob was given a new name: ______. (32:28) Easy
Answer: Israel. His whole nation would carry that name.


Q5. Multiple choice: “Israel” means “he struggles with God.” What does it say that God’s people carry that name? Hard
A) A real relationship with God includes honest struggle, not just easy obedience   B) God dislikes them   C) They always lose   D) Nothing
Answer: A. A real relationship with God includes honest struggle, not just easy obedience. God doesn’t want fake faith. He meets us in the wrestling.


Q6. Multiple choice: Jacob prayed, “I am unworthy of all the kindness and faithfulness you have shown your servant” (32:10). What had changed? Medium
A) He was rich now   B) The schemer had become humble and dependent on God   C) Nothing   D) He wanted more
Answer: B. The schemer had become humble and dependent on God. Jacob finally saw that everything he had came from God.


Q7. Multiple choice: Jacob said, “I will not let you go unless you bless me” (32:26). What does that teach about prayer? Medium
A) God welcomes persistent, honest seeking   B) Prayer should be short   C) God gets annoyed with us   D) We can force God’s hand
Answer: A. God welcomes persistent, honest seeking. Jacob held on to God, and God blessed him.


Q8. Multiple choice: Jacob prayed for help, and he also sent gifts ahead and divided his camp. What does that show? Hard
A) He was confused   B) Gifts are better than prayer   C) Prayer and wise planning can go together   D) He didn’t trust God
Answer: C. Prayer and wise planning can go together. Trusting God doesn’t mean doing nothing.


Genesis 33
Q1. Multiple choice: How many times did Jacob bow as he approached Esau? (33:3) Easy
A) Twelve times   B) Three times   C) Once   D) Seven times
Answer: D. Seven times. Seven times, a sign of deep respect.


Q2. Multiple choice: How did Esau's greeting differ from the violence Jacob had feared? (32:11; 33:4) Medium
A) Esau ran to him, embraced him and wept   B) Esau imprisoned him   C) Esau refused to speak   D) Esau attacked him
Answer: A. Esau ran to him, embraced him and wept. The reunion contrasts Jacob's fear with Esau's welcome. The narrator does not describe all that happened in Esau's heart beforehand.


Q3. Multiple choice: Jacob says seeing Esau’s face is like seeing God’s face, just after he wrestled with God. What connects the two? Hard
A) Esau looked like God   B) Being made right with God and being made right with people go together   C) Jacob was confused   D) Nothing
Answer: B. Being made right with God and being made right with people go together. Loving God and loving others can’t be separated.


Q4. True or false: Esau ran to meet Jacob and hugged him. (33:4) Easy
Answer: True. They both wept.


Q5. Fill in the blank: Jacob told Esau, “To see your face is like seeing the face of ______.” (33:10) Easy
Answer: God. Being welcomed back felt like grace.


Q6. Multiple choice: Esau ran, embraced and kissed his brother. Which of Jesus’ parables looks a lot like this? Hard
A) The prodigal son’s father running to his son   B) The mustard seed   C) The sower   D) The lost coin
Answer: A. The prodigal son’s father running to his son. Both show a welcome that comes before it’s deserved.


Q7. Multiple choice: Jacob insisted Esau keep his gifts (33:11). Why? Medium
A) To try to make right what he had taken   B) Esau was poor   C) To show off   D) He didn’t want them
Answer: A. To try to make right what he had taken. Jacob’s gift was a way of saying sorry.


Q8. True or false: Jacob built an altar and called it El Elohe Israel, meaning “God, the God of Israel.” (33:20) Medium
Answer: True. God was now Jacob’s own God, not just his father’s.


Genesis 34
Q1. Multiple choice: The Bible tells this dark story without making the brothers heroes. Why include it? Hard
A) Scripture tells the truth about flawed people without approving their sin   B) To entertain   C) It’s a mistake   D) To approve of what they did
Answer: A. Scripture tells the truth about flawed people without approving their sin. The Bible is honest about its people, and that’s part of why it can be trusted.


Q2. Fill in the blank: Jacob told Simeon and Levi, “You have brought ______ on me.” (34:30) Easy
Answer: trouble. Their violence put the whole family in danger.


Q3. True or false: Simeon and Levi attacked the city of Shechem. (34:25) Easy
Answer: True. They took violent revenge on the whole city.


Q4. True or false: God told Simeon and Levi to attack Shechem. Medium
Answer: False. It was their own revenge, and Jacob later condemned it (49:5–7).


Q5. Multiple choice: Who was Jacob’s daughter in this chapter? (34:1) Easy
A) Tamar   B) Rachel   C) Dinah   D) Leah
Answer: C. Dinah. Dinah was badly wronged by a man named Shechem.


Q6. Multiple choice: Jacob seemed mostly worried about his own reputation (34:30). What was missing from his response? Medium
A) Anger   B) Nothing   C) A plan   D) Care for Dinah and concern for what was right
Answer: D. Care for Dinah and concern for what was right. Jacob stayed passive when his daughter needed him.


Q7. Multiple choice: The brothers used circumcision, the sign of God’s covenant, as part of a trick. Why is that so serious? Medium
A) It made them sick   B) It isn’t   C) Circumcision was new   D) They used a holy sign to deceive and kill
Answer: D. They used a holy sign to deceive and kill. Using God’s things for our own revenge is a terrible misuse.


Q8. Multiple choice: What does this chapter show about where revenge leads? Hard
A) One wrong answered with another, worse wrong   B) Nothing happens   C) It makes things right   D) It brings peace
Answer: A. One wrong answered with another, worse wrong. Sin escalates into violence, as it did with Cain and Lamech.


Genesis 35
Q1. Fill in the blank: Rachel named her son Ben-Oni, but Jacob named him ______. (35:18) Easy
Answer: Benjamin. Rachel died giving birth to him.


Q2. Multiple choice: Where did God tell Jacob to go and build an altar? (35:1) Easy
A) Jerusalem   B) Haran   C) Egypt   D) Bethel
Answer: D. Bethel. Bethel, where God first met him.


Q3. True or false: Jacob told his household to get rid of their foreign gods. (35:2) Easy
Answer: True. They buried them before going to worship God.


Q4. Multiple choice: God promises Jacob nations, kings and land (35:11–12). It’s the same promise given to Abraham. What does that show? Hard
A) God’s promise is passed down and He stays faithful across generations   B) Jacob didn’t listen   C) Abraham failed   D) God repeats Himself for no reason
Answer: A. God’s promise is passed down and He stays faithful across generations. The covenant rests on God’s faithfulness.


Q5. True or false: Jacob and Esau buried their father Isaac together. (35:29) Medium
Answer: True. The brothers who had been divided stood side by side.


Q6. Multiple choice: Why did Jacob have his family get rid of their foreign gods before going to Bethel? Medium
A) They were heavy   B) They were broken   C) Esau wanted them   D) Worshiping God means removing anything that competes with Him
Answer: D. Worshiping God means removing anything that competes with Him. Idolatry is the main sin Israel struggles with all through the Old Testament.


Q7. Multiple choice: God told Jacob again, “Your name will be Israel” (35:10). Why repeat it? Medium
A) Jacob asked for a new name   B) To confuse Esau   C) God forgot   D) To remind Jacob of the identity God had given him
