Answer: D. To remind Jacob of the identity God had given him. Our identity is what God calls us.


Q8. Multiple choice: Bethel is where God met Jacob when he was running away (chapter 28). Why does it matter that he came back? Hard
A) It doesn’t   B) He forgot something   C) Esau lived there   D) Jacob returns to keep his vow. God finishes what He starts
Answer: D. Jacob returns to keep his vow. God finishes what He starts. God brought Jacob all the way back, just as He promised.


Genesis 36
Q1. Multiple choice: Esau is recorded in Scripture even though the promise passed through Jacob. What does that teach? Hard
A) Esau was secretly chosen   B) God cares for people outside the line of promise too, as He did for Hagar and Ishmael   C) Only the chosen line matters   D) Nothing
Answer: B. God cares for people outside the line of promise too, as He did for Hagar and Ishmael. God’s choosing one family was so that all peoples could be blessed.


Q2. Fill in the blank: “This is the account of the family line of Esau (that is, ______).” (36:1) Easy
Answer: Edom. Esau’s other name became the name of his nation.


Q3. Multiple choice: Why does Genesis include a long list of Esau’s family? Medium
A) It shows God blessed Esau too, as Isaac said he would   B) Esau was the chosen son   C) To fill space   D) It’s a mistake
Answer: A. It shows God blessed Esau too, as Isaac said he would. Not being in the line of the promise didn’t mean Esau was forgotten.


Q4. True or false: Esau and Jacob separated because the land couldn’t support both of their flocks. (36:6–7) Easy
Answer: True. Both brothers had been blessed with a lot.


Q5. True or false: After Esau lost the blessing, God forgot about him completely. Medium
Answer: False. Esau grew into a large family and nation.


Q6. Multiple choice: Edom and Israel later became enemies. Looking back to Jacob and Esau, what does that show? Hard
A) Esau won   B) Family conflict left unhealed can grow into conflict between whole peoples   C) It’s unrelated   D) Nations are always enemies
Answer: B. Family conflict left unhealed can grow into conflict between whole peoples. Sin and broken relationships spread from one generation to the next.


Q7. Multiple choice: Edom had kings long before Israel did (36:31). What does that show? Medium
A) Being chosen by God doesn’t mean being first in worldly success   B) Israel didn’t want kings   C) Nothing   D) Edom was God’s favorite
Answer: A. Being chosen by God doesn’t mean being first in worldly success. God’s timing and the world’s timing are different.


Q8. Multiple choice: Esau’s descendants became which nation? (36:1, 9) Easy
A) Israel   B) Edom   C) Babylon   D) Egypt
Answer: B. Edom. Edom, Israel’s neighbor to the south.


Genesis 37
Q1. True or false: Reuben planned to rescue Joseph from the pit and take him back to their father. (37:22) Medium
Answer: True. But he wasn’t there when the others sold Joseph.


Q2. Multiple choice: Joseph was 17 and told everyone his dreams. What can we learn from how he handled them? Hard
A) Never share what God shows you   B) Joseph was lying   C) Even true gifts from God need to be handled with humility and wisdom   D) Dreams don’t matter
Answer: C. Even true gifts from God need to be handled with humility and wisdom. Joseph started out proud but became faithful. God shapes us over time.


Q3. True or false: The brothers let Jacob believe a wild animal had killed Joseph. (37:31–33) Easy
Answer: True. They dipped his robe in goat’s blood and let their father grieve.


Q4. Multiple choice: What did Joseph’s dreams show? (37:5–11) Medium
A) A new robe   B) A trip to Egypt   C) A coming flood   D) His family bowing down to him
Answer: D. His family bowing down to him. God was showing what was ahead, though no one understood it yet.


Q5. Multiple choice: The brothers deceived Jacob with Joseph’s robe and goat’s blood. How does that echo Jacob’s own past? Hard
A) Jacob once deceived his father using Esau’s clothes and goat skins   B) Jacob had a robe too   C) It doesn’t   D) Jacob was a shepherd
Answer: A. Jacob once deceived his father using Esau’s clothes and goat skins. Sin repeats in families. The deceiver is now deceived by his own sons.


Q6. Multiple choice: What special gift did Jacob give Joseph? (37:3) Easy
A) A donkey   B) A sword   C) An ornate robe   D) A ring
Answer: C. An ornate robe. It showed Jacob loved Joseph more than his other sons.


Q7. Multiple choice: Where did the brothers’ hatred start? (37:3–4) Medium
A) Pharaoh   B) Joseph stole from them   C) Their father openly favored Joseph   D) Joseph was older
Answer: C. Their father openly favored Joseph. Favoritism in a family hurts everyone. Isaac and Rebekah each had favorites too.


Q8. Fill in the blank: Joseph’s brothers sold him for twenty shekels of ______. (37:28) Easy
Answer: silver. They sold their own brother as a slave.


Genesis 38
Q1. Fill in the blank: Judah admitted, “She is more ______ than I.” (38:26) Easy
Answer: righteous. Judah finally owned his own wrong.


Q2. Multiple choice: Why is Judah’s confession, “She is more righteous than I,” a turning point? Hard
A) He moved to Egypt   B) It’s the first sign Judah is changing, which leads to him offering his life for Benjamin in chapter 44   C) It made him king   D) It isn’t
Answer: B. It’s the first sign Judah is changing, which leads to him offering his life for Benjamin in chapter 44. Admitting we are wrong is where change begins.


Q3. Multiple choice: Judah promised Tamar his youngest son Shelah as a husband but never kept his word (38:11, 14). What did that do to her? Medium
A) It left her with no family and no future   B) It made her happy   C) Nothing   D) It made her rich
Answer: A. It left her with no family and no future. A broken promise can do real harm to someone with no one else to protect them.


Q4. Multiple choice: What were the names of Tamar’s twin sons? (38:29–30) Easy
A) Cain and Abel   B) Perez and Zerah   C) Ephraim and Manasseh   D) Jacob and Esau
Answer: B. Perez and Zerah. Perez and Zerah.


Q5. True or false: Judah was quick to condemn Tamar until he learned he was involved. (38:24–26) Medium
Answer: True. It’s easy to judge others while hiding our own sin.


Q6. Multiple choice: Perez appears in which family line? (Ruth 4:18–22; Matthew 1:3) Hard
A) Esau’s   B) Laban’s   C) The line of King David and Jesus   D) Pharaoh’s
Answer: C. The line of King David and Jesus. God works through messy families. Jesus’ family tree is full of grace.


Q7. True or false: Judah left his brothers and went to live somewhere else for a while. (38:1) Easy
Answer: True. This story takes a break from Joseph to follow Judah.


Q8. Multiple choice: Why is Judah’s story placed right in the middle of Joseph’s story? Medium
A) Because Judah was the oldest   B) By mistake   C) To set the two side by side: Judah gives in to temptation, while Joseph resists it in chapter 39   D) It has no purpose
Answer: C. To set the two side by side: Judah gives in to temptation, while Joseph resists it in chapter 39. Hebrew stories often put two characters next to each other so we compare them.


Genesis 39
Q1. True or false: Joseph was rewarded right away for doing the right thing. Medium
Answer: False. He was thrown in prison. Faithfulness doesn’t always pay off right away, but God still sees it.


Q2. Multiple choice: Joseph said, “How then could I do such a wicked thing and sin against God?” (39:9). Who did Joseph see the sin as being against? Medium
A) Only Potiphar   B) God, first of all   C) Himself   D) No one
Answer: B. God, first of all. Every sin hurts people, but it is first a sin against God.


Q3. Multiple choice: Who bought Joseph and put him in charge of his house? (39:1–4) Easy
A) Laban   B) The cupbearer   C) Potiphar   D) Pharaoh
Answer: C. Potiphar. Potiphar saw that God was blessing everything Joseph did.


Q4. Multiple choice: Compare Joseph with Eve in Genesis 3, who saw, took and ate. What did Joseph do differently? Hard
A) Nothing   B) He refused to linger and ran, breaking the pattern before it started   C) He blamed someone else   D) He asked the serpent for advice
Answer: B. He refused to linger and ran, breaking the pattern before it started. Temptation is easiest to beat early, before we stay and look.


Q5. Multiple choice: Potiphar’s wife kept pressuring Joseph day after day (39:10). What did he do? Medium
A) Quit his job   B) Told Pharaoh   C) Gave in eventually   D) Kept refusing and stayed away from her
Answer: D. Kept refusing and stayed away from her. Joseph didn’t just say no once. He kept away from the temptation.


Q6. Fill in the blank: “The Lord was ______ Joseph so that he prospered.” (39:2) Easy
Answer: with. Even as a slave, Joseph was not alone.


Q7. Multiple choice: “The Lord was with Joseph” is said in Potiphar’s house and again in prison (39:21). What is the lesson? Hard
A) Prison is a good place   B) God only helps successful people   C) Joseph was lucky   D) God’s presence doesn’t depend on our circumstances
Answer: D. God’s presence doesn’t depend on our circumstances. God was just as near to Joseph in prison as in a palace.


Q8. True or false: When Potiphar’s wife tried to get Joseph to sin with her, he ran away. (39:12) Easy
Answer: True. Sometimes the wisest thing to do with temptation is run.


Genesis 40
Q1. Multiple choice: Which two officials did Joseph meet in prison? (40:1–3) Easy
A) Pharaoh’s cupbearer and baker   B) Two brothers   C) Two soldiers   D) Potiphar and his wife
Answer: A. Pharaoh’s cupbearer and baker. Both had angered Pharaoh.


Q2. True or false: The cupbearer remembered Joseph as soon as he got out of prison. Medium
Answer: False. “The chief cupbearer did not remember Joseph; he forgot him” (40:23).


Q3. Fill in the blank: Joseph said, “Do not interpretations belong to ______?” (40:8) Easy
Answer: God. Joseph knew his gift came from God.


Q4. Multiple choice: Joseph told the baker his dream meant he would die. What does that show? Medium
A) He told the truth even when it was hard to hear   B) He made it up   C) He didn’t like the baker   D) Joseph was cruel
Answer: A. He told the truth even when it was hard to hear. Faithfulness means telling the truth, not just good news.


Q5. Multiple choice: What does Joseph being forgotten by the cupbearer teach about times when people let us down? Hard
A) We should get revenge   B) It means God is angry   C) We should stop trusting anyone   D) People may forget us, but God does not
Answer: D. People may forget us, but God does not. The story keeps going even when someone fails us.


Q6. Multiple choice: Joseph asked the cupbearer to remember him to Pharaoh (40:14). Was asking for help a lack of faith? Hard
A) No. Trusting God and asking people for help can go together   B) Yes, he was being proud   C) Yes, he should only have prayed   D) It doesn’t matter
Answer: A. No. Trusting God and asking people for help can go together. Joseph trusted God and still did what he could.


Q7. True or false: Both of Joseph’s dream interpretations came true. (40:20–22) Easy
Answer: True. God gave Joseph the meaning, and it happened just as he said.


Q8. Multiple choice: Joseph noticed the two men looked sad and asked them why (40:6–7). What does this show about Joseph? Medium
A) He was nosy   B) Even while suffering unfairly, he cared about other people   C) He was bored   D) He wanted something from them
Answer: B. Even while suffering unfairly, he cared about other people. Joseph’s own pain didn’t make him stop seeing others.


Genesis 41
Q1. Multiple choice: Joseph was 17 when he was sold (37:2) and 30 when he stood before Pharaoh (41:46). What do those 13 years teach? Hard
A) Joseph did something wrong   B) Nothing   C) God forgot Joseph   D) God’s timing is often slower than ours, and He is at work in the waiting
Answer: D. God’s timing is often slower than ours, and He is at work in the waiting. God was shaping Joseph for this moment the whole time.


Q2. Fill in the blank: “I cannot do it,” Joseph replied, “but ______ will give Pharaoh the answer he desires.” (41:16) Easy
Answer: God. Joseph pointed away from himself.


Q3. True or false: Pharaoh put Joseph in charge of all Egypt, second only to himself. (41:41) Easy
Answer: True. In one day Joseph went from prisoner to ruler.


Q4. Multiple choice: Joseph told Pharaoh, “I cannot do it” (41:16). What does this teach? Medium
A) Joseph lacked confidence   B) Dreams can’t be explained   C) Joseph didn’t want the job   D) Humility: giving God the credit for what He does through us
Answer: D. Humility: giving God the credit for what He does through us. Joseph had real gifts, but he knew where they came from.


Q5. True or false: Joseph told Pharaoh to enjoy and spend everything during the good years. Medium
Answer: False. He told Pharaoh to store up a fifth of the harvest for the famine ahead (41:34–36).


Q6. Multiple choice: What did Pharaoh dream about? (41:1–7) Easy
A) Seven fat cows and seven thin cows, and seven good and seven thin heads of grain   B) A great flood   C) A ladder to heaven   D) Stars and the moon
Answer: A. Seven fat cows and seven thin cows, and seven good and seven thin heads of grain. God was warning Egypt about what was coming.


Q7. Multiple choice: The cupbearer forgot Joseph for two full years (41:1). Looking back, why might the delay have been good? Hard
A) The cupbearer was busy   B) It wasn’t good   C) Joseph was remembered exactly when Pharaoh needed him. If he’d been freed earlier, he might have just gone home   D) Joseph needed more sleep
Answer: C. Joseph was remembered exactly when Pharaoh needed him. If he’d been freed earlier, he might have just gone home. God’s delays are not the same as God saying no.


Q8. Multiple choice: Joseph named his second son Ephraim, saying, “God has made me fruitful in the land of my suffering” (41:52). What’s the lesson? Medium
A) Suffering is good   B) Egypt was a good place   C) God can bring growth and blessing in the hardest places   D) Names don’t matter
Answer: C. God can bring growth and blessing in the hardest places. Joseph didn’t pretend Egypt wasn’t hard, but he saw God at work there.


Genesis 42
Q1. Multiple choice: Joseph turned away and wept when he heard his brothers’ regret (42:24), but he kept testing them. Why not tell them who he was right away? Hard
A) He needed to know their hearts had changed. Forgiving and rebuilding trust are not the same thing   B) He didn’t care   C) He forgot   D) Pharaoh wouldn’t allow it
Answer: A. He needed to know their hearts had changed. Forgiving and rebuilding trust are not the same thing. Joseph had forgiven them, but trust needed to be rebuilt.


Q2. Multiple choice: When the brothers bowed to Joseph, what came true? Medium
A) Pharaoh’s dream   B) Nothing   C) Joseph’s dreams from Genesis 37   D) Jacob’s blessing
Answer: C. Joseph’s dreams from Genesis 37. God’s word came true, even after more than twenty years.


Q3. Multiple choice: The brothers said, “Surely we are being punished because of our brother” (42:21). What does this show? Medium
A) They had forgotten Joseph   B) They had carried guilt for years   C) They didn’t believe in God   D) They blamed Joseph
Answer: B. They had carried guilt for years. Sin we hide is not forgotten by our conscience.


Q4. Multiple choice: Jacob cried, “Everything is against me!” (42:36). What didn’t he know? Hard
A) That everything was actually leading toward his family’s rescue   B) That the famine was over   C) That Simeon was dead   D) That Benjamin was sick
Answer: A. That everything was actually leading toward his family’s rescue. We can’t see the whole picture. God was working while Jacob saw only loss.


Q5. Fill in the blank: Joseph’s brothers ______ down to him with their faces to the ground. (42:6) Easy
Answer: bowed. Exactly what Joseph had dreamed years before.


Q6. True or false: Joseph kept Simeon in prison while the others went home. (42:24) Medium
Answer: True. Simeon stayed until they returned with Benjamin.


Q7. Multiple choice: Why did Joseph’s brothers go to Egypt? (42:1–3) Easy
A) To fight Egypt   B) To visit Pharaoh   C) To find Joseph   D) To buy grain during the famine
Answer: D. To buy grain during the famine. The famine sent them to the very brother they had sold.


Q8. True or false: The brothers recognized Joseph right away. (42:8) Easy
Answer: False. Joseph recognized them, but they didn’t recognize him.


Genesis 43
Q1. True or false: Jacob was eager to send Benjamin to Egypt. Easy
Answer: False. He had already lost Joseph and was afraid of losing Benjamin too.


Q2. Multiple choice: Why might Joseph have given Benjamin five times more than the others? Hard
A) It was an accident   B) To see if they would be jealous of the favored son, as they had been of Joseph   C) Egyptian custom   D) Benjamin was hungrier
Answer: B. To see if they would be jealous of the favored son, as they had been of Joseph. Jealousy had once led them to sell Joseph. Joseph was checking whether that had changed.


Q3. True or false: The brothers were seated in order of age at the feast, and they were amazed. (43:33) Medium
Answer: True. Someone in Egypt knew far more about them than they realized.


Q4. Multiple choice: Joseph hurried out of the room to weep when he saw Benjamin (43:30). What does this show? Medium
A) He was sick   B) He still loved his family deeply   C) He was afraid   D) He was angry
Answer: B. He still loved his family deeply. Behind the tests, Joseph’s heart was already toward his brothers.


Q5. Fill in the blank: Judah told Jacob, “I myself will ______ his safety.” (43:9) Easy
Answer: guarantee. Judah took personal responsibility for his brother.


Q6. Multiple choice: Reuben offered to let Jacob kill his own two sons if Benjamin didn’t return (42:37). Judah offered himself instead (43:9). Why was Judah’s offer better? Hard
A) Reuben was joking   B) It wasn’t better   C) Judah was richer   D) He put himself on the line instead of others
Answer: D. He put himself on the line instead of others. Real leadership costs the leader, not other people.


Q7. Multiple choice: Jacob finally said, “If I am bereaved, I am bereaved” (43:14). What was he doing? Medium
A) Blaming his sons   B) Refusing to send Benjamin   C) Giving up on God   D) Letting go of what he couldn’t control and trusting God with it
Answer: D. Letting go of what he couldn’t control and trusting God with it. Sometimes faith means opening your hands.


Q8. Multiple choice: How much bigger was Benjamin’s portion at Joseph’s feast? (43:34) Easy
A) Three times as big   B) The same   C) Five times as big   D) Twice as big
Answer: C. Five times as big. Five times as much as anyone else.


Genesis 44
Q1. True or false: The brothers left Benjamin behind and went home without him. Easy
Answer: False. They tore their clothes and all went back to the city with him (44:13).


Q2. Multiple choice: What does Judah’s offer show? Hard
A) He didn’t care about his father   B) He wanted to live in Egypt   C) Real repentance: a changed heart willing to sacrifice for the brother they once would have discarded   D) He was trying to impress Joseph
Answer: C. Real repentance: a changed heart willing to sacrifice for the brother they once would have discarded. Repentance shows up in changed actions.


Q3. Multiple choice: Judah said, “God has uncovered your servants’ guilt” (44:16). What guilt was he really thinking of? Medium
A) Stealing the cup   B) Eating too much   C) Lying to Pharaoh   D) What they had done to Joseph years before
Answer: D. What they had done to Joseph years before. They hadn’t taken the cup, but their consciences still carried what they did to Joseph.


Q4. Fill in the blank: Judah offered to stay as a ______ in Benjamin’s place. (44:33) Easy
Answer: slave. He was willing to give up his own freedom for his brother.


Q5. True or false: Judah was the brother who first suggested selling Joseph. (37:26–27) Medium
Answer: True. The same brother who sold Joseph now offers himself to save Benjamin.


Q6. Multiple choice: Judah offering himself in his brother’s place points forward to whom? Hard
A) Jesus, a descendant of Judah, who took our place   B) Joseph   C) Moses   D) Pharaoh
Answer: A. Jesus, a descendant of Judah, who took our place. Judah’s willingness to be a substitute is a small picture of what Jesus would do.


Q7. Multiple choice: Why did Joseph test his brothers this way? Medium
A) Pharaoh told him to   B) He wanted the cup back   C) To see if they would abandon Benjamin the way they had abandoned him   D) He was still angry
Answer: C. To see if they would abandon Benjamin the way they had abandoned him. Joseph needed to know whether their hearts had changed.


Q8. Multiple choice: What was hidden in Benjamin’s sack? (44:12) Easy
A) A gold ring   B) A letter   C) Pharaoh’s crown   D) Joseph’s silver cup
Answer: D. Joseph’s silver cup. Joseph set up a test for his brothers.


Genesis 45
Q1. True or false: Joseph’s brothers were terrified when he told them who he was. (45:3) Easy
Answer: True. They were face to face with the brother they had wronged.


Q2. Multiple choice: Joseph forgave his brothers before they asked. What does that show about forgiveness? Hard
A) It isn’t really needed   B) It must wait for an apology   C) It can be offered first, the way God offers it to us   D) It means pretending nothing happened
Answer: C. It can be offered first, the way God offers it to us. “While we were still sinners, Christ died for us” (Romans 5:8). Joseph’s forgiveness looks like God’s.


Q3. Fill in the blank: Joseph said to his brothers, “I am ______! Is my father still living?” (45:3) Easy
Answer: Joseph. After years of hiding who he was, Joseph finally told them.


Q4. Multiple choice: Who did Joseph send for to come and live in Egypt? (45:9–10) Easy
A) His father Jacob and the whole family   B) Only Benjamin   C) Pharaoh’s army   D) The Egyptians
Answer: A. His father Jacob and the whole family. Joseph wanted to provide for his whole family during the famine.


Q5. True or false: When Jacob heard Joseph was alive, he believed it right away. Medium
Answer: False. He was stunned and didn’t believe until he saw the carts Joseph sent (45:26–27).


Q6. Multiple choice: Joseph said, “It was to save lives that God sent me ahead of you” (45:5). What changed how Joseph saw his suffering? Medium
A) He got rich   B) His brothers apologized first   C) He forgot about it   D) He saw God’s purpose behind it
Answer: D. He saw God’s purpose behind it. Seeing God’s hand didn’t erase the pain, but it gave it meaning.


Q7. Multiple choice: As his brothers left, Joseph said, “Don’t quarrel on the way!” (45:24). Why? Medium
A) He knew they might start blaming each other   B) He wanted them to hurry   C) The road was dangerous   D) Egyptians didn’t like noise
Answer: A. He knew they might start blaming each other. Forgiveness means not reopening the blame.


Q8. Multiple choice: Joseph said, “It was not you who sent me here, but God” (45:8). Does this mean the brothers did nothing wrong? Hard
A) Joseph was lying   B) God made them do it   C) No. They were responsible, and God was also working through what they did   D) Yes, they were innocent
Answer: C. No. They were responsible, and God was also working through what they did. The Bible holds both together: people are responsible for sin, and God is still in control.


Genesis 46
Q1. Multiple choice: The family enters Egypt as 70 people. By Exodus 1 they fill the land. What promise is God keeping? Hard
A) To make Abraham’s descendants a great nation   B) To give them Egypt   C) To end the famine   D) To make Pharaoh rich
Answer: A. To make Abraham’s descendants a great nation. God promised Abraham descendants like the stars. He keeps that promise even far from home.


Q2. Multiple choice: Before leaving for Egypt, Jacob stopped at Beersheba and offered sacrifices (46:1). What does that show? Medium
A) He was lost   B) It was a holiday   C) Pharaoh told him to   D) He sought God before making a big move
Answer: D. He sought God before making a big move. Jacob didn’t go without first going to God.


Q3. Multiple choice: God promised Jacob, “I will go down to Egypt with you” (46:4). What does this teach? Medium
A) God’s presence goes with His people wherever they go   B) Egypt’s gods would protect them   C) Jacob would go alone   D) God only lives in Canaan
Answer: A. God’s presence goes with His people wherever they go. God isn’t tied to one place. He is with His people.


Q4. Multiple choice: Years earlier, God told Abraham his descendants would be strangers in a land that wasn’t theirs (15:13). How does Genesis 46 fit? Hard
A) God changed His mind   B) Abraham was wrong   C) It’s the beginning of what God foretold, so even the hard seasons are part of His plan   D) It doesn’t fit
Answer: C. It’s the beginning of what God foretold, so even the hard seasons are part of His plan. God knew the road ahead, including the hard parts, and said He would bring them out.


Q5. True or false: When Joseph and Jacob finally met again, Joseph wept for a long time. (46:29) Easy
Answer: True. After more than twenty years, a lost son was found.


Q6. Multiple choice: How many people from Jacob’s family went to Egypt in all? (46:27) Easy
A) 1,000   B) 70   C) 40   D) 12
Answer: B. 70. Seventy people. In Exodus they will become a nation.


Q7. Fill in the blank: God told Jacob, “Do not be ______ to go down to Egypt.” (46:3) Easy
Answer: afraid. God had a plan for them there.


Q8. True or false: The Egyptians looked down on shepherds, so Jacob’s family was kept apart in Goshen. (46:34) Medium
Answer: True. Living apart helped Israel stay a separate people instead of blending into Egypt.


Genesis 47
Q1. Multiple choice: Which area of Egypt did Pharaoh give Jacob’s family to live in? (47:6) Easy
A) Canaan   B) Ur   C) Babylon   D) Goshen
Answer: D. Goshen. Goshen was good land, and the family grew there.


Q2. Multiple choice: While Egyptians were losing their land, what was happening to Israel’s family in Goshen? (47:27) Hard
A) They were enslaved   B) They moved back to Canaan   C) They were starving   D) They gained property and grew greatly in number
Answer: D. They gained property and grew greatly in number. God was keeping His promise to make them fruitful, even in a famine.


Q3. Multiple choice: Pharaoh ended up owning almost all the land and people of Egypt. Looking ahead to Exodus, why does this matter? Hard
A) It doesn’t matter   B) Pharaoh gave it all to Joseph   C) A later Pharaoh will use that power to enslave Israel   D) Egypt became the promised land
Answer: C. A later Pharaoh will use that power to enslave Israel. Power gathered in good times can be abused later by people who don’t fear God.


Q4. Multiple choice: Why is it surprising that Jacob blessed Pharaoh? Medium
A) Pharaoh asked for a curse   B) A poor old shepherd blessed the king of Egypt, which shows God’s promise to bless the nations through Abraham’s family   C) Blessings were against the law   D) Jacob didn’t like Pharaoh
Answer: B. A poor old shepherd blessed the king of Egypt, which shows God’s promise to bless the nations through Abraham’s family. Israel was blessed to be a blessing.


Q5. True or false: During the famine, the Egyptians sold their land and themselves to Pharaoh in exchange for food. (47:20–21) Medium
Answer: True. Joseph’s plan saved lives, but Pharaoh ended up owning nearly everything.


Q6. Fill in the blank: Jacob told Pharaoh he was ______ years old. (47:9) Easy
Answer: 130. He had lived a long and hard life, but God carried him through it.


Q7. Multiple choice: Jacob told Pharaoh his years had been “few and difficult” (47:9). What does his honesty show? Medium
A) He had lost his faith   B) He wanted pity   C) Faith can be honest about pain and hardship   D) He was lying
Answer: C. Faith can be honest about pain and hardship. Walking with God doesn’t mean pretending life is easy.


Q8. True or false: Jacob blessed Pharaoh. (47:7, 10) Easy
Answer: True. God promised Abraham his family would bless the nations, even the most powerful king on earth.


Genesis 48
Q1. Multiple choice: Jacob was nearly blind when he blessed Joseph’s sons, just as Isaac was when Jacob tricked him. What is different this time? Hard
A) This time the younger son is blessed openly and on purpose, with no deception   B) The older son was blessed   C) Joseph tricked Jacob   D) Nothing is different
Answer: A. This time the younger son is blessed openly and on purpose, with no deception. God redeemed Jacob’s story. What began with a lie ends with honest faith.


Q2. Multiple choice: How did Joseph react when Jacob put his right hand on the younger son? (48:17) Medium
A) He was displeased and tried to move his father’s hand   B) He laughed   C) He didn’t notice   D) He left the room
Answer: A. He was displeased and tried to move his father’s hand. God’s choices don’t always follow what we expect.


Q3. Multiple choice: Jacob spoke of “the Angel who has delivered me from all harm” (48:16). What does this show about Jacob at the end of his life? Hard
A) The man who once relied on tricks had learned to depend on God   B) He didn’t believe in God   C) He was afraid of angels   D) He was still a schemer
Answer: A. The man who once relied on tricks had learned to depend on God. God changed Jacob over a lifetime.


Q4. True or false: Jacob gave the bigger blessing to the younger son, Ephraim. (48:19) Easy
Answer: True. He crossed his hands on purpose.


Q5. Fill in the blank: Jacob called God the One “who has been my ______ all my life to this day.” (48:15) Easy
Answer: shepherd. Looking back, Jacob saw that God had led and cared for him the whole way.


Q6. Multiple choice: This isn’t the first time Genesis puts the younger son ahead of the older. Which is another example? Medium
A) Adam over Eve   B) Pharaoh over Joseph   C) Noah over Ham   D) Jacob over Esau
Answer: D. Jacob over Esau. God chooses by grace, not by birth order or human ranking.


Q7. Multiple choice: What were the names of Joseph’s two sons? (48:1) Easy
A) Simeon and Levi   B) Ephraim and Manasseh   C) Cain and Abel   D) Esau and Jacob
Answer: B. Ephraim and Manasseh. Jacob blessed both of them as his own sons.


Q8. True or false: Jacob adopted Joseph’s two sons as his own, so each became a tribe of Israel. (48:5) Medium
Answer: True. Joseph received a double portion through his two sons.


Genesis 49
Q1. Multiple choice: How many sons did Jacob bless before he died? Easy
A) 40   B) 7   C) 12   D) 10
Answer: C. 12. Twelve sons, who became the twelve tribes of Israel.


Q2. Multiple choice: In Revelation 5:5, Jesus is called “the Lion of the tribe of Judah.” Which part of Genesis does that reach back to? Hard
A) Genesis 1   B) Jacob’s blessing of Judah in Genesis 49   C) The Tower of Babel   D) The flood
Answer: B. Jacob’s blessing of Judah in Genesis 49. Jacob called Judah a lion and promised a lasting king. That promise grows clearer until it is fulfilled in Jesus.


Q3. Multiple choice: Judah, who once suggested selling Joseph, received the promise of kings. What does that show? Hard
A) It was random   B) God uses flawed people who truly change, since Judah later offered his life for Benjamin   C) Judah tricked his father   D) God rewards bad behavior
Answer: B. God uses flawed people who truly change, since Judah later offered his life for Benjamin. God works through people who repent. He doesn’t approve of their sin.


Q4. True or false: Jacob asked to be buried with Abraham and Isaac in the cave in Canaan. (49:29–30) Easy
Answer: True. To the end, Jacob trusted God’s promise of the land.


Q5. Multiple choice: Jacob said Simeon and Levi would be scattered in Israel because of their anger (49:5–7). What had they done? Medium
A) Taken violent revenge on the city of Shechem   B) Refused to go to Egypt   C) Stolen their father’s money   D) Lied to Pharaoh
Answer: A. Taken violent revenge on the city of Shechem. Violence has consequences that last for generations.


Q6. True or false: Everything Jacob said to his sons was a happy blessing. Medium
Answer: False. Some sons were warned or rebuked. Jacob was honest about each one.


Q7. Multiple choice: Reuben was the oldest, but Jacob said, “Turbulent as the waters, you will no longer excel” (49:4). What can we learn? Medium
A) A privileged place can be lost through lack of self-control   B) Water is dangerous   C) Being first-born guarantees blessing   D) Reuben was too young
Answer: A. A privileged place can be lost through lack of self-control. Character matters more than position.


Q8. Fill in the blank: “The scepter will not depart from ______.” (49:10) Easy
Answer: Judah. Israel’s kings, including David, came from Judah, and so did Jesus.


Genesis 50
Q1. Multiple choice: Joseph asked his brothers, “Am I in the place of God?” (50:19). What did he mean? Medium
A) He was angry at God   B) He wanted to be treated like God   C) He was confused   D) Judging and paying people back belongs to God, not to him
Answer: D. Judging and paying people back belongs to God, not to him. Joseph left justice to God, which freed him to forgive.


Q2. True or false: After Jacob died, Joseph’s brothers were afraid he would finally pay them back. (50:15) Easy
Answer: True. Guilt is hard to shake. They needed to hear again that Joseph’s forgiveness was real.


Q3. Multiple choice: What does Joseph’s whole story teach about how God works when people do evil? Hard
A) God ignores it   B) Evil always wins in the end   C) God causes the evil   D) God doesn’t approve of the evil, but He brings good out of it and saves many lives
Answer: D. God doesn’t approve of the evil, but He brings good out of it and saves many lives. The brothers were guilty, yet God used it all to save their family and many others.


Q4. True or false: Joseph made his brothers his slaves to make up for what they had done. Medium
Answer: False. He promised to provide for them and their children and spoke kindly to them (50:21).


Q5. Multiple choice: Before he died, Joseph made Israel promise to carry his bones out of Egypt (50:25). Why? Medium
A) He hated Egypt   B) He believed God would bring them back to the promised land   C) It was an Egyptian custom   D) Pharaoh asked him to
Answer: B. He believed God would bring them back to the promised land. Joseph’s last words were about God keeping His promise. Faith looks ahead to what God has said He will do.


Q6. Multiple choice: Where was Jacob buried? (50:13) Easy
A) At Bethel   B) In the cave in Canaan that Abraham bought   C) In Haran   D) In Egypt
Answer: B. In the cave in Canaan that Abraham bought. Jacob wanted to rest in the promised land. He trusted God would give it to his family.


Q7. Multiple choice: Genesis begins with life in a garden and ends with Joseph in a coffin in Egypt. What does that ending point to? Hard
A) Nothing important   B) The story isn’t finished. God’s people are waiting for Him to keep His promises   C) Egypt is the promised land   D) God failed
Answer: B. The story isn’t finished. God’s people are waiting for Him to keep His promises. Genesis ends with hope still waiting. Exodus picks up the story of God keeping His word.


Q8. Fill in the blank: Joseph told his brothers, “You intended to harm me, but God intended it for ______.” (50:20) Easy
Answer: good. God can bring good out of what people meant for evil.


Exodus
Exodus 1
Q1. Multiple choice: Why did Pharaoh's oppression fail to slow Israel's growth? (1:12) Medium
A) The Hebrews escaped into the hills   B) God stopped Pharaoh's army   C) The Egyptians grew tired of the plan   D) The more they were oppressed, the more they multiplied
Answer: D. The more they were oppressed, the more they multiplied. Human plans cannot stop what God has promised.


Q2. Multiple choice: Why did the midwives refuse to kill the Hebrew baby boys? (1:17) Easy
A) They were paid by the Hebrews   B) They were afraid of the Egyptian army   C) They feared God more than the king   D) They wanted to leave Egypt
Answer: C. They feared God more than the king. Fearing God can mean refusing a command that is clearly wrong.


Q3. Multiple choice: The king feared losing control to a growing people. What does his fear teach? (1:9-10) Hard
A) Fear of losing control turns people against God's promises   B) Fear makes people more peaceful   C) Fear of others is always wise   D) Fear helps people obey God
Answer: A. Fear of losing control turns people against God's promises. Fear of losing control often becomes hostility toward God's people and His promises.


Q4. Fill in the blank: A new king arose over Egypt who did not know ______. (1:8) Easy
Answer: Joseph. When people forget what God has done, they fear God's people instead of trusting Him.


Q5. True or false: Moses is named in the first chapter of Exodus. (1:22; 2:10) Medium
Answer: False. The deliverer's story starts after the chapter shows the oppression in full.


Q6. True or false: God had stopped remembering His promise to Abraham while Israel suffered. (1:7; 2:24) Hard
Answer: False. Silence is not absence: God remembers His covenant even when His people's suffering seems to go unanswered.


Q7. Fill in the blank: Pharaoh commanded that every newborn ______ boy be thrown into the Nile. (1:22) Medium
Answer: Hebrew. Evil often hides behind the language of policy and order, so God's people must call it what it is.


Q8. True or false: God blessed the midwives because they feared Him and let the boys live. (1:20-21) Easy
Answer: True. God sees faithful people who stand against evil, even when no one important notices.


Exodus 2
Q1. True or false: Moses' exile in Midian was the end of God's plan for Israel. (2:23-25) Hard
Answer: False. Exile was a stop in God's plan, not the end of it, because God heard the groaning from Egypt.


Q2. True or false: Moses' sister Miriam watched over him and arranged for his own mother to nurse him. (2:4-8) Easy
Answer: True. God's care often comes through ordinary, faithful acts like a sister's watchfulness.


Q3. Multiple choice: What does Moses' time in exile before his calling teach about God's timing? (2:15-22) Hard
A) God's calling depends on a person's age   B) Exile is always punishment   C) God humbles and shapes leaders in hidden seasons before sending them   D) God forgot Moses for a while
Answer: C. God humbles and shapes leaders in hidden seasons before sending them. God often prepares people in quiet seasons before He uses them.


Q4. True or false: Jethro, Moses' father-in-law in Midian, was an Israelite from the tribe of Levi. (2:16-21) Medium
Answer: False. God works through people outside Israel's family too, as Jethro's welcome of Moses shows.


Q5. Fill in the blank: Moses' mother placed him in a basket of ______ on the Nile. (2:3) Easy
Answer: papyrus. God can use fragile places to carry out His rescue.


Q6. Multiple choice: Why did Moses flee to Midian? (2:11-15) Medium
A) He was banished from the land   B) He killed an Egyptian who was beating a Hebrew   C) He wanted to become a shepherd   D) God commanded him to go
Answer: B. He killed an Egyptian who was beating a Hebrew. Moses tried to rescue Israel in his own strength, and it sent him into exile.


Q7. Fill in the blank: God heard their groaning and remembered His ______ with Abraham, Isaac and Jacob. (2:24) Medium
Answer: covenant. God's rescue begins with His faithfulness to His promises, not with Israel's worthiness.


Q8. Multiple choice: Who found baby Moses in the basket? (2:5-6) Easy
A) Miriam   B) Pharaoh   C) Aaron   D) Pharaoh's daughter
Answer: D. Pharaoh's daughter. God works through people outside His covenant to protect His plan.


Exodus 3
Q1. True or false: The name "I AM" tells Israel that God's promises depend on Israel's behavior. (3:14-15) Hard
Answer: False. God is the source of His own faithfulness; He does not depend on Israel to fulfill His promises.


Q2. True or false: God told Moses that the place where he stood was holy ground. (3:5) Easy
Answer: True. Holiness comes from God's presence, not from the place by itself.


Q3. Multiple choice: Why did God say He was sending Moses? (3:7-10) Medium
A) Because Moses wanted to lead   B) Because God heard Israel's cries and remembered His covenant   C) Because Moses was the strongest man in Egypt   D) Because Pharaoh asked for him
Answer: B. Because God heard Israel's cries and remembered His covenant. God's call comes from His compassion and promise, not from the qualifications of the one called.


Q4. True or false: Moses immediately said yes and went to Pharaoh with no objections. (3:11; 4:1) Medium
Answer: False. God answers honest objections rather than shaming the person He calls.


Q5. Fill in the blank: God's promise to Moses was, "I will be ______ you." (3:12) Medium
Answer: with. The promise of God's presence is the foundation of every call He gives.


Q6. Multiple choice: When Moses asked "Who am I?", what was God's answer? (3:11-12) Hard
A) "Go, and find your own strength."   B) "Send someone else."   C) "I will be with you."   D) "Wait for a sign from Pharaoh."
Answer: C. "I will be with you.". Our answer to inadequacy is not our confidence but God's presence with us.


Q7. Multiple choice: Where did God first appear to Moses? (3:2) Easy
A) On the bank of the Nile   B) On Mount Sinai in a storm   C) In a burning bush that was not consumed   D) In a dream in Midian
Answer: C. In a burning bush that was not consumed. God meets people in unexpected, ordinary places, and holiness begins where He is present.


Q8. Fill in the blank: God said, "I AM WHO I AM" when Moses asked for His ______. (3:13-14) Easy
Answer: name. God is self-existent and faithful, and His name is a promise that He is present.


Exodus 4
Q1. Multiple choice: Why did God tell Moses that Israel was His firstborn son? (4:22-23) Hard
A) To show that Israel was the most powerful nation   B) To tell Pharaoh that Israel would rule Egypt   C) To show that Israel belongs to God and is His special people   D) To explain why Moses was born
Answer: C. To show that Israel belongs to God and is His special people. Israel's identity comes from belonging to God, not from its strength or its rulers.


Q2. Fill in the blank: When Moses said he was slow of speech, God appointed ______ as his spokesman. (4:14-16) Easy
Answer: Aaron. God's call often comes with help and partnership, not with a demand to do everything alone.


Q3. Multiple choice: What sign did God give Moses to show His power to Israel? (4:2-4) Easy
A) A rain of bread   B) A river of wine   C) A cloud over the camp   D) A staff that became a snake
Answer: D. A staff that became a snake. God gives visible signs so that His people can trust His word.


Q4. Multiple choice: Why did Moses object that he was not a good speaker? (4:10-12) Hard
A) He was afraid of Aaron   B) He had no staff   C) He doubted that God could speak through a weak person   D) He wanted to stay in Midian
Answer: C. He doubted that God could speak through a weak person. God uses the people He calls, and He is the one who gives them the words and the strength.


Q5. True or false: Moses went back to Egypt alone with no help from his brother. (4:27-28) Medium
Answer: False. God brings Aaron to meet Moses, so the call is shared rather than carried alone.


Q6. True or false: Moses' wife Zipporah circumcised their son, which turned God's anger away from Moses. (4:24-26) Easy
Answer: True. God takes His covenant sign seriously, and obedience in the home matters to Him.


Q7. Fill in the blank: God called Israel His ______ son, and told Pharaoh to let him go. (4:22) Medium
Answer: firstborn. Israel belongs to God, and God's claim on His people is more important than Pharaoh's demands.


Q8. Multiple choice: What did God say He would do to Pharaoh's heart? (4:21) Medium
A) He would soften it   B) He would take Pharaoh's throne   C) He would harden it   D) He would make Pharaoh leave on his own
Answer: C. He would harden it. God's hardening of Pharaoh shows that He is in control of the outcome, even when people resist Him.


Exodus 5
Q1. Multiple choice: Why did the Israelites first blame Moses and Aaron for their suffering? (5:20-21) Medium
A) They wanted to return to Pharaoh's favor   B) The harsher orders made their work worse, and they blamed Moses and Aaron   C) They did not want to leave Egypt   D) They were angry about the new taxes
Answer: B. The harsher orders made their work worse, and they blamed Moses and Aaron. When hope fails, people often turn on the messengers of God instead of trusting Him.


Q2. Multiple choice: What happened when Moses and Aaron first asked Pharaoh to let Israel go? (5:1-9) Easy
A) Pharaoh agreed right away   B) Pharaoh sent Israel away with gifts   C) Pharaoh refused and made the work harder   D) Pharaoh asked God for help
Answer: C. Pharaoh refused and made the work harder. Obeying God does not guarantee an easy path, and worse conditions can follow faithful obedience.


Q3. Multiple choice: What does Moses' complaint in chapter 5 show about faithful prayer? (5:22-23) Hard
A) God only hears prayers of praise   B) God wants His people to bring honest pain to Him   C) It is wrong to bring complaints to God   D) God ignores complaints
Answer: B. God wants His people to bring honest pain to Him. Faithful people bring their complaints to God rather than hiding them or walking away from Him.


Q4. True or false: Moses accepted the hard situation without speaking to God about it. (5:22-23) Medium
Answer: False. Moses brings his complaint to the LORD, which is the right response to discouragement.


Q5. Multiple choice: Israel's suffering grew after the first request to Pharaoh. What does God's delay teach? (5:22-6:1) Hard
A) God forgot His people   B) God works His purposes in ways people cannot see at first   C) Pharaoh's power was greater than God's promise   D) God was weaker than Pharaoh
Answer: B. God works His purposes in ways people cannot see at first. God's delays are not His failures, and His answer often comes in a way people do not expect.


Q6. Fill in the blank: Moses asked God, "Why did you ______ this people?" (5:22) Medium
Answer: send me. Honest questions to God are part of faithful prayer, and God's people can bring their pain to Him directly.


Q7. Fill in the blank: Pharaoh answered, "Who is the ______, that I should obey his voice?" (5:2) Easy
Answer: LORD. Pharaoh's question sets up the contest of the whole book: who is God, and who is in control?


Q8. True or false: The Israelite foremen were beaten because they had failed to deliver enough bricks. (5:14) Easy
Answer: True. Leaders who stand between the oppressed and the oppressor often suffer for their people.


Exodus 6
Q1. Fill in the blank: God said, "I am the ______," and repeated it to tell Israel who was speaking. (6:2) Easy
Answer: LORD. God's self-revelation is the foundation for trusting every promise He makes.


Q2. Multiple choice: Why did Israel not listen to Moses' message? (6:9) Medium
A) Their spirit was broken and they were discouraged by harsh labor   B) They wanted to stay in Egypt   C) They did not trust Aaron   D) Their crops had failed
Answer: A. Their spirit was broken and they were discouraged by harsh labor. Hardship can make people deaf to hope, so God's people need patient encouragement.


Q3. Multiple choice: What did God promise Israel in Exodus 6:7? (6:7) Easy
A) They would be given a new king   B) They would become a nation of traders   C) He would take them as His people and be their God   D) They would rule over Egypt
Answer: C. He would take them as His people and be their God. God's covenant rests on His own promise to be the God of His people.


Q4. True or false: Israel listened eagerly to Moses' message of hope because they were encouraged. (6:9) Easy
Answer: False. Discouragement can keep people from hearing good news, and God's promise must reach them through His word.


Q5. Multiple choice: What does God's command to repeat "I am the LORD" teach about faith? (6:2-8) Hard
A) God wants repetition for its own sake   B) Faith rests on who God is, not on how circumstances feel   C) God is hard to know   D) The people needed to memorize a phrase
Answer: B. Faith rests on who God is, not on how circumstances feel. Faith is anchored in God's character, which holds steady when circumstances change.


Q6. Multiple choice: What does God's naming of Himself in chapter 6 teach about His covenant? (6:2-8) Hard
A) The covenant is only for Moses   B) God binds Himself by His own name and promise   C) The covenant was broken and needed to be rebuilt   D) The covenant depends on Israel's strength
Answer: B. God binds Himself by His own name and promise. God's covenant is secure because He is faithful and keeps the promises He made in His own name.


Q7. True or false: The family record traces both Moses and Aaron through the tribe of Levi. (6:16-27) Medium
Answer: True. It names their Levitical family and identifies them as the men commissioned to speak to Pharaoh.


Q8. Fill in the blank: God told Moses to speak to Pharaoh, and said, "I will bring you out from under the ______ of the Egyptians." (6:6) Medium
Answer: burdens. God frees His people from bondage and makes them His own, not just His servants.


Exodus 7
Q1. Multiple choice: Why did God send plagues on Egypt that His people did not suffer from? (7:14-25) Hard
A) To show Pharaoh that he was weaker than Moses   B) To show that God distinguishes His people and that He is the true God   C) To show Israel that Egypt was evil and deserved destruction   D) To punish Israel for their unbelief
Answer: B. To show that God distinguishes His people and that He is the true God. God's judgments reveal who He is, and they show His people that He is the Lord who protects them.


Q2. Multiple choice: What did the first plague against Egypt show about the gods of Egypt? (7:14-25) Medium
A) They wanted Israel to leave   B) They sided with Moses   C) They were powerless against the LORD   D) They were stronger than the LORD
Answer: C. They were powerless against the LORD. God's judgments expose the emptiness of the idols that human power depends on.


Q3. Multiple choice: What follows the magicians' imitation of the blood sign? (7:22-24) Hard
A) The Nile immediately becomes clean   B) Pharaoh refuses to listen, and Egyptians dig for drinking water   C) Moses is crowned king   D) Pharaoh releases Israel
Answer: B. Pharaoh refuses to listen, and Egyptians dig for drinking water. The imitation does not resolve Egypt's water crisis or change Pharaoh's response. The passage does not explain why the magicians did not reverse the plague.


Q4. Fill in the blank: Pharaoh's ______ was hardened, and he would not listen to Moses or Aaron. (7:14) Medium
Answer: heart. Hard hearts resist God's word, and God's judgments reveal what is in the human heart.


Q5. Fill in the blank: God said Moses would be like a ______ to Pharaoh, and Aaron would speak for him. (7:1) Easy
Answer: god. God gives His servants authority and makes them stand in His place, while the words belong to Him.


Q6. Multiple choice: What happened when Aaron's staff became a snake before Pharaoh? (7:10-12) Easy
A) The staff disappeared   B) Pharaoh's magicians did the same   C) Pharaoh fled from the court   D) The snake died immediately
Answer: B. Pharaoh's magicians did the same. Human imitation can look impressive, but it cannot overturn what God is doing.


Q7. True or false: The first plague turned the Nile to blood, and Pharaoh's heart was softened by it. (7:20-22) Easy
Answer: False. Pharaoh's heart was hardened, which shows that God's judgment does not always lead to repentance.


Q8. True or false: The Egyptian magicians also turned water into blood. (7:22) Medium
Answer: True. The text reports a similar sign by their secret arts, after which Pharaoh still refused to listen.


Exodus 8
Q1. Fill in the blank: Pharaoh's magicians could not match the plague of ______, and they said, "This is the finger of God." (8:19) Easy
Answer: gnats. Those who oppose God eventually face the limits of their own power and wisdom.


Q2. Multiple choice: Pharaoh tried to make a deal with Moses. What does this teach about bargaining with God? (8:25-32) Hard
A) God accepts any compromise   B) A partial obedience to God is still disobedience   C) Negotiating with God is wise   D) God wants people to delay
Answer: B. A partial obedience to God is still disobedience. Half-hearted obedience is not obedience, and God calls His people to follow Him fully.


Q3. Multiple choice: What plague did Egypt suffer after the frogs? (8:16-17) Easy
A) Darkness   B) Gnats   C) Locusts   D) Hail
Answer: B. Gnats. God's plagues came in an order that showed His power, and each one exposed the limits of Egypt's gods.


Q4. True or false: After the plague of flies, Pharaoh agreed to let Israel go fully, with no conditions. (8:25-32) Medium
Answer: False. Pharaoh's promise came with limits, which shows that half-hearted freedom is not what God asks for.


Q5. Multiple choice: What did God do to set Goshen apart during the plague of flies? (8:22-23) Medium
A) He sent a hail storm there   B) He sent frogs to protect Goshen   C) He moved Israel out of Goshen   D) He sent flies only on Egypt and spared Goshen
Answer: D. He sent flies only on Egypt and spared Goshen. God makes a clear distinction between His people and those who oppose Him, and He protects His own.


Q6. Multiple choice: Why did Moses tell Pharaoh that Israel must go into the wilderness to worship? (8:26-27) Hard
A) So Israel could leave Egypt forever without returning   B) So Israel could escape from the Egyptian army   C) So Israel could meet Pharaoh's demands   D) So Israel could sacrifice to the LORD as God commanded
Answer: D. So Israel could sacrifice to the LORD as God commanded. Worship of God is the purpose of Israel's freedom, and it cannot be replaced by compromise.


Q7. Fill in the blank: The plague of flies came on Pharaoh's house, and the land was ruined by the swarm of ______. (8:24) Medium
Answer: flies. God's judgments reach into the places people trust most, including the palace.


Q8. True or false: Pharaoh repented and let Israel go immediately after the frogs were removed. (8:8-15) Easy
Answer: False. Pharaoh hardened his heart again after the plague ended, which shows the danger of a half-hearted repentance.


Exodus 9
Q1. Fill in the blank: Pharaoh said, "I have sinned this time; the LORD is in the right, and I and my people are in the ______." (9:27) Easy
Answer: wrong. A confession about consequences is not the same as a turned heart.


Q2. Multiple choice: What does Pharaoh's confession followed by renewed hardness teach? (9:27-35) Hard
A) Confession alone is true repentance   B) God does not hear confessions   C) Pharaoh was sincere but weak   D) Sorrow that only wants relief can vanish once the pain ends
Answer: D. Sorrow that only wants relief can vanish once the pain ends. Repentance means turning to God, not only wanting relief from pain.


Q3. True or false: Some Egyptians who feared the word of the LORD brought their servants and livestock inside. (9:19-21) Hard
Answer: True. Those who take God's word seriously prepare for it, and some Egyptians responded in fear and obedience.


Q4. True or false: Moses prayed, and the thunder and hail stopped. (9:33) Medium
Answer: True. God answers prayer, and His power over creation is complete.


Q5. Fill in the blank: The hail was the heaviest that had ever fallen on Egypt since it became a ______. (9:24) Medium
Answer: nation. God's judgments are public and measured, so people can see who He is.


Q6. True or false: Pharaoh changed his heart permanently after the hail and let Israel go. (9:34-35) Easy
Answer: False. Pharaoh's repentance was shallow; when the pressure eased, he sinned again.


Q7. Multiple choice: Which plague struck the livestock of Egypt but spared Israel's livestock? (9:4-6) Easy
A) Livestock disease   B) Darkness   C) Boils   D) Hail
Answer: A. Livestock disease. God marks the difference between His people and those who oppose Him, and He protects His own.


Q8. Multiple choice: Why did God say He had let Pharaoh live? (9:16) Medium
A) So Egypt would keep a king   B) So Pharaoh could build more cities   C) So God's power would be declared throughout all the earth   D) Because Moses asked for mercy
Answer: C. So God's power would be declared throughout all the earth. God's judgments have a larger purpose: to make His name known to everyone.


Exodus 10
Q1. Fill in the blank: Moses said to Pharaoh, "How long will you refuse to humble yourself before ______?" (10:3) Easy
Answer: me. God calls leaders to humble themselves before Him, and pride refuses that call.


Q2. True or false: The locusts covered the land and ate what the hail had left behind. (10:12-15) Medium
Answer: True. God's judgments follow one after another, and Pharaoh's hardness did not break until the final blow.


Q3. Multiple choice: What did Pharaoh offer after the locusts were announced? (10:8-11) Easy
A) Let only the men go to worship   B) Let Moses stay in Egypt   C) Let Israel go and keep their livestock   D) Let all Israel go
Answer: A. Let only the men go to worship. Partial obedience looks reasonable to proud people, but God requires His people as a whole.


Q4. Multiple choice: What was the plague of darkness? (10:21-23) Medium
A) Clouds that never rained   B) A night full of stars   C) Smoke from fires   D) Three days of thick darkness that could be felt, while Israel had light
Answer: D. Three days of thick darkness that could be felt, while Israel had light. God's light is with His people even when the world around them is dark.


Q5. Multiple choice: What did Pharaoh say to Moses after the darkness? (10:28-29) Hard
A) "Let us talk tomorrow."   B) "Your God is good."   C) "Get away from me! Do not see my face again."   D) "Take your people and go."
Answer: C. "Get away from me! Do not see my face again.". Pride pushes away the messenger God sends to help.


Q6. Fill in the blank: The plague of darkness was so thick that it could be ______. (10:21) Medium
Answer: felt. Some judgments reach the body and the spirit, so people cannot pretend they do not see.


Q7. True or false: Pharaoh agreed that the livestock could go with Israel. (10:24-26) Easy
Answer: False. Pharaoh tried to keep leverage over God's people; Moses insisted that not a hoof would be left behind.


Q8. True or false: God had rejected Moses and Aaron after Pharaoh banished them from his sight. (10:28-11:1) Hard
Answer: False. God does not reject His servants when people reject them; His plan keeps moving forward.


Exodus 11
Q1. True or false: Moses was afraid of Pharaoh and hid from him after the tenth plague was announced. (11:8) Hard
Answer: False. Moses went out in hot anger, and the text shows his emotion honestly without letting it overrule God's word.


Q2. Fill in the blank: God told Moses that after one more plague, Pharaoh would ______ the Israelites out of Egypt. (11:1) Easy
Answer: drive. God's final judgment ends in Israel's release, showing that His plan does not depend on the king.


Q3. True or false: God said there would be a great cry in Egypt, but not a dog would bark against Israel. (11:4-7) Medium
Answer: True. God distinguishes His people. The line between judgment and mercy runs through the nation.


Q4. Fill in the blank: The Egyptian people regarded Moses as very great in the land of ______. (11:3) Medium
Answer: Egypt. God honors His servant when He works through him, and humility keeps the glory with the Lord.


Q5. True or false: God announced the plague on Egypt because Egypt had been kind to Israel. (11:1-9) Easy
Answer: False. The plagues answer Egypt's oppression; they are about God's justice, not about rewarding kindness.


Q6. Multiple choice: What did God tell the Israelites to ask from their Egyptian neighbors? (11:2) Easy
A) Horses   B) Silver and gold items   C) Servants   D) Land
Answer: B. Silver and gold items. God gives His people honor and provision, even out of places of oppression.


Q7. Multiple choice: Why did God say He would multiply His wonders in Egypt? (11:9-10) Medium
A) So God's signs and wonders would be multiplied in the land of Egypt   B) Because Egypt needed a new king   C) So Moses would become famous   D) Because Pharaoh asked for them
Answer: A. So God's signs and wonders would be multiplied in the land of Egypt. God uses even hardened hearts to display His power and make His name known.


Q8. Multiple choice: What does the announcement of the firstborn's death reveal about God? (11:4-8) Hard
A) God takes oppression seriously and brings justice against it   B) God has no concern for Egypt   C) Egypt's fault was small   D) God is weak against Pharaoh
Answer: A. God takes oppression seriously and brings justice against it. God's justice is real, and those who oppress His people face judgment in time.


Exodus 12
Q1. True or false: The Passover applies only to native-born Israelites and cannot include foreigners who live among them. (12:43-49) Hard
Answer: False. Foreigners who join Israel and are circumcised may eat it; God's salvation is open to those who come under His covenant.


Q2. Fill in the blank: God told Israel that the blood on the doorposts would be a ______ for them. (12:13) Easy
Answer: sign. God gives visible signs of His mercy, and the blood marks a life spared by grace.


Q3. Fill in the blank: Eat it in haste, for it is the LORD's ______. (12:11) Medium
Answer: Passover. Remembering God's saving acts is part of worship, and God's people are called to remember.


Q4. Multiple choice: What did the blood on the doorposts signal to the LORD? (12:12-13) Easy
A) The house was a Hebrew house   B) The family had a debt to pay   C) The family wanted to leave   D) The house was protected from the destroyer
Answer: D. The house was protected from the destroyer. Rescue comes through a substitute's blood and God's word, not by the merit of those who are saved.


Q5. Multiple choice: What does the Passover teach about Israel's deliverance? (12:11-13) Medium
A) Israel earned freedom by their own strength   B) Moses planned the escape   C) Israel escaped through the Red Sea alone   D) Israel was rescued by God's grace through a provided sacrifice
Answer: D. Israel was rescued by God's grace through a provided sacrifice. The Passover shows that rescue comes from God, through a sacrifice He provides.


Q6. Multiple choice: Why did God call the Passover "a statute forever"? (12:14) Hard
A) To make the people proud   B) To keep Moses busy   C) So the people would remember God's saving act and teach it to the next generation   D) Because the Egyptians required it
Answer: C. So the people would remember God's saving act and teach it to the next generation. God designs worship that teaches the next generation what He has done.


Q7. True or false: God passed over the houses marked with the blood of the lamb. (12:13) Easy
Answer: True. The Passover points forward to Christ, the Lamb of God whose blood saves those who trust Him.


Q8. True or false: Israel ate unleavened bread for seven days to remember the haste of their departure. (12:15-20) Medium
Answer: True. Remembering God's deliverance shapes the ordinary days of His people.


Exodus 13
Q1. Multiple choice: Why did God set apart every firstborn male, human and animal? (13:2, 13-15) Easy
A) Because Pharaoh demanded it   B) Because the firstborn were the strongest   C) To provide food for the priests only   D) To remind Israel that God spared their firstborn and that they belonged to Him
Answer: D. To remind Israel that God spared their firstborn and that they belonged to Him. What God has spared belongs to Him, and that deliverance creates a debt of thanks.


Q2. Multiple choice: What did Moses take with him when he left Egypt? (13:19) Medium
A) Gold from Egypt   B) Egyptian weapons   C) The bones of Joseph   D) Pharaoh's records
Answer: C. The bones of Joseph. Joseph's trust in God's promise was honored, which shows that God's promises outlast generations.


Q3. True or false: God led Israel by a pillar of cloud by day and a pillar of fire by night. (13:21-22) Easy
Answer: True. God's guidance was constant and visible, and it carried His people through every stage of the journey.


Q4. Multiple choice: How does the Feast of Unleavened Bread teach the next generation? (13:8) Hard
A) It teaches Egyptian customs   B) Parents explain that it is because of what the LORD did when He brought them out   C) It is only for priests   D) It is only a historical festival
Answer: B. Parents explain that it is because of what the LORD did when He brought them out. Faith is passed on when parents explain what God has done, not only when they keep the custom.


Q5. Fill in the blank: The LORD said that the law of the feast would be a ______ on your hand. (13:9) Easy
Answer: sign. Remembering God's deliverance should show in daily life, in what we do and what we think about.


Q6. True or false: A firstborn donkey was redeemed with a lamb, or else its neck was broken. (13:13) Hard
Answer: True. God requires a substitute for what belongs to Him, which shows that redemption costs something.


Q7. True or false: God led Israel by the shortest route, through the land of the Philistines, to avoid war. (13:17-18) Medium
Answer: False. God's leading is wise and protective, even when it is not the route people would choose.


Q8. Fill in the blank: God did not lead the people by the way of the land of the ______, though it was near. (13:17) Medium
Answer: Philistines. God leads His people by the path that serves their good, even when it is not the shortest route.


Exodus 14
Q1. True or false: The Egyptians fled from the sea and reached home safely. (14:27-28) Hard
Answer: False. God's justice is complete; no one who sets himself against the LORD escapes by his own strength.


Q2. Multiple choice: How did the Egyptians pursue Israel? (14:5-9) Easy
A) They sent spies to Moses   B) They pursued Israel with chariots and horsemen to the sea   C) They sent the navy   D) They waited for them at Sinai
Answer: B. They pursued Israel with chariots and horsemen to the sea. Pride and fear drive the wicked toward judgment, and God lets them go as far as their hardened hearts take them.


Q3. Multiple choice: What does Moses' staff represent in the crossing of the sea? (14:16, 21) Hard
A) A magic charm   B) Moses' own power to split the sea   C) A weapon for Israel's army   D) A sign that God's power works through His servant's obedience
Answer: D. A sign that God's power works through His servant's obedience. God's power works through obedient servants, and the power is always His.


Q4. True or false: When Israel was trapped at the sea, God told Moses to stretch out his hand over the sea. (14:15-16) Easy
Answer: True. God opens a way through the impossible when His people obey His word.


Q5. True or false: Israel believed the LORD and His servant Moses after they saw the Egyptians dead on the shore. (14:31) Medium
Answer: True. Faith often follows God's visible deliverance, and God's people respond with trust.


Q6. Fill in the blank: Israel saw the great work that the LORD did against the ______. (14:31) Medium
Answer: Egyptians. Seeing God's work should lead to fear of Him and to belief in His servant.


Q7. Fill in the blank: Moses told Israel, "The LORD will fight for you, and you have only to be ______." (14:14) Easy
Answer: silent. Peace in danger comes from trusting God's fight, not from our own striving.


Q8. Multiple choice: What happened when the Egyptians followed Israel into the sea? (14:23-28) Medium
A) They built a bridge   B) They drowned Moses   C) The waters returned and covered them   D) They turned back immediately
Answer: C. The waters returned and covered them. God's deliverance of His people is also judgment on those who oppose Him.


Exodus 15
Q1. True or false: God's people have to repeat the same song each day to earn God's favor. (15:1-18) Medium
Answer: False. The song praises what God has done, not what people must do to earn His favor.


Q2. Multiple choice: The song calls the LORD "a man of war." What does this teach? (15:3) Medium
A) God only fights in the wilderness   B) God takes sides against His people   C) God fights for His people against evil and their enemies   D) God is unwilling to fight
Answer: C. God fights for His people against evil and their enemies. God's power is greater than any human enemy, and He defends His people.


Q3. Multiple choice: What does it mean that Miriam led the women in song? (15:20-21) Hard
A) Women were forbidden to sing   B) Miriam was asserting power over Moses   C) Women should lead worship only in the wilderness   D) Worship of God includes the whole people, and a prophetess shares in praise
Answer: D. Worship of God includes the whole people, and a prophetess shares in praise. Everyone who has been delivered has a part to play in praising the LORD.


Q4. True or false: God led Israel into the wilderness and then gave them rest at Elim, with water and palm trees. (15:27) Hard
Answer: True. God leads His people through hard places and provides places of refreshment along the way.


Q5. Fill in the blank: Moses and Israel sang, "The LORD is my strength and my ______." (15:2) Easy
Answer: song. Worship grows out of God's deliverance; the song is the response to His saving act.


Q6. Fill in the blank: "Who is like you, O LORD, among the ______?" (15:11) Medium
Answer: gods. No other god can rescue or hold together; God's holiness is unique.


Q7. True or false: God said, "I am the LORD, your healer," after Israel was given sweet water. (15:26) Easy
Answer: True. God is both deliverer and healer, and His commands are part of how His people walk in health.


Q8. Multiple choice: What did God show Moses that made the bitter water at Marah sweet? (15:23-25) Easy
A) Bread   B) A tree   C) A cloud   D) A stone
Answer: B. A tree. God provides a remedy for bitter circumstances, often through means His people would not expect.


Exodus 16
Q1. True or false: The manna continued for Israel until they reached the border of the land of Canaan. (16:35) Hard
Answer: True. God provided faithfully for forty years until His people could eat the produce of the land.


Q2. Fill in the blank: "The LORD has given you the Sabbath; therefore on the sixth day He gives you bread for ______ days." (16:29) Medium
Answer: two. Rest is a gift from God, and He provides so that His people can rest without fear.


Q3. Multiple choice: Why did God give a double portion of manna on the sixth day? (16:22-26) Medium
A) To punish Israel   B) For the Egyptians   C) To feed the animals   D) So Israel could store food for the Sabbath, when no manna fell
Answer: D. So Israel could store food for the Sabbath, when no manna fell. God honors the Sabbath rest and provides for His people's rest as well as their work.


Q4. True or false: Some people went out on the Sabbath to gather manna, and God was pleased. (16:27-28) Medium
Answer: False. Rest is God's gift, and refusing to rest shows a lack of trust in His provision.


Q5. Fill in the blank: God gave Israel bread from heaven, which they called ______. (16:15) Easy
Answer: manna. God provides daily for His people, and they learn to depend on Him day by day.


Q6. Multiple choice: What was the rule about gathering manna? (16:16-20) Easy
A) Gather only on the Sabbath   B) Gather as much as you can   C) Gather what you need for each day, and do not store it until morning   D) Gather only for the priests
Answer: C. Gather what you need for each day, and do not store it until morning. God trains His people to trust Him for daily provision rather than hoard.


Q7. Multiple choice: Why was a jar of manna placed before the LORD? (16:32-34) Hard
A) To keep a memorial for future generations of God's provision   B) As Moses' personal reminder   C) As a treat for the priests   D) To show the Egyptians
Answer: A. To keep a memorial for future generations of God's provision. God's faithful provision is to be remembered and taught.


Q8. True or false: Some Israelites kept manna until the morning, and it bred worms and stank. (16:19-20) Easy
Answer: True. Trying to store up what God gives for today shows a lack of trust in His daily care.


Exodus 17
Q1. True or false: Amalek was defeated because Israel's army was stronger than Amalek's. (17:10-13) Medium
Answer: False. The victory belongs to the LORD, and Israel's army fought under His power.


Q2. Multiple choice: What determined whether Israel prevailed against Amalek? (17:8-11) Easy
A) Whether Moses' hands were raised   B) The number of soldiers   C) The strength of Joshua's army   D) The weather
Answer: A. Whether Moses' hands were raised. Victory depends on God's power received through prayer and faith, not on human strength alone.


Q3. Fill in the blank: Moses named the altar "The LORD Is My ______." (17:15) Easy
Answer: banner. God's presence is the source of victory, and worship names Him as that source.


Q4. Multiple choice: What does the altar "The LORD Is My Banner" mean? (17:15-16) Hard
A) A memorial to the war dead   B) God's presence is the source of Israel's victory   C) Moses' personal flag   D) A trade symbol
Answer: B. God's presence is the source of Israel's victory. God's people trust Him as the one who fights for them, and they remember it.


Q5. True or false: Moses sat on a stone while Joshua fought, and Aaron and Hur held up his hands. (17:12) Hard
Answer: True. God's people need one another to hold up their hands in hard battles.


Q6. Fill in the blank: The people asked, "Is the LORD among us or ______?" (17:7) Medium
Answer: not. Testing God's presence after He has shown it is a form of unbelief.


Q7. True or false: Moses struck the rock at Horeb, and water came out for the people to drink. (17:6) Easy
Answer: True. God provides water in the wilderness when His people have no way to find it themselves.


Q8. Multiple choice: Why did the people test the LORD at Massah and Meribah? (17:7) Medium
A) They wanted gold   B) They wanted to go back to Egypt   C) They wanted to see whether God was present   D) Moses commanded it
Answer: C. They wanted to see whether God was present. Asking whether the LORD is among us after He has shown Himself reveals unbelief.


Exodus 18
Q1. True or false: Jethro offered burnt offerings and sacrifices to God in front of Moses and Aaron. (18:12) Medium
Answer: True. God is known beyond Israel's camp, and Jethro's worship shows that His reach is wide.


Q2. Multiple choice: Why does it matter that Moses listened to Jethro's counsel? (18:13-24) Hard
A) It showed humility and let God work through others   B) Pharaoh required it   C) It showed weakness   D) It cost him nothing
Answer: A. It showed humility and let God work through others. Humble leaders receive counsel, and God uses wise counsel to shape His people.


Q3. True or false: Moses rejected his father-in-law's advice because he thought he alone should judge. (18:24) Easy
Answer: False. Humility receives wise counsel.


Q4. Fill in the blank: Leaders must be able men who fear God, men of truth, hating ______ gain. (18:21) Medium
Answer: dishonest. Leaders are tested by integrity as well as skill.


Q5. Multiple choice: What qualities were the new leaders to have? (18:21) Medium
A) Military rank   B) Wealth   C) Ability, fear of God, trustworthiness, and hatred of dishonest gain   D) Age only
Answer: C. Ability, fear of God, trustworthiness, and hatred of dishonest gain. Godly character matters more than status in leadership.


Q6. Fill in the blank: Jethro said, "You will surely wear yourself out, for the task is too heavy for ______." (18:18) Easy
Answer: you. Good leaders learn to share responsibility, and God's work is carried by many.


Q7. True or false: The appointed judges handled the small cases, and Moses kept the great cases for himself. (18:22) Hard
Answer: False. The appointed judges were to bring the great matters to Moses, and they handled the small ones.


Q8. Multiple choice: What did Jethro recommend to Moses? (18:21) Easy
A) Israel should return to Midian   B) Moses should judge every case himself   C) Capable men of character should be appointed as leaders over thousands, hundreds, fifties and tens   D) Moses should stop judging
Answer: C. Capable men of character should be appointed as leaders over thousands, hundreds, fifties and tens. God often lifts the burden of leadership through shared, godly responsibility.


Exodus 19
Q1. Multiple choice: What did God require of the people before He came down on Sinai? (19:10-11) Medium
A) Building a temple   B) Choosing a king   C) Consecration and washing their garments   D) Sacrifices every day
Answer: C. Consecration and washing their garments. Preparation shows that God's people approach Him with reverence.


Q2. Fill in the blank: God told the people to consecrate themselves and wash their ______. (19:10) Medium
Answer: garments. God calls His people to prepare their hearts and lives to meet Him.


Q3. Multiple choice: What did God call Israel? (19:6) Easy
A) A kingdom of priests and a holy nation   B) A nation of merchants   C) A nation of kings for Pharaoh   D) A nation of soldiers
Answer: A. A kingdom of priests and a holy nation. Israel is called to be set apart for God and to represent Him among the nations.


Q4. True or false: The people were told not to touch the mountain, on pain of death. (19:12) Easy
Answer: True. God's holiness requires a careful approach, and access comes by His grace and mediation.


Q5. True or false: God's offer at Sinai was conditional on Israel's obedience, after He had already shown grace in the Exodus. (19:4-5) Hard
Answer: True. The law follows grace: God saves first and then calls His people to obedience.


Q6. Multiple choice: Why did God say "I bore you on eagles' wings"? (19:4) Hard
A) Eagles guided Israel   B) To show God's personal, protective deliverance and that He brought them to Himself   C) It is a title for Moses   D) To show Israel's pride
Answer: B. To show God's personal, protective deliverance and that He brought them to Himself. God's saving work is tender and personal, carrying His people toward Himself.
