Q7. True or false: Moses went up the mountain, and the people were allowed to break through to see God. (19:20-24) Medium
Answer: False. God's presence was real but guarded; the people were told not to break through to see Him.


Q8. Fill in the blank: God said, "I bore you on eagles' ______ and brought you to myself." (19:4) Easy
Answer: wings. God's deliverance is personal and protective, and He brings His people near to Himself.


Exodus 20
Q1. Multiple choice: What does the command against images teach? (20:4-6) Medium
A) Only Moses could make images   B) God wants no art at all   C) Images are useful for worship   D) God is not to be represented by created things, because He is jealous for His own honor
Answer: D. God is not to be represented by created things, because He is jealous for His own honor. God's people worship Him as He has revealed Himself, not as they imagine Him.


Q2. Multiple choice: Which command concerns the Sabbath? (20:8-11) Easy
A) Honor your father and mother   B) Do not steal   C) Do not murder   D) Remember the Sabbath day, to keep it holy
Answer: D. Remember the Sabbath day, to keep it holy. Rest is built into creation and into God's law, and God's people trust Him enough to stop.


Q3. Fill in the blank: "You shall not take the name of the LORD your God in ______." (20:7) Medium
Answer: vain. God's name carries His character, and using it carelessly misrepresents Him.


Q4. True or false: The people were terrified when they heard God's voice and asked Moses to speak to them instead of God. (20:18-19) Hard
Answer: True. Fear of God's holiness drives people to a mediator, which points forward to Christ.


Q5. Fill in the blank: "You shall have no other ______ before me." (20:3) Easy
Answer: gods. The first command guards the heart, because nothing else can take God's place.


Q6. True or false: The Ten Commandments begin with a reminder of God's deliverance from Egypt. (20:2) Easy
Answer: True. The law rests on grace: God first rescued His people, and then He shaped them.


Q7. True or false: Honoring father and mother is linked to living long in the land the LORD gives Israel. (20:12) Medium
Answer: True. The command explicitly connects honoring parents with long life in the land; Ephesians 6:2 also draws attention to its promise.


Q8. Multiple choice: What does "You shall not covet" reveal about the law? (20:17) Hard
A) It only forbids actions   B) It applies only to kings   C) It is only a civil law   D) It reaches the heart and the desire for what belongs to another
Answer: D. It reaches the heart and the desire for what belongs to another. God's law reaches inward; sin begins in desire before it becomes action.


Exodus 21
Q1. True or false: The law required a person who struck his servant to be killed immediately, whatever happened. (21:20-21) Easy
Answer: False. Even servants are protected by the law, because a servant is a person made in God's image, not property.


Q2. True or false: If a master struck out a servant's tooth, that servant was to be released. (21:27) Medium
Answer: True. This law requires release for the injury. Describing this ancient slavery regulation does not endorse slavery or make every feature of it a model for today.


Q3. Multiple choice: What did the law say about a servant who chose to stay with his master? (21:5-6) Easy
A) He should be set free at once   B) He should be sold   C) He should be put to death   D) He would serve for life, marked by an ear pierced at the doorpost
Answer: D. He would serve for life, marked by an ear pierced at the doorpost. Freedom and commitment both matter, and love can choose to stay under God's ordering.


Q4. Multiple choice: What does the law say about an open pit that an ox falls into? (21:33-34) Medium
A) The pit must be filled with stone   B) The owner owes nothing   C) The pit must be sold   D) The owner of the pit must pay for the animal
Answer: D. The owner of the pit must pay for the animal. Responsibility for harm to others is part of loving one's neighbor.


Q5. Fill in the blank: The law of retribution said, "Eye for ______, tooth for tooth." (21:24) Easy
Answer: eye. Justice is measured and proportionate; it restrains vengeance rather than inflaming it.


Q6. Multiple choice: Why does the law protect a servant who loses an eye or a tooth at his master's hand? (21:26-27) Hard
A) It was Pharaoh's law   B) So servants could work harder   C) God's law sees servants as persons and protects them, and the harmed servant goes free   D) Owners had no duty to servants
Answer: C. God's law sees servants as persons and protects them, and the harmed servant goes free. God's law protects the vulnerable, and the strong must answer for harm they do.


Q7. True or false: If a master knocked out a servant's eye or tooth, the servant had to be set free. (21:26-27) Hard
Answer: True. God's law sets limits on power and grants freedom when a servant is harmed.


Q8. Fill in the blank: If an open pit causes an ox to be lost, the owner of the pit shall make ______ to the owner of the ox. (21:34) Medium
Answer: restitution. God's justice restores what was lost and treats harm to others as a real debt.


Exodus 22
Q1. True or false: God forbids charging interest to a poor person among His people. (22:25) Easy
Answer: True. Generosity to the poor is part of covenant faithfulness.


Q2. Multiple choice: What did the law say about a neighbor's cloak taken as a pledge? (22:26-27) Medium
A) Return it by sunset, because the poor need it to sleep   B) Burn it   C) Sell it at market   D) Keep it forever
Answer: A. Return it by sunset, because the poor need it to sleep. Mercy looks at the need of the neighbor, not only at the value of the debt.


Q3. True or false: The firstborn sons were to be given back to the LORD, as the firstborn of animals were. (22:29-30) Medium
Answer: True. What belongs to God is given back to Him, which shows devotion rather than possession.


Q4. Fill in the blank: "You shall not ______ a sojourner or oppress him, for you were sojourners in Egypt." (22:21) Medium
Answer: wrong. Israel was once a stranger in Egypt, so they must not wrong others who are strangers.


Q5. True or false: God told Israel to remember that they were once strangers in Egypt. (22:21) Hard
Answer: True. Remembering their own history of suffering shapes how God's people treat the vulnerable.


Q6. Multiple choice: What did God say He would do if a widow or orphan cried out to Him? (22:22-24) Easy
A) Hear their cry, and His anger would burn against their oppressors   B) Send them to Pharaoh   C) Ignore them   D) Give them land
Answer: A. Hear their cry, and His anger would burn against their oppressors. God hears the cries of the oppressed and acts for justice.


Q7. Multiple choice: What does "you shall be holy men to me" (22:31) teach? (22:31) Hard
A) Holiness is optional   B) Holiness is only for priests   C) God's people are set apart in every part of life, including what they eat and how they treat others   D) Holiness is only about diet
Answer: C. God's people are set apart in every part of life, including what they eat and how they treat others. Holiness shapes ordinary choices, not only worship.


Q8. Fill in the blank: "You shall not ______ a widow or an orphan." (22:22) Easy
Answer: afflict. God takes special notice of the vulnerable and defends those who cannot defend themselves.


Exodus 23
Q1. Multiple choice: What does the Sabbath year (23:10-11) teach? (23:10-11) Medium
A) Israel must fight every year   B) The land rests, and the poor may eat what grows, which shows God's provision and care   C) Only the rich may eat   D) The land must be sold
Answer: B. The land rests, and the poor may eat what grows, which shows God's provision and care. God cares for the land and for the poor, and His people rest in His provision.


Q2. Fill in the blank: "Three times in the year you shall keep a ______ to me." (23:14) Medium
Answer: feast. Worship is regular and joyful, a rhythm of remembering what God has done.


Q3. True or false: God said He would drive out the nations gradually, so that the land would not become desolate. (23:29-30) Easy
Answer: True. God's timing in judgment and in giving the land is patient and wise, not hasty.


Q4. Multiple choice: What does God's warning against making covenants with the nations (23:32-33) teach? (23:32-33) Hard
A) Compromise with idols becomes a snare, so God's people must not serve their gods   B) Israel should be isolated for its own sake   C) God does not care about the nations   D) Israel should trade with all nations
Answer: A. Compromise with idols becomes a snare, so God's people must not serve their gods. Compromise with other gods is a danger to faith, so God's people must guard their worship.


Q5. Fill in the blank: The LORD said He would send an ______ before Israel to guard the way. (23:20) Easy
Answer: angel. God guards His people's journey and leads them into the place He has prepared.


Q6. Multiple choice: Which three feasts were Israel to keep each year? (23:14-17) Easy
A) Feast of Unleavened Bread, Feast of Harvest, and Feast of Ingathering   B) Feast of Trumpets, Feast of Lights, and Feast of Tabernacles   C) Passover, Pentecost, and Purim   D) Sabbath, New Moon, and Jubilee
Answer: A. Feast of Unleavened Bread, Feast of Harvest, and Feast of Ingathering. Israel's annual feasts keep God's saving acts central to the calendar of His people.


Q7. True or false: God promised that He would not drive out the enemies at all. (23:27-31) Medium
Answer: False. God promised to drive out the nations, though gradually, so Israel's future would be secure.


Q8. True or false: God told Israel not to bow down to the gods of the nations or serve them. (23:24) Hard
Answer: True. Worship is exclusive, and God's people are not to imitate the worship of the nations around them.


Exodus 24
Q1. Multiple choice: Who went up the mountain with Moses? (24:1, 9) Easy
A) The Egyptian magicians   B) Pharaoh's army   C) Aaron, Nadab, Abihu and seventy of the elders   D) Only Joshua
Answer: C. Aaron, Nadab, Abihu and seventy of the elders. God invites His people into His presence, and covenant worship is shared.


Q2. Multiple choice: Why did the people say, "All that the LORD has spoken we will do"? (24:3, 7) Medium
A) They did not understand the words   B) They were forced to say it   C) They wanted to leave Sinai   D) They committed themselves to obey the covenant terms
Answer: D. They committed themselves to obey the covenant terms. Covenant commitment is a response to God's word, which the people accepted.


Q3. True or false: Israel's covenant was made without blood, because God does not need blood. (24:5-8) Hard
Answer: False. God sealed the covenant with blood, which points to the cost of redemption.


Q4. Fill in the blank: Moses sprinkled the blood and said, "Behold the blood of the ______ that the LORD has made with you." (24:8) Easy
Answer: covenant. God's covenant is sealed with blood, which points to a deeper covenant sealed by Christ's blood.


Q5. True or false: Moses was on the mountain for forty days and forty nights. (24:18) Medium
Answer: True. God's leaders wait on Him, and formation often takes time.


Q6. Fill in the blank: "Moses went up into the mountain, and the cloud covered the ______." (24:15) Medium
Answer: mountain. God's glory is present but veiled, and His people wait on Him.


Q7. Multiple choice: What does the glory of the LORD on Sinai teach? (24:15-17) Hard
A) God hid from Moses   B) Sinai is a volcano   C) God is far away   D) God's holiness is real, and His presence, though glorious, is approached through covenant
Answer: D. God's holiness is real, and His presence, though glorious, is approached through covenant. God is both holy and near to His people.


Q8. True or false: The leaders of Israel saw the God of Israel and ate and drank in His presence. (24:9-11) Easy
Answer: True. God's grace let His people draw near in covenant friendship, though no one could see Him fully.


Exodus 25
Q1. Multiple choice: What was the bread of the Presence set on the table for? (25:30) Medium
A) Offerings for sin   B) Food for the priests only   C) Decoration   D) A continual reminder before the LORD of His people
Answer: D. A continual reminder before the LORD of His people. God's people stand before Him continually, and He provides for them.


Q2. Multiple choice: Why were the ark's poles to stay in its rings? (25:14-15) Hard
A) The poles were only decoration   B) To keep the ark from falling   C) So it could be carried without anyone touching it   D) To keep the poles from rotting
Answer: C. So it could be carried without anyone touching it. Holy things were approached in God's way, not casually.


Q3. True or false: God showed Moses a pattern on the mountain, and the tabernacle was to be made exactly to that pattern. (25:40) Hard
Answer: True. Worship is shaped by God's design, not by human invention.


Q4. True or false: The LORD asked that the offering be given only by the priests, not by the people. (25:2) Easy
Answer: False. Every willing person was invited to give toward the house of God.


Q5. Fill in the blank: The lampstand was to be made of pure ______. (25:31) Medium
Answer: gold. Light in God's house points to Him as the source of light, as in Genesis 1.


Q6. Fill in the blank: The LORD told Moses to take an offering from every person whose ______ moved him to give. (25:2) Easy
Answer: heart. God invites willing giving that comes from the heart.


Q7. True or false: The mercy seat was to be made of solid gold, with cherubim at each end. (25:17-19) Medium
Answer: True. The place of atonement and God's presence is made of pure gold, showing His holiness.


Q8. Multiple choice: What was the ark of the covenant to be made of? (25:10-11) Easy
A) Bronze only   B) Solid silver   C) Stone from the river   D) Acacia wood overlaid with pure gold
Answer: D. Acacia wood overlaid with pure gold. God's presence is kept in a holy place made with care and worship.


Exodus 26
Q1. Fill in the blank: The tent was to be fastened with clasps so that it would be a ______. (26:11) Hard
Answer: single whole. God is building one dwelling place for His people.


Q2. True or false: The veil was to be hung on four pillars of acacia wood overlaid with gold. (26:32) Medium
Answer: True. The barrier before God's presence was costly, sturdy and set in place by God's command.


Q3. Multiple choice: What was the veil between the Holy Place and the Most Holy Place made of? (26:31) Easy
A) Goat hair   B) Leather   C) Plain linen only   D) Blue, purple and scarlet yarn with cherubim worked into it
Answer: D. Blue, purple and scarlet yarn with cherubim worked into it. The veil separated God's holy presence from the people, and it was made with beauty.


Q4. Fill in the blank: The tabernacle was to have ______ curtains of fine twined linen. (26:1) Easy
Answer: ten. The number and the fine linen show care and beauty in worship.


Q5. Multiple choice: What did God require at the entrance of the tent? (26:36) Hard
A) A curtain of blue, purple and scarlet yarn   B) Wooden doors with bronze locks   C) A stone gate   D) Nothing at all
Answer: A. A curtain of blue, purple and scarlet yarn. Access to God's presence is governed by His word and handled with care.


Q6. Fill in the blank: The boards of the tabernacle were made of ______ wood. (26:15) Medium
Answer: acacia. The sanctuary was built from common materials made holy by God's purpose.


Q7. Multiple choice: What covering was placed over the tent to protect it? (26:14) Medium
A) Woven reeds   B) Stone slabs   C) Rams' skins dyed red and goatskins   D) Gold sheets
Answer: C. Rams' skins dyed red and goatskins. God provides protection for the place where He meets His people.


Q8. True or false: The boards of the tabernacle were to be set in silver bases. (26:19) Easy
Answer: True. Each part of the holy place was made to stand firm.


Exodus 27
Q1. True or false: The bronze altar had horns at its four corners. (27:2) Medium
Answer: True. The horns marked the altar as a holy place set apart for God's work.


Q2. True or false: Israel was to bring pure oil of beaten olives for the lamp to burn continually. (27:20) Easy
Answer: True. God's light is kept burning through the continual offering of His people.


Q3. True or false: The courtyard of the tabernacle was to be 100 cubits long and 50 cubits wide. (27:18) Hard
Answer: True. The space around God's presence was measured and set apart by His exact plan.


Q4. Multiple choice: What was the altar of burnt offering for? (27:1) Easy
A) Storing gold   B) Burnt offerings   C) Holding court   D) Washing clothes
Answer: B. Burnt offerings. Sacrifice is central to approaching God.


Q5. Multiple choice: Who was to keep the lamp burning from evening to morning before the LORD? (27:21) Medium
A) The elders   B) Aaron and his sons   C) Joshua   D) Moses alone
Answer: B. Aaron and his sons. God appoints priests to keep worship faithful.


Q6. Fill in the blank: The altar of burnt offering was to be made of acacia wood overlaid with ______. (27:1-2) Easy
Answer: bronze. The altar was built to bear the weight of many sacrifices.


Q7. Multiple choice: What were the bowls, shovels and basins of the altar to be made of? (27:3) Hard
A) Bronze   B) Acacia wood   C) Silver   D) Gold
Answer: A. Bronze. Worship items were made with care, as God designed.


Q8. Fill in the blank: The courtyard was to have curtains of fine ______ linen. (27:9) Medium
Answer: twined. God's holiness is marked at every boundary of His presence.


Exodus 28
Q1. Fill in the blank: Aaron was to bear the iniquity of the holy things, so that the people's gifts would be ______ before the LORD. (28:38) Hard
Answer: accepted. The priest carries the people's offerings before God so that worship is accepted.


Q2. Multiple choice: What was written on the gold plate on Aaron's turban? (28:36) Easy
A) Glory to Pharaoh   B) Israel is king   C) Holiness to the LORD   D) Blessed be Abraham
Answer: C. Holiness to the LORD. Worship must be holy, and the priest carries that holiness for the people.


Q3. True or false: Aaron's sons were to wear linen undergarments to cover their nakedness. (28:42) Medium
Answer: True. Priests approached God's altar with modesty and reverence.


Q4. Fill in the blank: The ephod was to be made of gold, blue, purple, scarlet and fine ______ linen. (28:6) Medium
Answer: twined. Holy garments were crafted with skill and beauty.


Q5. Multiple choice: How many stones were in the breastpiece? (28:17-21) Medium
A) Seven   B) Forty   C) Twelve   D) Six
Answer: C. Twelve. Israel's twelve tribes are carried into God's presence.


Q6. Multiple choice: Why were the priests to wear linen undergarments? (28:42-43) Hard
A) For warmth   B) So they would not incur guilt and die when they approached the altar   C) As a sign of rank   D) To hide their faces
Answer: B. So they would not incur guilt and die when they approached the altar. Approaching God's presence requires holiness and reverence.


Q7. True or false: The priestly garments were made for glory and for beauty. (28:2) Easy
Answer: True. God cares about beauty in worship that honors Him.


Q8. Fill in the blank: Aaron was to wear the names of the sons of Israel on the stones of the ______ over his heart. (28:21) Easy
Answer: breastpiece. The priest carries God's people on his heart before the LORD.


Exodus 29
Q1. Multiple choice: What was Moses instructed to pour on Aaron's head? (29:7) Easy
A) Honey   B) Anointing oil   C) Water from the Nile   D) Wine
Answer: B. Anointing oil. The verse specifically commands Aaron's anointing. Verse 21 separately describes sprinkling Aaron and his sons with blood and anointing oil.


Q2. Fill in the blank: Blood from the ram of ordination was to be put on the right ______ of Aaron and his sons. (29:20) Medium
Answer: ear. Every part of the priest's life is dedicated to God.


Q3. Multiple choice: Why was blood put on the ear, thumb and toe of the priests? (29:20) Hard
A) As decoration   B) To mark a tribe   C) To cure disease   D) To show that the priest's whole life is given to God
Answer: D. To show that the priest's whole life is given to God. Holiness covers hearing, acting and walking.


Q4. Fill in the blank: The LORD told Moses to consecrate Aaron and his sons to serve as ______ to Him. (29:1) Easy
Answer: priests. God calls and sets apart leaders for His service.


Q5. True or false: Aaron and his sons were to wash with water before they put on their garments. (29:4) Easy
Answer: True. Those who serve God come clean before they serve.


Q6. Fill in the blank: The priests were to eat the bread and the meat of the ordination offering, because it was ______. (29:33) Hard
Answer: holy. Holy things belong to holy service.


Q7. True or false: God promised to dwell among the Israelites and to be their God. (29:45) Medium
Answer: True. God's promise of His presence is at the heart of the covenant.


Q8. Multiple choice: What was offered every morning and every evening as a continual burnt offering? (29:38-39) Medium
A) Grain only   B) Two lambs one year old   C) A bull   D) A goat
Answer: B. Two lambs one year old. Daily sacrifice reminds God's people of their constant need.


Exodus 30
Q1. True or false: The bronze basin was for the priests to wash their hands and feet before entering the tent. (30:18-19) Easy
Answer: True. Priests approached God clean.


Q2. True or false: The altar of incense was to be made of acacia wood overlaid with pure gold. (30:1-3) Hard
Answer: True. Prayer and worship before God are holy and costly.


Q3. Multiple choice: What was the atonement money each person was to give when the people were counted? (30:12-13) Easy
A) Ten shekels   B) A goat   C) A half shekel   D) Nothing
Answer: C. A half shekel. Everyone is counted equal before God and redeemed by the same price.


Q4. Fill in the blank: Aaron and his sons were to wash so that they would not ______. (30:20-21) Medium
Answer: die. God's holy service requires cleansing and reverence.


Q5. Multiple choice: What was the holy anointing oil made of? (30:23-25) Medium
A) Water and salt   B) Olive oil with fragrant spices   C) Animal fat   D) Wine
Answer: B. Olive oil with fragrant spices. The oil is holy and not to be used for common purposes.


Q6. Multiple choice: Why did each person give the same half shekel, rich or poor? (30:15) Hard
A) So rich and poor stand as equals before God and need the same atonement   B) It was only for the poor   C) Pharaoh's rule   D) Rich people had to pay more
Answer: A. So rich and poor stand as equals before God and need the same atonement. Before God, rich and poor stand as equals and need the same atonement.


Q7. True or false: The incense recipe was to be made for the LORD alone, not for personal use. (30:37-38) Medium
Answer: True. Holy things are set apart for worship alone.


Q8. Fill in the blank: Aaron was to burn fragrant ______ on the altar of incense every morning. (30:7) Easy
Answer: incense. Prayer rises before God like fragrant incense.


Exodus 31
Q1. Multiple choice: How was the Sabbath to be kept? (31:14-15) Medium
A) As a market day   B) As a day of rest, holy to the LORD   C) As a feast for kings   D) As a day of war
Answer: B. As a day of rest, holy to the LORD. Rest is a holy commandment, not an option.


Q2. Fill in the blank: God filled Bezalel with the ______ of God, with skill and ability for every craft. (31:3) Easy
Answer: Spirit. God gives skill for His work through His Spirit.


Q3. Fill in the blank: God wrote the covenant on two tablets of ______ with His own finger. (31:18) Medium
Answer: stone. God's law is written by God Himself and it is lasting.


Q4. Multiple choice: The Sabbath is tied to which event in this passage? (31:17) Hard
A) The Exodus from Egypt   B) God's rest after creation   C) The giving of manna   D) The flood
Answer: B. God's rest after creation. Israel's rest echoes God's own rest after creation.


Q5. True or false: Anyone who did work on the Sabbath was to be put to death. (31:14-15) Medium
Answer: True. God takes His covenant and the holiness of rest seriously.


Q6. Fill in the blank: God's Spirit gave Bezalel skill in ______, in silver, in bronze and in other crafts. (31:4-5) Hard
Answer: gold. Skilled craft and worship belong together, and God gives the ability.


Q7. True or false: The Sabbath was to be a sign between God and Israel forever. (31:17) Easy
Answer: True. The Sabbath is a sign of God's covenant and of rest.


Q8. Multiple choice: Who was Bezalel's helper in the work of the tabernacle? (31:6) Easy
A) Aaron   B) Joshua   C) Oholiab   D) Caleb
Answer: C. Oholiab. God gives gifts and partners for the work of His house.


Exodus 32
Q1. True or false: The LORD relented from the disaster He had said He would bring on His people. (32:14) Medium
Answer: True. God's mercy answers intercession, and He is patient.


Q2. Multiple choice: What did Moses do when he came down and saw the calf? (32:19) Medium
A) He worshiped it   B) He ran away   C) He threw down the tablets, breaking them   D) He wrote a new law
Answer: C. He threw down the tablets, breaking them. God's holiness demands a response to sin.


Q3. True or false: Aaron declared, "Tomorrow shall be a feast to the LORD," and the people worshiped the calf. (32:5) Easy
Answer: True. Good-sounding words cannot make worship of an idol right.


Q4. Fill in the blank: Moses prayed to the LORD and asked Him not to let His wrath burn against His ______. (32:11) Medium
Answer: people. Moses stands between God and the people and pleads for them.


Q5. Multiple choice: Which group gathered to Moses after the calf was made? (32:26) Hard
A) Judah   B) Dan   C) The sons of Levi   D) Benjamin
Answer: C. The sons of Levi. Those who stand with the LORD are called to act for His holiness.


Q6. True or false: Moses asked God to blot him out of His book if He would not forgive Israel's sin. (32:32) Hard
Answer: True. Moses offers himself for the people, pointing toward a greater mediator.


Q7. Fill in the blank: The people said to Aaron, "Up, make us ______ who shall go before us." (32:1) Easy
Answer: gods. Impatience turned into idolatry when the people stopped trusting God's timing.


Q8. Multiple choice: What did Aaron make from the gold the people gave him? (32:4) Easy
A) A golden lampstand   B) A golden crown   C) A golden altar   D) A golden calf
Answer: D. A golden calf. Idolatry puts a made thing in the place of the living God.


Exodus 33
Q1. Multiple choice: Why did God hide Moses in the cleft of the rock? (33:22) Hard
A) So he could see God's glory and live   B) For rest   C) To hide from Pharaoh   D) To keep him out of the sun
Answer: A. So he could see God's glory and live. Grace protects sinners in the presence of a holy God.


Q2. Fill in the blank: The LORD told Moses to lead the people to a land flowing with milk and ______. (33:3) Easy
Answer: honey. God keeps His promise of the land even after Israel sinned.


Q3. Multiple choice: What did Moses ask God for, so that he could know His ways? (33:13) Medium
A) An army   B) Gold   C) To know God's ways and find favor in His sight   D) Land taken by force
Answer: C. To know God's ways and find favor in His sight. Moses asks for God Himself, not only for His gifts.


Q4. Fill in the blank: The LORD said, "You cannot see my face, for man shall not see me and ______." (33:20) Medium
Answer: live. God's holiness is too great for sinful people to see and live.


Q5. Multiple choice: Where did Moses pitch the tent of meeting, outside the camp? (33:7) Easy
A) In Pharaoh's palace   B) At the top of Sinai   C) Outside the camp   D) Far from the people
Answer: C. Outside the camp. God meets with those who seek Him.


Q6. True or false: God put Moses in a cleft of the rock as His glory passed by. (33:22) Medium
Answer: True. God protects His people in His presence.


Q7. True or false: God said He would send an angel ahead, but He would not go up among the people Himself. (33:3) Easy
Answer: True. Sin had put Israel in danger, and God's holy presence was the question that mattered most.


Q8. True or false: When Moses entered the tent of meeting, the people rose and worshiped at their tent doors. (33:10) Hard
Answer: True. The people honored God's presence and the role Moses carried.


Exodus 34
Q1. True or false: The LORD said He is a jealous God. (34:14) Hard
Answer: True. God is exclusive in love and worship and will not share His people.


Q2. True or false: God told Israel not to make a covenant with the people who lived in the land. (34:12) Medium
Answer: True. God's people must not join themselves to the worship of other gods.


Q3. Fill in the blank: The LORD told Moses to cut two tablets of stone like the first, and He would write the ______ on them. (34:1) Easy
Answer: words. God restores His covenant with His people after their failure.


Q4. Fill in the blank: Moses covered his face with a ______ because the skin of his face shone. (34:33) Medium
Answer: veil. The glory of God's presence is holy and must be handled with care.


Q5. Multiple choice: Which feast did God tell Israel to keep with unleavened bread for seven days? (34:18) Hard
A) Feast of Unleavened Bread   B) Feast of Booths   C) Pentecost   D) Feast of Trumpets
Answer: A. Feast of Unleavened Bread. Remembering the Exodus shapes Israel's worship.


Q6. Multiple choice: How did God describe Himself as He passed before Moses? (34:6) Easy
A) Angry and distant   B) Hidden in fire only   C) Merciful and gracious, slow to anger   D) Silent
Answer: C. Merciful and gracious, slow to anger. God's character is mercy and truth.


Q7. True or false: God said He would never forgive the guilty at all. (34:7) Easy
Answer: False. God keeps steadfast love for thousands, but He does not leave the guilty unpunished.


Q8. Multiple choice: What happened to Moses' face when he came down with the new tablets? (34:29-35) Medium
A) He hid in a cave   B) It shone with the glory of God   C) He slept for days   D) He threw the tablets down
Answer: B. It shone with the glory of God. Time with God changes people, and they reflect Him.


Exodus 35
Q1. Multiple choice: What did the people do after Moses called for offerings? (35:21) Easy
A) They refused   B) They came, every willing person, and brought offerings   C) They stayed home   D) They sent gold to Pharaoh
Answer: B. They came, every willing person, and brought offerings. Worship moves people to give generously.


Q2. True or false: The offerings brought by the people were to be used for the work of the tabernacle. (35:21) Medium
Answer: True. Gifts serve the worship and the work of the house of God.


Q3. Fill in the blank: Oholiab was a craftsman who worked with ______ and weaving. (35:35) Medium
Answer: embroidery. Each gift serves God's house.


Q4. Fill in the blank: Moses said, "Let everyone whose heart is ______ bring the LORD's offering." (35:5) Easy
Answer: willing. Gifts come from willing hearts, as God taught in chapter 25.


Q5. Fill in the blank: Those who were wise-hearted were to do all the work the LORD commanded in the ______ of the tabernacle. (35:10) Hard
Answer: building. Skilled work for God's house is holy service.


Q6. Multiple choice: What command about the Sabbath was repeated in chapter 35? (35:2) Hard
A) Do no work on the Sabbath   B) Travel on the Sabbath   C) Plant grain on the Sabbath   D) Fight wars on the Sabbath
Answer: A. Do no work on the Sabbath. The Sabbath was guarded even in the work of building worship.


Q7. Multiple choice: Which craftsman did God fill with wisdom and skill? (35:30-35) Medium
A) Joshua   B) Bezalel   C) Korah   D) Pharaoh
Answer: B. Bezalel. God gives skill for His work.


Q8. True or false: Bezalel and Oholiab were chosen by name for the work of the tabernacle. (35:30-34) Easy
Answer: True. God calls particular people by name for particular tasks.


Exodus 36
Q1. True or false: The curtain of the tent was made of goat hair. (36:14) Hard
Answer: True. Goat hair curtains protect the sanctuary.


Q2. Multiple choice: How were the curtains of the tabernacle joined together? (36:18) Medium
A) With gold pins   B) With bronze clasps   C) They were not joined   D) With ropes of goat hair
Answer: B. With bronze clasps. The tent was made to be a single whole.


Q3. True or false: The craftsmen made the tabernacle exactly as God had commanded Moses. (36:1) Easy
Answer: True. Obedience in the details honors God.


Q4. Fill in the blank: There were forty bases of ______ for the frames of the tabernacle. (36:24) Medium
Answer: silver. Costly materials show the worth of the house of God.


Q5. Fill in the blank: The people brought so many offerings that Moses had to ______ them from bringing more. (36:6) Easy
Answer: restrain. Generosity is a joyful response, not a burden.


Q6. Multiple choice: What was the purpose of the veil with cherubim worked into it? (36:35) Hard
A) To hang lamps   B) Decoration only   C) To cover the people   D) To separate the Holy Place from the Most Holy Place
Answer: D. To separate the Holy Place from the Most Holy Place. The barrier shows that God's holiness is set apart.


Q7. Multiple choice: How many curtains were made for the tabernacle? (36:8) Easy
A) Seven   B) Twelve   C) Ten   D) Four
Answer: C. Ten. The tabernacle is built as God commanded.


Q8. True or false: The boards of the tabernacle were made of acacia wood and set upright. (36:20) Medium
Answer: True. The structure is sturdy and set upright.


Exodus 37
Q1. Fill in the blank: Bezalel made the ark of acacia wood and overlaid it inside and outside with pure ______. (37:2) Easy
Answer: gold. The ark, the place of God's covenant, was covered in pure gold.


Q2. Multiple choice: How many branches came out of the lampstand? (37:18) Medium
A) Four   B) Seven   C) Six   D) Twelve
Answer: C. Six. The light in God's house points to Him as its source.


Q3. Multiple choice: What were the poles used to carry the ark made from? (37:4-5) Hard
A) Acacia wood overlaid with gold   B) Stone   C) Bronze   D) Cedar
Answer: A. Acacia wood overlaid with gold. The ark is carried by poles, so no one touches the holy thing.


Q4. Multiple choice: What was on the ends of the mercy seat that covered the ark? (37:7-9) Easy
A) Two stone tablets   B) A golden lion   C) A cup of oil   D) Two cherubim of hammered gold
Answer: D. Two cherubim of hammered gold. Cherubim guard the holy place, and mercy rests on the ark.


Q5. True or false: The lampstand was made of pure hammered gold. (37:17) Medium
Answer: True. Light and holiness are shaped by careful craftsmanship in gold.


Q6. True or false: The incense was prepared by a perfumer as holy incense set apart for God. (37:29) Hard
Answer: True. What is set apart for God is prepared with care.


Q7. True or false: The table of the bread was made of acacia wood and overlaid with gold. (37:10-11) Easy
Answer: True. Holy furnishings are made with honor and care.


Q8. Fill in the blank: Bezalel made the altar of incense of acacia wood and overlaid it with pure ______. (37:25) Medium
Answer: gold. Prayer is holy and set apart before the LORD.


Exodus 38
Q1. Fill in the blank: The pegs of the tabernacle and of the courtyard were made of ______. (38:20) Medium
Answer: bronze. The materials of God's house are durable and set firmly in place.


Q2. True or false: The hangings of the courtyard were made of fine twined linen. (38:9) Easy
Answer: True. The courtyard reflects holiness at the edges of God's presence.


Q3. Multiple choice: Who kept the accounts of the work of the tabernacle? (38:21) Easy
A) Caleb   B) Pharaoh   C) Ithamar, son of Aaron   D) Joshua
Answer: C. Ithamar, son of Aaron. The work of God's house is recorded faithfully and openly.


Q4. True or false: The people's gifts of gold, silver and bronze were counted by weight. (38:24-31) Medium
Answer: True. God's work is accounted for carefully, and every gift is honored.


Q5. Multiple choice: What were the posts of the courtyard set in? (38:10-11) Medium
A) Clay   B) Bronze bases   C) Stone   D) Wood
Answer: B. Bronze bases. The courtyard is sturdy and secure around God's presence.


Q6. Fill in the blank: The bronze offering was 70 talents and 2,400 ______. (38:29) Hard
Answer: shekels. The bronze was given freely to build God's house.


Q7. Fill in the blank: The bronze basin was made from the mirrors of the women who ______ at the entrance of the tent of meeting. (38:8) Easy
Answer: served. Even personal gifts are given for God's work.


Q8. Multiple choice: What was the source of the silver for the bases of the sanctuary? (38:25-27) Hard
A) Traders from Egypt   B) The spoils of war   C) Pharaoh's tribute   D) The half shekel census money
Answer: D. The half shekel census money. Atonement money given by every person built the sanctuary.


Exodus 39
Q1. Multiple choice: Why was the breastpiece tied to the ephod? (39:21) Hard
A) For ornament only   B) So it would not slip and would stay in its place   C) To hold gold   D) To keep the priest warm
Answer: B. So it would not slip and would stay in its place. The garments were fixed firmly so the priest's service would not be disordered.


Q2. Multiple choice: How were the twelve stones arranged in the breastpiece? (39:10-13) Easy
A) Six in two rows   B) Twelve in four rows   C) Forty in one row   D) Seven in one row
Answer: B. Twelve in four rows. The twelve tribes stand before the LORD.


Q3. Multiple choice: What was on the hem of the robe of the ephod? (39:24-26) Medium
A) Lions   B) Stars   C) Pomegranates and bells of gold   D) Nothing
Answer: C. Pomegranates and bells of gold. Sound and beauty were part of the priest's service, as God commanded.


Q4. Fill in the blank: The breastpiece was made in the same way as the ______. (39:8) Medium
Answer: ephod. The priest's garments form one consistent whole, matched to God's pattern.


Q5. True or false: The breastpiece was made square, doubled, a span in length and a span in width. (39:9) Medium
Answer: True. The priest's breastpiece is careful and exact.


Q6. True or false: Moses inspected the work and blessed the people because it was done as the LORD commanded. (39:43) Easy
Answer: True. Faithful work done as God commanded is blessed.


Q7. Fill in the blank: They made the plate of pure gold and wrote on it, "Holiness to the ______." (39:30) Easy
Answer: LORD. Holiness belongs to God alone.


Q8. Fill in the blank: The robe of the ephod was made entirely of ______ yarn. (39:22) Hard
Answer: blue. Holy garments reflect the holiness of God in color and in design.


Exodus 40
Q1. True or false: Moses could not enter the tent of meeting when the cloud settled on it. (40:35) Easy
Answer: True. God's presence was so full that even Moses could not enter.


Q2. True or false: Moses set up the courtyard and its gate, and he completed the work. (40:33) Medium
Answer: True. The work was finished as God directed.


Q3. Multiple choice: What did Moses do with the altar of burnt offering? (40:6) Hard
A) He melted it   B) He placed it before the tent of meeting   C) He gave it to Pharaoh   D) He buried it
Answer: B. He placed it before the tent of meeting. Each part of the tabernacle was placed in its right position before God.


Q4. Fill in the blank: Moses set the table in the tent of meeting and placed the bread of the ______ before the LORD. (40:23) Hard
Answer: Presence. God's people stand before Him continually.


Q5. Multiple choice: When did the Israelites set out on their journey? (40:36-37) Medium
A) After seven years   B) At night   C) When the cloud was lifted from over the tabernacle   D) At Moses' command alone
Answer: C. When the cloud was lifted from over the tabernacle. Israel follows God's guidance in every journey.


Q6. Fill in the blank: Moses anointed the tabernacle and everything in it, and so made it ______. (40:9) Medium
Answer: holy. Things set apart for God's use are made holy by His command.


Q7. Fill in the blank: The LORD told Moses to set up the tabernacle on the first day of the ______ month. (40:2) Easy
Answer: first. God's house is set up at God's appointed time.


Q8. Multiple choice: What filled the tabernacle when Moses finished the work? (40:34) Easy
A) The glory of the LORD   B) Gold dust   C) Water   D) Smoke from Egypt
Answer: A. The glory of the LORD. The LORD's glory filled the house He commanded to be built.


Leviticus
Leviticus 1
Q1. True or false: The burnt offering was to be a male animal without defect. (1:3) Easy
Answer: True. God requires the best, without blemish.


Q2. Multiple choice: What did the offerer do with his hand before the burnt offering was accepted? (1:4) Easy
A) He washed it in oil   B) He laid it on the head of the animal   C) He held it up to the sky   D) He hid it in his robe
Answer: B. He laid it on the head of the animal. Laying on the hand shows the offerer's identification with the offering.


Q3. Fill in the blank: The priests were to ______ the blood against the sides of the altar. (1:5) Medium
Answer: throw. The blood of atonement is central to worship.


Q4. Multiple choice: Why were the entrails and legs of the burnt offering to be washed? (1:9) Hard
A) For use as fuel   B) So the whole offering is clean and holy before God   C) To be eaten by the priests   D) For decoration
Answer: B. So the whole offering is clean and holy before God. Holiness requires clean and whole offerings.


Q5. Fill in the blank: A burnt offering is a pleasing ______ to the LORD, an offering made by fire. (1:9) Easy
Answer: aroma. The whole offering rises to God and shows full devotion.


Q6. True or false: If the offering was birds, the priest was to wring off the head of the bird. (1:14-15) Medium
Answer: True. Even the poor could bring an acceptable offering.


Q7. True or false: The burnt offering was burned entirely on the altar, with nothing kept back. (1:9) Hard
Answer: True. Complete devotion is offered to God.


Q8. Multiple choice: What did the offerer do with the animal before it was offered? (1:5) Medium
A) He sold it   B) He hid it   C) He killed it before the LORD   D) He released it
Answer: C. He killed it before the LORD. Coming to God means taking responsibility before Him.


Leviticus 2
Q1. Multiple choice: What was the priest's share of the grain offering? (2:3, 10) Medium
A) Nothing   B) A portion that is holy and most holy   C) Only the oil   D) All of it
Answer: B. A portion that is holy and most holy. Priests share in the holy portions of God's table.


Q2. Multiple choice: What was to be included in every grain offering? (2:13) Easy
A) Honey   B) Salt   C) Leaven   D) Sugar
Answer: B. Salt. The salt of the covenant was never to be missing from God's offerings.


Q3. Fill in the blank: The grain offering baked in the oven was made of ______ flour mixed with oil. (2:4) Medium
Answer: fine. Offerings are prepared with careful work.


Q4. True or false: The grain offering of firstfruits was offered with oil and frankincense laid on it. (2:15) Hard
Answer: True. Fragrance and oil show honor offered to God.


Q5. Multiple choice: Which statement best summarizes the grain offering? (2:1-16) Hard
A) Thanks and devotion with the fruit of human labor   B) A replacement for blood   C) A feast for the dead   D) A payment for sin
Answer: A. Thanks and devotion with the fruit of human labor. Daily labor belongs to God.


Q6. Fill in the blank: A grain offering was made of fine flour mixed with ______ and frankincense. (2:1) Easy
Answer: oil. Thanksgiving and devotion are offered from daily bread and labor.


Q7. True or false: A grain offering of firstfruits could be new grain roasted in the fire and crushed. (2:14-16) Medium
Answer: True. Gratitude to God for the first harvest is part of worship.


Q8. True or false: Leaven and honey were to be burned on the altar as a food offering to the LORD. (2:11-12) Easy
Answer: False. Leaven and honey were not to be offered by fire to the LORD.


Leviticus 3
Q1. True or false: Blood and fat were not to be eaten at any time by the people. (3:17) Easy
Answer: True. Life belongs to God, so blood is not eaten.


Q2. Multiple choice: What is the rule about eating fat and blood? (3:16-17) Medium
A) Only priests may eat blood   B) Fat belongs to the LORD, and blood must not be eaten   C) They may eat both   D) Blood is for sale
Answer: B. Fat belongs to the LORD, and blood must not be eaten. Life and the best portions belong to God.


Q3. Multiple choice: Which part of the sheep was offered whole with the fat? (3:9) Hard
A) The horns   B) The hooves   C) The fat tail   D) The wool
Answer: C. The fat tail. God receives the best portions of the offering.


Q4. True or false: The peace offering was made from animals of the herd or the flock. (3:1) Medium
Answer: True. Fellowship offerings come from God's provision in the flock and herd.


Q5. True or false: The fat and blood rule was to be kept as a statute forever throughout the generations. (3:17) Hard
Answer: True. The law of worship is lasting, not temporary.


Q6. Fill in the blank: The peace offering could come from the herd or the flock, and it had to be ______. (3:1) Easy
Answer: without defect. God is honored with the best.


Q7. Fill in the blank: Whether the offering was a lamb or a goat, it was brought before the ______ of the tent of meeting. (3:7) Medium
Answer: door. Worship is brought to the place where God meets His people.


Q8. Multiple choice: Which fat was to be burned on the altar in the peace offering? (3:3-4) Easy
A) The skin   B) The bones   C) The fat around the entrails and the kidneys   D) The meat
Answer: C. The fat around the entrails and the kidneys. The fat belongs to the LORD.


Leviticus 4
Q1. Multiple choice: Who was to lay a hand on the head of the goat in the sin offering of a leader? (4:24) Easy
A) Pharaoh   B) The leader himself   C) The king's son   D) Moses only
Answer: B. The leader himself. The one who sins brings the offering.


Q2. True or false: The fat of the sin offering was burned on the altar, as with the peace offering. (4:8-10) Medium
Answer: True. The LORD receives His portion of every sacrifice.


Q3. True or false: Sin offerings were made for sins committed unintentionally. (4:2) Easy
Answer: True. God makes provision even for sins committed without knowledge.


Q4. True or false: The sin offering for the whole congregation included a young bull. (4:13-14) Hard
Answer: True. Corporate sin brings corporate guilt and a need for atonement.


Q5. Fill in the blank: The priest shall make ______ for them, and they shall be forgiven. (4:20) Medium
Answer: atonement. Forgiveness comes through the offering God appointed.


Q6. Multiple choice: Who was to bring a goat for a sin offering if a common person sinned? (4:27-28) Hard
A) The priest only   B) The common person   C) Pharaoh   D) The king's guard
Answer: B. The common person. Each person is responsible for their own sin and its offering.


Q7. Multiple choice: Where was the blood of the priest's sin offering placed? (4:6-7) Medium
A) On the ground   B) Seven times before the LORD, on the horns of the altar of incense   C) On the people   D) In the river
Answer: B. Seven times before the LORD, on the horns of the altar of incense. Atonement is made before the LORD's presence.


Q8. Fill in the blank: If a priest sinned unintentionally, he was to bring a young bull without ______. (4:3) Easy
Answer: defect. Sin brings guilt on the people, so the offering must be pure.


Leviticus 5
Q1. Multiple choice: What must a person do when he is guilty of sin? (5:5) Medium
A) Confess the sin   B) Hide it   C) Blame others   D) Sell property
Answer: A. Confess the sin. Confession comes before restoration.


Q2. Fill in the blank: If a person unintentionally breaks faith with the LORD in the ______ things, he shall bring a guilt offering. (5:15) Medium
Answer: holy. Holy things must be honored and, when wronged, repaired.


Q3. Multiple choice: What was to be brought when a person could not afford a lamb? (5:7) Easy
A) Gold   B) Nothing   C) Two turtledoves or two pigeons   D) A bull
Answer: C. Two turtledoves or two pigeons. God makes worship possible for the poor.


Q4. True or false: The guilt offering required the person to add a fifth to the value of what was wronged. (5:16) Medium
Answer: True. God's justice calls for repayment as well as honest acknowledgment.


Q5. Fill in the blank: A person who was called to testify but kept silent bears ______ for it. (5:1) Easy
Answer: guilt. Silence in the face of wrong carries responsibility.


Q6. True or false: A person who touched something unclean and did not know it was still guilty and had to bring an offering. (5:2) Easy
Answer: True. Uncleanness and guilt are taken seriously before God.


Q7. Multiple choice: What was the guilt offering for? (5:14-16) Hard
A) Restoring what was wronged and making atonement   B) Celebrating the harvest   C) Feasting   D) Dedicating a house
Answer: A. Restoring what was wronged and making atonement. Restitution and atonement go together.


Q8. True or false: The priest was to make atonement for the unintentional sin, and the person would be forgiven. (5:18) Hard
Answer: True. Forgiveness comes through the provision God appointed.


Leviticus 6
Q1. True or false: The priests were to eat the grain offering in a holy place within the courtyard of the tent of meeting. (6:16) Easy
Answer: True. Priests share in the holy portions God provides.


Q2. Multiple choice: What was to be done with a sin offering whose blood was brought into the tent of meeting? (6:30) Medium
A) It was kept   B) It was given to the poor   C) It was burned with fire and not eaten   D) It was sold
Answer: C. It was burned with fire and not eaten. Some offerings are holy beyond ordinary sharing.


Q3. Fill in the blank: The sin offering was to be eaten in the holy place of the ______ of the tent of meeting. (6:26) Medium
Answer: courtyard. Holiness governs who eats what and where.


Q4. Multiple choice: What were the priests to do with the ashes from the burnt offering? (6:10-11) Easy
A) Keep them as treasure   B) Burn them again in a lamp   C) Sprinkle them on people   D) Carry them outside the camp to a clean place
Answer: D. Carry them outside the camp to a clean place. What is holy is handled with order.


Q5. True or false: Every male among the descendants of Aaron may eat the grain offering. (6:18) Medium
Answer: True. Holy portions go to the priests who serve God.


Q6. Multiple choice: What was the priest's offering every day, on the day he was anointed? (6:20) Hard
A) A bull   B) Oil only   C) Incense   D) A tenth of an ephah of fine flour, half in the morning and half in the evening
Answer: D. A tenth of an ephah of fine flour, half in the morning and half in the evening. Daily faithfulness is part of worship.


Q7. Fill in the blank: The priest was to put on his linen robe and linen ______. (6:10) Easy
Answer: undergarments. Priests approach God's holy work dressed in holiness.


Q8. True or false: The fire on the altar was to burn continually and never go out. (6:13) Hard
Answer: True. God's worship is meant to burn continually in His house.


Leviticus 7
Q1. True or false: Anyone who ate blood was to be cut off from his people. (7:27) Easy
Answer: True. Blood belongs to God and is not to be eaten.


Q2. True or false: The priest who offers a burnt offering may keep its skin. (7:8) Hard
Answer: True. God's law provides for the priest's service.


Q3. Fill in the blank: The guilt offering was to be ______ in the holy place. (7:6) Easy
Answer: eaten. The holy portion is eaten by the priests in God's presence.


Q4. Multiple choice: Who was to eat the flesh of a thank offering from the peace offerings? (7:15) Easy
A) The one who brought it, on the day it was offered   B) The animals   C) No one   D) Only the priests, a year later
Answer: A. The one who brought it, on the day it was offered. Fellowship meals celebrate God's goodness.


Q5. Multiple choice: What was a wave offering? (7:30) Hard
A) Offered by fire only   B) Offered by pouring water   C) Offered by throwing into a river   D) Offered by lifting it up before the LORD
Answer: D. Offered by lifting it up before the LORD. Offerings lifted before God are presented to Him.


Q6. True or false: The fat of an animal that died on its own could be eaten by Israelites. (7:24) Medium
Answer: False. Holy boundaries apply to what God gives.


Q7. Multiple choice: What is the priest's share of the peace offering? (7:31-34) Medium
A) The head   B) The breast and the right thigh   C) The skin   D) The bones
Answer: B. The breast and the right thigh. God provides for those who serve Him.


Q8. Fill in the blank: A thank offering was to be eaten on the day it was offered, and none was to be left until the ______. (7:15) Medium
Answer: morning. Thanksgiving is received and remembered fresh.


Leviticus 8
Q1. Multiple choice: What did Moses put on the tip of Aaron's right ear, right thumb and right big toe? (8:23) Medium
A) Incense   B) Honey   C) Gold   D) Blood of the ram of ordination
Answer: D. Blood of the ram of ordination. The whole life of the priest is set apart for God.


Q2. Fill in the blank: Aaron and his sons were to remain at the entrance of the tent of meeting for ______ days. (8:33) Medium
Answer: seven. Consecration takes time and faithful waiting before God.


Q3. True or false: Moses anointed the tabernacle and everything in it with oil to make it holy. (8:10-11) Easy
Answer: True. Holiness is God's gift and His command.


Q4. Fill in the blank: Moses took Aaron and his sons, their garments, the anointing oil, a bull, two rams and a basket of ______ bread. (8:2) Easy
Answer: unleavened. Holy service is prepared with exact obedience.


Q5. Multiple choice: What did the priests do with the bread and meat of the ram of ordination? (8:31) Hard
A) Burned them   B) Gave them to Pharaoh   C) Ate them in a holy place at the entrance of the tent of meeting   D) Buried them
Answer: C. Ate them in a holy place at the entrance of the tent of meeting. The priests eat the holy portion as God commanded.


Q6. True or false: Moses did everything the LORD commanded through him. (8:36) Hard
Answer: True. Obedience is the mark of faithful service.


Q7. Multiple choice: Who did Moses wash before they were clothed as priests? (8:6) Easy
A) Pharaoh   B) Aaron and his sons   C) The Levites only   D) The elders
Answer: B. Aaron and his sons. Priests must be washed clean before they serve.


Q8. True or false: The priests were to stay at the tent of meeting day and night for seven days and keep the LORD's charge. (8:35) Medium
Answer: True. Faithfulness in God's service is required.


Leviticus 9
Q1. Multiple choice: What did fire from the LORD do after Aaron lifted his hands and blessed the people? (9:24) Medium
A) It burned the camp   B) It consumed the offering on the altar   C) It went out   D) It warmed the tent
Answer: B. It consumed the offering on the altar. God accepts sacrifice offered in obedience.


Q2. True or false: Moses and Aaron went into the tent of meeting, came out and blessed the people. (9:23) Medium
Answer: True. Leaders serve God by blessing the people.


Q3. Fill in the blank: On the eighth day Moses called Aaron, his sons and the elders of ______. (9:1) Easy
Answer: Israel. Worship is led in the presence of the community.


Q4. Multiple choice: Who was to bring the offering for the people after the offering for himself? (9:15) Hard
A) Pharaoh   B) Aaron offered the people's sin offering   C) Moses only   D) Joshua
Answer: B. Aaron offered the people's sin offering. Priests bring the people's sins before God after dealing with their own.


Q5. Multiple choice: What did Aaron do first when he began the sacrifices? (9:8) Easy
A) He offered nothing   B) He offered a calf for his own sin   C) He offered gold   D) He fled
Answer: B. He offered a calf for his own sin. Priests deal with their own sin before serving others.


Q6. True or false: Aaron lifted his hands toward the people and offered them as a wave offering before the LORD. (9:21) Hard
Answer: True. Offerings are presented before God by the priest.


Q7. Fill in the blank: The people shouted and fell on their ______ when they saw the fire from the LORD. (9:24) Medium
Answer: faces. Awe before God's glory is the right response.


Q8. True or false: The glory of the LORD appeared to all the people after the offerings were made. (9:23) Easy
Answer: True. God shows His presence when His people worship in His way.


Leviticus 10
Q1. Multiple choice: Why could Aaron and his sons not mourn openly for Nadab and Abihu? (10:6-7) Medium
A) They were in exile   B) They had no feelings   C) The anointing oil was on them, and they had to stay at the tent of meeting   D) They were forbidden to speak
Answer: C. The anointing oil was on them, and they had to stay at the tent of meeting. Service before the LORD comes first.


Q2. True or false: God said priests must distinguish between the holy and the common, and between the clean and the unclean. (10:10) Medium
Answer: True. Holy living requires careful discernment.


Q3. True or false: God said, "Among those who are near me I will be shown holy." (10:3) Easy
Answer: True. God is honored by those who draw near in His way.


Q4. Multiple choice: What happened to Nadab and Abihu? (10:2) Easy
A) Fire came out from the LORD and consumed them   B) They became kings   C) They were forgiven at once   D) They were exiled
Answer: A. Fire came out from the LORD and consumed them. God's holiness is not to be treated lightly.


Q5. Multiple choice: Why did Moses become angry with Eleazar and Ithamar? (10:16-18) Hard
A) They worshiped idols   B) They refused to work   C) They were drunk   D) They did not eat the sin offering in the holy place as commanded
Answer: D. They did not eat the sin offering in the holy place as commanded. Following God's exact instructions matters, even in grief.


Q6. True or false: Aaron was silent when this happened. (10:3) Hard
Answer: True. Aaron accepted God's judgment in silence.


Q7. Fill in the blank: Aaron and his sons were not to drink wine or strong drink when they entered the tent of meeting, so that they would not ______. (10:9) Medium
Answer: die. Clear minds are needed for holy service.


Q8. Fill in the blank: Nadab and Abihu offered ______ fire before the LORD, which He had not commanded. (10:1) Easy
Answer: strange. God must be approached in the way He has commanded.


Leviticus 11
Q1. Fill in the blank: Animals that chew the cud and have a divided hoof are ______. (11:3) Easy
Answer: clean. God marks boundaries that set His people apart.


Q2. Fill in the blank: The LORD said, "You shall be holy, for I am ______." (11:44) Medium
Answer: holy. God's holiness is the reason for His people's holiness.


Q3. True or false: Anything on which an unclean carcass falls became unclean. (11:32) Hard
Answer: True. Uncleanness can spread, so God's people guard their lives.


Q4. True or false: Creeping things that crawl on the ground were unclean. (11:41-43) Medium
Answer: True. Holiness extends to all of life.


Q5. Multiple choice: Which of these animals is unclean? (11:4) Easy
A) The camel   B) The goat   C) The sheep   D) The ox
Answer: A. The camel. God's distinctions teach Israel to be set apart for Him.


Q6. True or false: Fish with fins and scales may be eaten. (11:9) Easy
Answer: True. God's law gives clear guidance for daily life.


Q7. Multiple choice: What is the central reason given for the laws in Leviticus 11? (11:44-45) Hard
A) Diet improves health only   B) It was Pharaoh's rule   C) God is holy and brought Israel up out of Egypt   D) Animals are sacred
Answer: C. God is holy and brought Israel up out of Egypt. Israel's holiness reflects God's holiness and His deliverance.


Q8. Multiple choice: What must someone do after carrying the carcass of one of the animals described here? (11:24-25) Medium
A) Do nothing   B) Become a priest   C) Wash their clothes and remain unclean until evening   D) Pay the king
Answer: C. Wash their clothes and remain unclean until evening. Carrying the carcass requires washing clothes and waiting until evening. Ritual uncleanness is not a claim that a person has less dignity.


Leviticus 12
Q1. Multiple choice: What did a poor mother offer instead of a lamb? (12:8) Medium
A) A bull   B) Nothing   C) Gold   D) Two turtledoves or two pigeons
Answer: D. Two turtledoves or two pigeons. God provides for the poor in worship.


Q2. True or false: When the days of purification were over, she brought the offering to the priest at the entrance of the tent of meeting. (12:6) Medium
Answer: True. The woman returns to the community of worship.


Q3. True or false: Leviticus 12 gives different periods of purification for a son and for a daughter. (12:2-5) Hard
Answer: True. God's law gives weight to every part of human life.


Q4. Multiple choice: How long was the mother's purification after the birth of a daughter? (12:5) Easy
A) Seven days   B) No time at all   C) Fourteen days, then sixty-six more days   D) A year
Answer: C. Fourteen days, then sixty-six more days. Purification is a time of separation and restoration.


Q5. True or false: After the purification period, a mother who could afford it brought a lamb and a bird. (12:6-8) Easy
Answer: True. The lamb was a burnt offering and the bird a purification or sin offering. The law also provides a less costly offering for a poor mother.


Q6. Fill in the blank: After a woman gave birth to a boy, she was unclean for ______ days. (12:2) Easy
Answer: seven. God cares for the ordinary, bodily life of His people.


Q7. Fill in the blank: The priest was to make ______ for the woman, and she would be clean. (12:7) Medium
Answer: atonement. Even the joys of life are covered by God's provision.


Q8. Multiple choice: What is the stated result of the priest's offering after childbirth? (12:6-8) Hard
A) A declaration that daughters lack worth   B) Atonement and cleansing from the flow of blood   C) Payment to the king   D) Punishment for having a child
Answer: B. Atonement and cleansing from the flow of blood. The passage concerns ritual purification. It does not call childbirth itself a moral sin or assign less human worth to a mother or daughter.


Leviticus 13
Q1. Fill in the blank: If the hair in a sore has turned white and the sore goes deeper than the skin, the priest shall pronounce the person ______. (13:3) Easy
Answer: unclean. The priest judges by careful examination, not by appearance alone.


Q2. Multiple choice: What rule applied to a person declared unclean by a skin disease? (13:45-46) Medium
A) He stayed in the camp as usual   B) He was executed at once   C) He became a priest   D) He lived outside the camp and called out, Unclean!
Answer: D. He lived outside the camp and called out, Unclean!. Uncleanness was contained to protect others and the holy camp.


Q3. True or false: The priest also examined garments for a mildew-like spot. (13:47) Medium
Answer: True. God's concern for holiness reached even clothing.


Q4. True or false: If the sore had not spread after the first seven days, the priest could shut the person up for seven more days. (13:5) Easy
Answer: True. God's law allows time for careful observation.


Q5. Multiple choice: What was the main purpose of the laws in Leviticus 13? (13:59) Hard
A) To punish sin with disease   B) To choose the king   C) To judge clean and unclean so the camp was protected and holy   D) To test people's wealth
Answer: C. To judge clean and unclean so the camp was protected and holy. God's law protects both the community and the holiness of worship.


Q6. True or false: A person's uncleanness from a skin disease was judged only by how the person felt. (13:2-3) Hard
Answer: False. The priest examined visible signs with careful judgment, not personal feeling.


Q7. Fill in the blank: The priest examined the person again on the ______ day. (13:6) Medium
Answer: seventh. Patient observation was part of God's law.


Q8. Multiple choice: What did the priest do with a sore that was not yet clearly defined? (13:4) Easy
A) Gave him a feast   B) Sent him to the king   C) Shut the person up for seven days   D) Burned his house
Answer: C. Shut the person up for seven days. Careful waiting protects the community.


Leviticus 14
Q1. Multiple choice: What was dipped into the blood of the slaughtered bird before the sprinkling? (14:6-7) Easy
A) Bread   B) The living bird, with cedar, scarlet yarn and hyssop   C) Oil   D) Gold
Answer: B. The living bird, with cedar, scarlet yarn and hyssop. Life and death are both part of restoration.


Q2. Fill in the blank: On the eighth day the cleansed person brought his offerings to the ______ at the entrance of the tent of meeting. (14:11) Medium
Answer: priest. Restoration to worship is confirmed before the LORD.


Q3. Multiple choice: Where did the priest put some of the blood of the guilt offering on the person being cleansed? (14:14) Hard
A) In his mouth   B) On the ground   C) On the bread   D) Right ear, right thumb and right big toe
Answer: D. Right ear, right thumb and right big toe. Consecration reached the whole person, from head to foot.


Q4. True or false: The priest sprinkled some of the oil with his finger seven times before the LORD. (14:16) Hard
Answer: True. Oil and blood were both applied as a sign of consecration.


Q5. True or false: The person being cleansed shaved all his hair, washed his clothes and bathed. (14:8-9) Easy
Answer: True. Cleansing involved visible, ordered steps of renewal.


Q6. Multiple choice: Which offering remained required when a poor person used birds in the cleansing ceremony? (14:21-22) Medium
A) One male lamb for a guilt offering, with flour and oil   B) A bull   C) A golden calf   D) No other offering
Answer: A. One male lamb for a guilt offering, with flour and oil. The reduced provision still includes a lamb, flour and oil as well as two birds. Saying the poor person brought only birds leaves out part of the requirement.


Q7. Fill in the blank: For the cleansing of a healed skin disease, the priest was to take two live, clean ______. (14:4) Easy
Answer: birds. Cleansing involved restored life.


Q8. True or false: If mildew returned to a house after it was scraped, the priest could have the house torn down. (14:43-45) Medium
Answer: True. The law took the health of homes seriously.


Leviticus 15
Q1. Fill in the blank: A man with the discharge described in this law was declared ritually ______. (15:2-3) Easy
Answer: unclean. This is a ritual classification; verse 13 prescribes further waiting and washing after the discharge has stopped.


Q2. True or false: God said these laws keep Israel separate from uncleanness so they do not die in it. (15:31) Hard
Answer: True. The laws aimed to protect life and God's dwelling among His people.


Q3. Multiple choice: How long was a man unclean after his discharge had stopped? (15:13) Easy
A) Seven days   B) Forever   C) A year   D) One day
Answer: A. Seven days. Cleansing required a period of waiting and washing.


Q4. True or false: A woman with a prolonged discharge was unclean for as long as the discharge lasted. (15:25) Medium
Answer: True. The rule for prolonged cases lasted as long as the condition did.


Q5. Fill in the blank: The regular menstrual period described here brought ritual uncleanness for ______ days. (15:19) Medium
Answer: seven. This temporary ritual status does not say that menstruation is a moral wrongdoing or that women have less worth.


Q6. True or false: Anyone who touched the bed of a man with a discharge had to wash his clothes and bathe. (15:5) Easy
Answer: True. Uncleanness passed by contact and required washing.


Q7. Multiple choice: What offering did a man bring after his cleansing from a discharge? (15:14-15) Medium
A) A bull only   B) Silver   C) Two doves or two pigeons   D) Nothing
Answer: C. Two doves or two pigeons. Cleansing led to offering before the LORD.


Q8. Multiple choice: What does Leviticus 15 teach about God's concern for holiness? (15:31) Hard
A) Holiness covers ordinary bodily life, not only the altar   B) Holiness is only for priests   C) There are no rules for bodily life   D) Holiness depends on wealth
Answer: A. Holiness covers ordinary bodily life, not only the altar. God's holiness reaches into everyday life.


Leviticus 16
Q1. True or false: The Day of Atonement was to be a permanent statute and a day of solemn rest for self-denial. (16:29-31) Hard
Answer: True. The day called people to humble themselves and rest before God.


Q2. True or false: The high priest was to bathe his body in water before and after the work of the day. (16:4, 16:24) Medium
Answer: True. Ritual washing marked the high priest's holy work.


Q3. True or false: Aaron was to cast lots over two goats, one for the LORD and one for the scapegoat. (16:8) Easy
Answer: True. God's choice guided the sacrifice, not human preference.


Q4. Fill in the blank: Aaron was to put his hands on the head of the live goat and confess the sins of the ______. (16:21) Medium
Answer: Israelites. Confession and substitution went together.


Q5. Multiple choice: How often was the annual Day of Atonement ceremony to be performed for Israel? (16:29-34) Easy
A) Every week   B) Never   C) Once a year   D) Every day
Answer: C. Once a year. The annual ceremony includes the high priest's prescribed entry behind the curtain. The chapter regulates how he approaches God's holy presence.


Q6. Multiple choice: What did the high priest do with the blood of the bull and the goat? (16:14-15) Hard
A) Poured it on the ground outside the camp   B) Sprinkled it on the mercy seat and before it   C) Drank it   D) Put it on the scapegoat
Answer: B. Sprinkled it on the mercy seat and before it. Blood made atonement for the holy place.


Q7. Fill in the blank: On the Day of Atonement, Aaron was to offer a young bull as a ______ offering for himself. (16:11) Easy
Answer: sin. Even the high priest needed atonement before serving.


Q8. Multiple choice: What was done with the goat sent into the wilderness? (16:21-22) Medium
A) It was sold   B) It was made king   C) It carried the people's sins away from the camp   D) It was eaten by the priests
Answer: C. It carried the people's sins away from the camp. Sin was carried away from the camp.


Leviticus 17
Q1. True or false: The LORD told Israel they could no longer offer sacrifices in the open field. (17:5) Medium
Answer: True. Sacrifice was to be brought to the LORD at the place He appointed.


Q2. True or false: Israelites could offer sacrifices wherever they chose in the open country. (17:3-5) Hard
Answer: False. God set one place for worship, so it was not a matter of personal choice.


Q3. Fill in the blank: The LORD said that the life of the flesh is in the ______. (17:11) Easy
Answer: blood. God gave blood on the altar to make atonement for life.


Q4. True or false: Any ox, lamb or goat killed in the camp had to be brought to the entrance of the tent of meeting. (17:3-4) Easy
Answer: True. Worship and daily life were kept under God's oversight.


Q5. Multiple choice: Why was Israel forbidden to eat blood? (17:11) Easy
A) Blood belonged to God as a means of atonement   B) Blood was sold to the king   C) Blood was poisonous   D) Blood made animals holy
Answer: A. Blood belonged to God as a means of atonement. Life belongs to God and is honored in how His people eat.


Q6. Multiple choice: Why did God command these rules in Leviticus 17? (17:11, 17:14) Hard
A) Because meat was expensive   B) Because the nations asked for them   C) To punish the poor   D) Because life is in the blood and belongs to God
Answer: D. Because life is in the blood and belongs to God. The sacredness of life shapes the way God's people eat.


Q7. Multiple choice: What had to happen to someone who ate an animal that died on its own or was torn by beasts? (17:15) Medium
A) He was executed at once   B) He became a priest   C) Nothing happened   D) He washed his clothes, bathed and was unclean until evening
Answer: D. He washed his clothes, bathed and was unclean until evening. Uncleanness was dealt with by washing and waiting.


Q8. Fill in the blank: Whoever eats blood, the LORD said He would set His face against that person and cut him off from his ______. (17:10) Medium
Answer: people. Sin against God's gift affects one's place among God's people.


Leviticus 18
Q1. Multiple choice: Why did God say the land spewed out the nations? (18:24-28) Hard
A) There was a famine   B) They defiled it with detestable practices   C) The land was too small   D) There was a war only
Answer: B. They defiled it with detestable practices. Sin has consequences for the whole land.


Q2. Multiple choice: What reason did God give for these laws? (18:3-5) Easy
A) Egypt was defeated   B) The priests wrote them   C) The king demanded it   D) I am the LORD your God
Answer: D. I am the LORD your God. The LORD's identity is the foundation of Israel's way of life.


Q3. True or false: God warned that Israel would be spewed out of the land too if they defiled it the same way. (18:28) Hard
Answer: True. God's warning applies to His own people too.


Q4. Fill in the blank: You shall not ______ the name of your God. (18:21) Medium
Answer: profane. God's name is holy and must not be dishonored.


Q5. True or false: God said that a person who keeps His statutes and rules shall live by them. (18:5) Easy
Answer: True. God's law was given to lead to life, not burden.


Q6. True or false: The land was defiled by the nations, and God said it would spew out its inhabitants for their sins. (18:24-25) Medium
Answer: True. The land's judgment reflected the nations' sins.


Q7. Fill in the blank: The LORD said, "You shall not do as they do in the land of Egypt, nor as they do in the land of ______." (18:3) Easy
Answer: Canaan. Holiness meant living differently from the surrounding nations.


Q8. Multiple choice: Which practice did God forbid in Leviticus 18:21? (18:21) Medium
A) Keeping the sabbath   B) Wearing linen   C) Giving children to the false god Molech   D) Planting fields
Answer: C. Giving children to the false god Molech. God's people must not give their children to idols.


Leviticus 19
Q1. Multiple choice: What did God require in trade? (19:35-36) Hard
A) Double weights   B) Whatever price the seller wants   C) No measures at all   D) Honest weights, measures and scales
Answer: D. Honest weights, measures and scales. Justice in business reflects God's character.


Q2. Multiple choice: What did God command about the edges of the field at harvest? (19:9-10) Easy
A) Sell them   B) Burn them   C) Leave them for the poor and the sojourner   D) Plant them with gold
Answer: C. Leave them for the poor and the sojourner. God provides for the poor through the generosity of others.


Q3. True or false: God told Israel not to hate their brother in their heart, but to rebuke him honestly. (19:17) Hard
Answer: True. Honest confrontation is better than hidden hatred.


Q4. True or false: The sojourner living among you was to be treated as a native among you. (19:34) Medium
Answer: True. God's love extended to the foreigner.


Q5. True or false: God said, "You shall not steal, nor deal falsely, nor lie to one another." (19:11) Easy
Answer: True. Honesty is part of holiness in daily life.


Q6. Multiple choice: What is the right way to treat the deaf and the blind? (19:14) Medium
A) Ignore them   B) Mock them   C) Do not curse the deaf or put a stumbling block before the blind   D) Make them work first
Answer: C. Do not curse the deaf or put a stumbling block before the blind. God's people protect the vulnerable.


Q7. Fill in the blank: You shall stand up before the gray head and honor the ______. (19:32) Medium
Answer: elderly. Respect for elders honors God.


Q8. Fill in the blank: "You shall love your ______ as yourself: I am the LORD." (19:18) Easy
Answer: neighbor. God's love for His people shapes how they love others.


Leviticus 20
Q1. Multiple choice: What did God say about separating Israel from the peoples? (20:24-26) Easy
A) He chose them for their wealth   B) He had separated them so they would be His own   C) He was ashamed of them   D) He forgot them
Answer: B. He had separated them so they would be His own. Holiness marks Israel's identity as God's own people.


Q2. True or false: God forbade His people to turn to mediums and spiritists for guidance. (20:6) Medium
Answer: True. God calls His people to seek Him alone.


Q3. Fill in the blank: "You shall consecrate yourselves and be ______, for I am the LORD your God." (20:7) Easy
Answer: holy. Holiness is both God's gift and Israel's calling.


Q4. True or false: God said, "You shall be holy to me, for I the LORD am holy." (20:26) Easy
Answer: True. God's holiness sets the pattern for His people.


Q5. Multiple choice: What reason did God give for removing the nations before Israel? (20:23) Hard
A) The land was poor   B) Egypt demanded it   C) They had done these abominations   D) Israel asked for it
Answer: C. They had done these abominations. God's judgment on sin is just.


Q6. Fill in the blank: If a man curses his father or his mother, he shall surely be put to ______. (20:9) Medium
Answer: death. Dishonor to parents was taken seriously.


Q7. Multiple choice: What penalty does this ancient Israelite law prescribe for cursing one's father or mother? (20:9) Medium
A) A tax exemption   B) A promotion   C) No penalty   D) Death
Answer: D. Death. This describes a judicial penalty in Israel's covenant law. It gives no private person permission to use violence against a family member.


Q8. True or false: The LORD told Israel to keep His statutes and rules so that the land would not spew them out. (20:22) Hard
Answer: True. Obedience secured Israel's place in the land.


Leviticus 21
Q1. True or false: A priest with a bodily blemish could still present the offerings to the LORD at the altar. (21:21) Hard
Answer: False. A blemish barred him from presenting offerings at the altar.
